, instead of testing
contexts. It is easy to check that the two definitions are
equivalent in
dCBN
. The benefit of our definition is that the
same testing contexts are also used to define

dCBV
-meaningfulness (
Section

2
).

As for the
dCBV
-case,
dCBN
-meaningfulness can be characterized
operationally
,
through the notion of surface-normalization, and
logically
,
through typability in type system
\mathcal{N}
.

Lemma 5.5 (
dCBN
Meaningful Characterizations
(
Bucciarelli et al. 2021
;
Bucciarelli et al. 2020
)
).

Let
t\in\Lambda
. Then, the following characterizations hold:

(Operational)

t
is
dCBN
-meaningful iff
t
is

dCBN
surface-normalizing.

(Logical)

t
is
dCBN
-meaningful iff
t
is

\mathcal{N}
-typable.

Surface Genericity.

As in
{\tt dCBV}
, since
the typing system characterizes meaningfulness, we can
prove a typed genericity result.

Lemma 5.6 (
dCBN
Typed Genericity).

Let
t\in\Lambda
be
dCBN
-meaningless. If
\Pi\triangleright_{\mathcal{N}}\Gamma\vdash\mathtt{C}\!\left<t\right>:\sigma
, then there is

\Pi^{\prime}\triangleright_{\mathcal{N}}\Gamma\vdash\mathtt{C}\!\left<u\right>:\sigma
for all
u\in\Lambda
.

Likewise, it can be transformed into a qualitative surface genericity
using the logical characterization
(
Lemma

5.5
).

Theorem 5.7 (
dCBN
Qualitative Surface Genericity).

Let
t\in\Lambda
be
dCBN
-meaningless. If

\mathtt{C}\!\left<t\right>
is
dCBN
-meaningful then
\mathtt{C}\!\left<u\right>
is also

dCBN
-meaningful for all
u\in\Lambda
.

5.2.
Call-by-Name Genericity

Partial
dCBN
-Calculus

The set
\mathbf{\Lambda}
of
partial terms
of the partial
dCBN
-calculus, denoted by the same symbols used for terms but in a
bold font, is given by the following inductive definition:

\begin{array}[]{r rcl}\textbf{(Partial terms)}&\mathbf{t},\mathbf{u}&\Coloneqq&x\in\mathcal{X}\;|\;\lambda x.\mathbf{t}\;|\;\mathbf{t}\mathbf{u}\;|\;\mathbf{t}[x\backslash\mathbf{u}]\;|\;\bot\end{array}

As in the
dCBN
-calculus, there is a stratified operational, but
now defined by means of partial contexts. The set of

partial list contexts

(\mathbf{L})
and
partial stratified contexts

(\mathbf{N}_{k})
for some
k\in\mathbb{N}^{\omega}
are
inductively defined as follows (where
i\in\mathbb{N}
):

\begin{array}[]{rcl}\mathbf{L}&\;\Coloneqq&\diamond\;|\;\mathbf{L}[x\backslash t]\\[5.69046pt]
\mathbf{N}_{0}&\;\Coloneqq&\diamond\;|\;\lambda x.\mathbf{N}_{0}\;|\;\mathbf{N}_{0}\,t\;|\;\mathbf{N}_{0}[x\backslash t]\\
\mathbf{N}_{i+1}&\;\Coloneqq&\diamond\;|\;\lambda x.\mathbf{N}_{i+1}\;|\;\mathbf{N}_{i+1}\,t\;|\;t\,\mathbf{N}_{i}\;|\;\mathbf{N}_{i+1}[x\backslash t]\;|\;t[x\backslash\mathbf{N}_{i}]\\
\mathbf{N}_{\omega}&\;\Coloneqq&\diamond\;|\;\lambda x.\mathbf{N}_{\omega}\;|\;\mathbf{N}_{\omega}\,t\;|\;t\,\mathbf{N}_{\omega}\;|\;\mathbf{N}_{\omega}[x\backslash t]\;|\;t[x\backslash\mathbf{N}_{\omega}]\end{array}

The following rewriting rules are the base components of our reduction
relations. Any partial term having the shape of the left-hand side of
one of these three rules is called a
redex
.

\begin{array}[]{c c c}\mathbf{L}\!\left<\lambda x.\mathbf{t}\right>\mathbf{u}\;\mapsto_{{\tt\textbf{dB}}}\;\mathbf{L}\!\left<\mathbf{t}[x\backslash\mathbf{u}]\right>&&\mathbf{t}[x\backslash\mathbf{u}]\;\mapsto_{{\tt\textbf{s}}}\;\mathbf{t}\{x\backslash\mathbf{u}\}\end{array}

Building upon the established pattern used for terms, the

stratified partial reduction
(or
\mathrm{n}_{k}
-reduction)

\rightarrow_{\mathbf{n}_{k}}
is defined as the partial
k
-stratified closure (
\mathbf{N}_{k}
-closure) of the
two rewriting rules
\mapsto_{{\tt\textbf{dB}}}
and

\mapsto_{{\tt\textbf{s}}}
. Its reflexive-transitive closure is
denoted by
\rightarrow^{*}_{\mathbf{n}_{k}}
.

Example 5.8.

\mathbf{t}_{0}=\big(\lambda y.\bot w\big)[w\backslash\lambda z.\bot]\rightarrow_{\mathbf{n}_{k}}\lambda y.\big(\bot\,\lambda z.\bot\big)

=\mathbf{t}_{1}
for any
k\in\mathbb{N}^{\omega}
. In particular,

\mathbf{t}_{1}
is
\mathbf{n}_{0}
-normal form.

Meaningful Approximation.

The
definition is the same as in
dCBV
, just adapting the notion of meaningfulness
to
dCBN
. Indeed, the
dCBN
-meaningful
approximant

\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(t\right)
of
t\in\Lambda
is

\bot
if
t
is
dCBN
-meaningless, and is defined
inductively on
t

otherwise
:

\displaystyle\hskip-8.5359pt\hskip-7.11317pt\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(x\right)

\displaystyle\coloneqq x

\displaystyle\hskip-7.11317pt\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(tu\right)

\displaystyle\coloneqq\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(t\right)\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(u\right)

\displaystyle\hskip-7.11317pt\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(\lambda x.t\right)

\displaystyle\coloneqq\lambda x.\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(t\right)

\displaystyle\hskip-7.11317pt\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(t[x\backslash u]\right)

\displaystyle\coloneqq\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(t\right)[x\backslash\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(u\right)]

The meaningful approximation of a term
t
approximates
t
:

Lemma 5.9.

Let
t\in\Lambda
, then
\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(t\right)\leq t
.

Lemma 5.10 (
dCBN
Approximant Genericity).

Let
\mathtt{C}
be a full context. If
t\in\Lambda

is
dCBN
-meaningless, then
\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(\mathtt{C}\!\left<t\right>\right)\leq\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(\mathtt{C}\!\left<u\right>\right)
for all
u\in\Lambda
.

Dynamic Approximation and Lifting.

To replicate
the proof of quantitative stratified genericity on the

dCBN
-calculus, we now need to be able to approximate term
reductions and lift partial reductions. Fortunately, both instances
of

B

and

A

for
dCBN
hold. Consequently, the partial
dCBN
-calculus can
effectively be used to approximate reductions of the

dCBN
-calculus.

Observability.

We now check that the
partial
dCBN
-calculus correctly captures the meaningful and
observable outcomes.

For
k\in\mathbb{N}^{\omega}
, the set
{\tt bno}_{\mathbf{n}_{k}}
of partial terms introduced below aims to provide
perfect approximation of them, being

\mathbf{n}_{k}
-normal forms without any occurrence of

\bot
at depth
k
.

\begin{array}[]{rcl}{\tt bne}_{\mathbf{n}_{0}}&\coloneqq&x\;|\;{\tt bne}_{\mathbf{n}_{0}}\mathbf{t}\\
{\tt bne}_{\mathbf{n}_{i+1}}&\coloneqq&x\;|\;{\tt bne}_{\mathbf{n}_{i+1}}{\tt bno}_{\mathbf{n}_{i}}\qquad(i\in\mathbb{N})\\
{\tt bne}_{\mathbf{n}_{\omega}}&\coloneqq&x\;|\;{\tt bne}_{\mathbf{n}_{\omega}}{\tt bno}_{\mathbf{n}_{\omega}}\\[5.69046pt]
{\tt bno}_{\mathbf{n}_{k}}&\coloneqq&\lambda x.{\tt bno}_{\mathbf{n}_{k}}\;|\;{\tt bne}_{\mathbf{n}_{k}}\qquad(k\in\mathbb{N}^{\omega})\end{array}

Remarkably, the partial terms in

{\tt bno}_{\mathbf{n}_{k}}
are precise enough to perfectly observe the

\mathrm{n}_{k}
-normal forms and entirely determine them up
to depth
k
, so that the
dCBN
instances of

C

and

D

hold.

Quantitative Stratified Genericity.

We now have
all the material to enhance our
dCBN
qualitative surface
genericity (
Theorem

5.7
) into a
stronger quantitative stratified genericity result.

Theorem 5.11 (
dCBN
Quantitative Stratified Genericity).

Let
k\in\mathbb{N}^{\omega}
. If
t\in\Lambda
is

dCBN
-meaningless and
\mathtt{C}\!\left<t\right>
is

\mathrm{n}_{k}
-normalizing, then there is
i\in\mathbb{N}
such that, for every
u\in\Lambda
, there are

t^{\prime},u^{\prime}\in{\tt no}_{\mathrm{n}_{k}}
such that
\mathtt{C}\!\left<t\right>\rightarrow^{i}_{\mathrm{n}_{k}}t^{\prime}\;\overset{\scriptscriptstyle k}{\approx}\;u^{\prime}\;\sideset{{}^{i}_{\mathrm{n}_{k}\!\!\!\!}}{}{\revArr}\;\mathtt{C}\!\left<u\right>
.

The proof of this theorem follows the exact same schematic as

Theorem

4.11
. A particular case
is given by the following statement since
k
-equality for

k=\omega
is just the equality between terms.

Corollary 5.12 (
dCBN
Quantitative Full Genericity).

If
t\in\Lambda
is
dCBN
-meaningless and
\mathtt{C}\!\left<t\right>\rightarrow^{*}_{\mathrm{n}_{\omega}}s
where
s
is

\mathrm{n}_{\omega}
-normal, then there is
i\in\mathbb{N}
such that, for every
u\in\Lambda
,

\mathtt{C}\!\left<t\right>\rightarrow^{i}_{\mathrm{n}_{\omega}}s\;\sideset{{}^{i}_{\mathrm{n}_{\omega}\!\!\!\!}}{}{\revArr}\;\mathtt{C}\!\left<u\right>
.

6.
Theories

In this section, we prove that
the meaningful (resp. observational)
theory
\bm{\mathcal{H}}
(resp.
\bm{\mathcal{H}}^{*}
) generated by
dCBV
and
dCBN
is
consistent
(resp.
consistent

and
sensible
). This is when genericity comes into play. To streamline
the presentation of our approach and avoid unnecessary repetitions, we
employ a
unified reasoning
methodology for both frameworks. To achieve
this, we begin by presenting the proofs of consistency and sensibility
for a
generic
calculus (
Section

6.1
), for which
we assume a set of axioms, mirroring our approach when describing the
methodology used to obtain genericity (
Section

3
).
Then, we instantiate the generic calculus with
dCBV
(
Section

6.2
)
and
dCBN
(
Section

6.3
),
showing that each of these instantiations enjoys the axioms stated
before for the generic calculus.

6.1.
Generic Theories

We introduce here a generic calculus and three associated
theories: the computational theory
\bm{\mathcal{R}}
, the meaningful
theory
\bm{\mathcal{H}}
and the observational theory
\bm{\mathcal{H}}^{*}
. We provide an
axiomatized proof of the consistency of
\bm{\mathcal{H}}
, and of the
consistency and sensibility of
\bm{\mathcal{H}}^{*}
.

Let us consider a
generic calculus
, based on the set of terms

\Lambda
equipped with a notion of stratified contexts for all

k\in\mathbb{N}^{\omega}
. In particular,
full contexts
(
k=\omega
) are denoted by
\mathtt{C}
. We also assume the
generic calculus to be equipped with a set
\mathsf{R}
of rewrite
rules. The following definitions should
be subscripted by
\mathsf{R}
but we omit it for better readability.
As in
Section

3
, we also assume the generic calculus to
be equipped with a notion of
stratified reduction

\rightarrow_{k}

generated by the closure of the rules in
\mathsf{R}
by stratified
contexts of level
k\in\mathbb{N}^{\omega}
. This gives rise to
corresponding notions of
k
-normal form and

k
-normalization. In particular the generic calculus is
equipped with a notion of surface reduction (level
k=0
) as
well as a notion of full reduction (level
k=\omega
). We
also assume that the generic calculus is equipped with an appropriate
notion of
meaningfulness
.

A
theory
is a set of equations of the form
t\doteq u
with

t,u\in\Lambda
. A
congruence

\mathcal{C}_{\mathsf{B}}
is the
smallest theory containing a set
\mathsf{B}
of
base
equations
, and closed by reflexivity, symmetry, transitivity and full
contexts. Alternatively,
\mathcal{C}_{\mathsf{B}}
can be specified by the rules
below:

\begin{array}[]{c}\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\vbox{\hbox{\hskip 5.97215pt\hbox{}}\vbox{\hbox{\hbox{\lower 3.21385pt\vbox{\vskip 3.01385pt\hrule height=0.4pt\vskip 3.01385pt\par}}\enskip\hbox{\lower 2.15277pt\hbox{\!\!\scriptsize$({\tt ref})$}}}\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\hbox{$\hfil\lx@intercol$}}\hbox{$t\doteq t$}}\par}\par}}}$}\hskip 14.22636pt\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\vbox{\hbox{}\vbox{\hbox{\hbox{\lower 3.21385pt\vbox{\vskip 3.01385pt\hrule height=0.4pt\vskip 3.01385pt\par}}\enskip\hbox{\lower 2.15277pt\hbox{\!\!\scriptsize$({\tt sym})$}}}\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\hbox{$t\doteq u$}}\hbox{$u\doteq t$}}\par}\par}}}$}\hskip 14.22636pt\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\vbox{\hbox{}\vbox{\hbox{\hbox{\lower 3.21385pt\vbox{\vskip 3.01385pt\hrule height=0.4pt\vskip 3.01385pt\par}}\enskip\hbox{\lower 2.15277pt\hbox{\!\!\scriptsize$({\tt tra})$}}}\hbox{\hskip 5.53821pt\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\hbox{\hbox{}\quad\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\hbox{$t\doteq s$}}\hbox{$s\doteq u$}}}}\hbox{$t\doteq u$}}}\par}\par}}}$}\\[8.5359pt]
\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\vbox{\hbox{\hskip 12.19455pt\hbox{}}\vbox{\hbox{\hbox{\lower 3.21385pt\vbox{\vskip 3.01385pt\hrule height=0.4pt\vskip 3.01385pt\par}}\enskip\hbox{\lower 2.15277pt\hbox{\!\!\scriptsize$({\tt ctx})$}}}\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\hbox{$t\doteq u\hfil\lx@intercol$}}\hbox{$\mathtt{C}\!\left<t\right>\doteq\mathtt{C}\!\left<u\right>$}}\par}\par}}}$}\hskip 14.22636pt\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\vbox{\hbox{}\vbox{\hbox{\hbox{\lower 3.21385pt\vbox{\vskip 3.01385pt\hrule height=0.4pt\vskip 3.01385pt\par}}\enskip\hbox{\lower 2.15277pt\hbox{\!\!\scriptsize$({\tt base})$}}}\hbox{\hskip 5.97232pt\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\hbox{$(t,u)\!\in\!\mathsf{B}$}}\hbox{$t\doteq u$}}}\par}\par}}}$}\end{array}

Given a theory
\mathsf{B}
, we write

\mathsf{B}\vdash t\doteq u
if
t\doteq u\in\mathcal{C}_{\mathsf{B}}
, and

\mathsf{B}\not\vdash t\doteq u
if
t\doteq u\notin\mathcal{C}_{\mathsf{B}}
. A congruence
\mathcal{C}_{\mathsf{B}}
is

consistent
, if it does not equate all terms (
i.e.
there are
t,u\in\Lambda
such that
\mathsf{B}\not\vdash t\doteq u
).

The
computational theory

\bm{\mathcal{R}}
is the congruence

\mathcal{C}_{\mathsf{R}}
(recall that

\mathsf{R}
is the set of rewrite rules of the generic calculus).
We write
t\mathbin{={{}_{\!\!\mathsf{R}}}}u
for the reflexive,
symmetric, transitive closure of the reduction
\rightarrow_{\omega}
.
As expected, the congruence
\bm{\mathcal{R}}
axiomatizes the equivalence

\mathbin{={{}_{\!\!\mathsf{R}}}}
:

Lemma 6.1.

Let
t,u\in\Lambda
, then
\mathsf{R}\vdash t\doteq u
if and
only if
t\mathbin{={{}_{\!\!\mathsf{R}}}}u
.

A
\lambda
-theory

\bm{\mathcal{E}}
is any congruence
containing the computational theory
\bm{\mathcal{R}}
, that is,
\bm{\mathcal{E}}=\mathcal{C}_{\mathsf{B}}
for some
\mathsf{B}\supseteq\mathsf{R}
.
A
\lambda
-theory
\mathcal{C}_{\mathsf{B}}
is

sensible
if it equates all meaningless terms, that is,
\mathsf{B}\vdash t\doteq u
if
t
and
u
are meaningless. The

meaningful theory
\bm{\mathcal{H}}
is the smallest sensible

\lambda
-theory (
i.e.

\bm{\mathcal{H}}\coloneqq\mathcal{C}_{\mathsf{H}}

where
\mathsf{H}\coloneqq\mathsf{R}\cup\{(t,u)\;|\;t,u\text{ meaningless}\}
).

Working directly on
\bm{\mathcal{H}}
is not easy, but it is possible to
refine its specification following
(
Barendregt 1984
)
(see

Appendix

D
for details).

In order to abstractly prove consistency of the
\lambda
-theory
\bm{\mathcal{H}}
, we first assume consistency of the
\lambda
-theory
\bm{\mathcal{R}}
as an
axiom
.

Axiom

E
(
Consistency
)
.

The
\lambda
-theory
\bm{\mathcal{R}}
is consistent.

Why this assumption is reasonable? The
\lambda
-theory
\bm{\mathcal{H}}

is built as an extension of
\bm{\mathcal{R}}
to ensure its invariance
under reduction. If
\bm{\mathcal{R}}
is inconsistent then all its
extensions (including
\bm{\mathcal{H}}
) are inconsistent as well, regardless
of the definition of meaningfulness. Taking
\bm{\mathcal{R}}

to be consistent as an axiom
is therefore reasonable in our framework.

We abstractly prove that consistency of
\bm{\mathcal{H}}
can be reduced to
that of
\bm{\mathcal{R}}
, following the same ideas
in
(
Barendregt 1984
)
. For that, we start by showing in particular
that
\bm{\mathcal{H}}
and
\bm{\mathcal{R}}
both agree on
\omega
-normal
forms, a forthcoming property
(
Lemma

6.2
) which
uses the fact that the generic calculus satisfies the following

F

and

G
.

Axiom

F
(
Confluence and Normal Form
)
.

Reduction
\rightarrow_{\omega}
is confluent and
there exists an
\omega
-normal form.

Indeed, normal forms represent the results of computations, thus being
without
\omega
-normal forms would mean that the calculus never
yields a result (also, variables are
\omega
-normal in any
reasonable rewriting system). Moreover, confluence of

\rightarrow_{\omega}
implies that two evaluations from the same term
cannot yield different outcomes.
Hence,
F
is a perfectly reasonable assumption.

Axiom

G
(
Full Genericity
)
.

If
t\in\Lambda
is meaningless and
\mathtt{C}\!\left<t\right>\rightarrow^{*}_{\omega}s
where
s
is
\omega
-normal, then
for every
u\in\Lambda
,

\mathtt{C}\!\left<t\right>\rightarrow^{*}_{\omega}s\;\sideset{{}^{*}_{\omega}}{}{\revArr}\;\mathtt{C}\!\left<u\right>
.

Since
\bm{\mathcal{H}}
is the smallest sensible
\lambda
-theory, proving

\mathtt{C}\!\left<t\right>\doteq\mathtt{C}\!\left<u\right>
in
\bm{\mathcal{H}}
can be done either by using the rules for

\bm{\mathcal{R}}
or –thanks to sensibility– by replacing
t
with
u
when they are both meaningless.
Full genericity (
G
) allows one to do that directly in

\bm{\mathcal{R}}
, in the special case where
\mathtt{C}\!\left<t\right>
or
\mathtt{C}\!\left<u\right>
is
\omega
-normalizing.
Therefore,

Lemma 6.2.

If
u\in\Lambda
has a
\omega
-normal form, then for
any
t\in\Lambda
, if
\mathsf{H}\vdash t\doteq u
then

\mathsf{R}\vdash t\doteq u
.

Theorem 6.3.

The
\lambda
-theory
\bm{\mathcal{H}}
is consistent.

Proof.

By
E
, there exist two terms
t_{1},t_{2}\in\Lambda
such that
t_{1}\doteq t_{2}
is not provable in

\bm{\mathcal{R}}
. Moreover, using
F
, there exists an

\omega
-normal form
u\in\Lambda
. Suppose by absurd that

\bm{\mathcal{H}}
is inconsistent, then
\mathsf{H}\vdash t_{1}\doteq u
and

\mathsf{H}\vdash u\doteq t_{2}
. Thus
\mathsf{R}\vdash t_{1}\doteq u
and

\mathsf{R}\vdash u\doteq t_{2}
using

Lemma

6.2
, hence

\mathsf{R}\vdash t_{1}\doteq t_{2}
by transitivity which
contradicts
E
. One then concludes that
\bm{\mathcal{H}}
is consistent.
∎

Now we study the
observational theory
\bm{\mathcal{H}}^{*}
associated
with a generic calculus, which is defined by the following set:

\bm{\mathcal{H}}^{*}\coloneqq\{t\doteq u\;|\;\forall\;\mathtt{C},\;\mathtt{C}\!\left<t\right>\text{ meaningful }\Leftrightarrow\mathtt{C}\!\left<u\right>\text{ meaningful}\}

To prove that the theory
\bm{\mathcal{H}}^{*}
is a consistent and
sensible
\lambda
-theory, we use the following complementary
axioms, which are natural and self-explained, as well as consistency of
\bm{\mathcal{H}}
(
Theorem

6.3
).

Axiom

H
(
Meaningfulness Stability
)
.

Let
t,u\in\Lambda
such that
t\rightarrow_{\omega}u
,
then
t
is meaningful if and only if
u
is meaningful.

Axiom

I
(
Surface Genericity
)
.

If
t\in\Lambda
is meaningless and
\mathtt{C}\!\left<t\right>

is meaningful, then for any
u\in\Lambda
,
\mathtt{C}\!\left<u\right>

is meaningful.

Axiom

J
(
Meaningful Existence
)
.

There is a meaningless term in the generic calculus.

Theorem 6.4.

\bm{\mathcal{H}}^{*}
is a sensible and consistent
\lambda
-theory.

Proof.

(
\lambda
-theory) It suffices to show
\bm{\mathcal{H}}^{*}=\mathcal{C}_{\bm{\mathcal{H}}^{*}}
and
\mathsf{R}\subseteq\bm{\mathcal{H}}^{*}
.
If
(t,u)\in\mathsf{R}
then, for every full context
\mathtt{C}
, we have
\mathtt{C}\!\left<t\right>\rightarrow_{\omega}\mathtt{C}\!\left<u\right>
and so
t\doteq u\in\bm{\mathcal{H}}^{*}
by
H
.
By definition of
\bm{\mathcal{H}}^{*}
​, it is easy to
check that
t\!\doteq\!u\in\bm{\mathcal{H}}^{*}
iff
t\!\doteq\!u\in\mathcal{C}_{\bm{\mathcal{H}}^{*}}
.

(Sensibility) As
\bm{\mathcal{H}}^{*}
is a
\lambda
-theory, it suffices to show that
\bm{\mathcal{H}}^{*}
equates all meaningless terms.
Let
t,u\in\Lambda
be meaningless. By
surface genericity (
I
),
\mathtt{C}\!\left<t\right>

is meaningful iff so is
\mathtt{C}\!\left<u\right>
, thus
t\doteq u\in\bm{\mathcal{H}}^{*}
.

(Consistency) As
\bm{\mathcal{H}}
is consistent
(
Theorem

6.3
),
\mathsf{H}\not\vdash t\doteq u
for some
t,u\in\Lambda
. This means that
t
or
u
–say
t
– is meaningful
(otherwise
\mathsf{H}\vdash t\doteq u
). By
J
,
there is a meaningless
s\in\Lambda
. For
\mathtt{C}=\diamond
,
\mathtt{C}\!\left<t\right>=t
is meaningful and
\mathtt{C}\!\left<s\right>=s
is
meaningless, then
t\doteq s\notin\bm{\mathcal{H}}^{*}=\mathcal{C}_{\bm{\mathcal{H}}^{*}}
thus
\bm{\mathcal{H}}^{*}\not\vdash t\doteq s
, that is,
\bm{\mathcal{H}}^{*}
is consistent.
∎

6.2.
dCBV
Theories

We now instantiate the generic theories
above to the
dCBV
-calculus: let
\bm{\mathcal{H}}_{{\tt dCBV}}
(resp.
\bm{\mathcal{H}}^{*}_{{\tt dCBV}}
) be the instantiation of
\bm{\mathcal{H}}
(resp.
\bm{\mathcal{H}}^{*}
) obtained by replacing “meaningful” with “
dCBV
-meaningful” (see
Definition

2.4
) in its definiton.
Thus, we prove consistency (resp. and
sensibility) of
\bm{\mathcal{H}}_{{\tt dCBV}}
(resp.
\bm{\mathcal{H}}^{*}_{{\tt dCBV}}
). We also prove
that
\bm{\mathcal{H}}^{*}_{{\tt dCBV}}
is the
unique

maximal
consistent

\lambda_{{\tt dCBV}}
-theory extending
\bm{\mathcal{H}}_{{\tt dCBV}}
.

Theorem 6.5.

The
dCBV
-calculus verifies

F
,
G
,
E
,
H
,
I
and
J
.

Proof.

For
F
see
(
Accattoli and Paolini 2012
)
.

G

is a special case of
Corollary

4.12
.

H
is proved using Full
Subject Reduction and Expansion in system
\mathcal{V}

(
Bucciarelli et al. 2020
)
and

Lemma

2.5
. For

I
we use

Theorem

2.8
. For

J
: the term
\Omega
is
\rightarrow_{\mathrm{v}_{0}}

divergent thus by
Lemma

2.5
,
one concludes that it is
dCBV
-meaningless.
∎

Corollary 6.6.

(1)

The
\lambda_{{\tt dCBV}}
-theory
\bm{\mathcal{H}}_{{\tt dCBV}}
is consistent.

(2)

The theory
\bm{\mathcal{H}}^{*}_{{\tt dCBV}}
is a sensible and
consistent
\lambda_{{\tt dCBV}}
-theory.

Sensibility means that
\bm{\mathcal{H}}^{*}_{{\tt dCBV}}
is an extension of
\bm{\mathcal{H}}_{{\tt dCBV}}
.
We prove that
\bm{\mathcal{H}}^{*}_{{\tt dCBV}}
is actually the unique consistent
maximal
\lambda_{{\tt dCBV}}
-theory extending
\bm{\mathcal{H}}_{{\tt dCBV}}
,
where
maximal
means that, for every
t,u\in\Lambda
, either
\bm{\mathcal{H}}^{*}_{{\tt dCBV}}\vdash t\doteq u
or
\bm{\mathcal{H}}^{*}_{{\tt dCBV}}
extended with the base equation
t\doteq u
is inconsistent.
For that we need the following property.

Lemma 6.7.

Let
t,u\in\Lambda
. If
\bm{\mathcal{H}}_{{\tt dCBV}}
is extended with the
base equation
t\doteq u
is consistent, then for all full
context
\mathtt{C}
, one has that
\mathtt{C}\!\left<t\right>
is

dCBV
-meaningful iff
\mathtt{C}\!\left<u\right>
is
dCBV
-meaningful.

By
Lemma

6.7
and sensibility of

\bm{\mathcal{H}}^{*}_{{\tt dCBV}}
(
Theorem

6.4
), we have:

Corollary 6.8.

The theory
\bm{\mathcal{H}}^{*}_{{\tt dCBV}}
is the unique maximal consistent and sensible

\lambda_{{\tt dCBV}}
-theory.

The
\lambda_{{\tt dCBV}}
-theory
\bm{\mathcal{H}}^{*}_{{\tt dCBV}}
equates
more
then

\bm{\mathcal{H}}_{{\tt dCBV}}
. For example,
\mathtt{I}\doteq\lambda x.\lambda y.xy

is provable in
\bm{\mathcal{H}}^{*}_{{\tt dCBV}}
but not in
\bm{\mathcal{H}}_{{\tt dCBV}}
.

Finally, we can now prove our last contribution: the

\lambda_{{\tt dCBV}}
-theory
\bm{\mathcal{H}}^{*}_{{\tt dCBV}}
coincides with the observational
equivalence
\cong
defined in
Section

2
. To prove this, we
define the auxiliary notion of
open-observational equivalence

\cong^{o}
in
dCBV
: given
t,u\in\Lambda
,
t\cong^{o}u
if
for
every
full context
\mathtt{C}
,
\mathtt{C}\!\left<t\right>\rightarrow^{*}_{\mathrm{v}_{\omega}}v_{1}
iff
\mathtt{C}\!\left<u\right>\rightarrow^{*}_{\mathrm{v}_{\omega}}v_{2}
for some values
v_{1},v_{2}\in\Upsilon
. Note that, differently from
\cong
,
\cong^{o}

quantifies over all full contexts and not only on
closing
full
contexts, hence
\cong^{o}\,\subseteq\,\cong
.

Corollary 6.9.

Let
t,u\in\Lambda
, then
t\cong u

iff
\bm{\mathcal{H}}^{*}_{{\tt dCBV}}\vdash t\doteq u
.

Proof.

\bm{\mathcal{H}}^{*}_{{\tt dCBV}}\vdash t\doteq u
means that for every full context
\mathtt{C}
,

\mathtt{C}\!\left<t\right>
is meaningless iff
\mathtt{C}\!\left<u\right>

is. By quantitative stratified genericity with

k=0

(
Theorem

4.11
), this is
equivalent to say that, for every full context
\mathtt{C}
,

\mathtt{C}\!\left<t\right>\rightarrow^{*}_{\mathrm{v}_{0}}v
for some value
v
iff

\mathtt{C}\!\left<u\right>\rightarrow^{*}_{\mathrm{v}_{0}}v^{\prime}
for some value
v^{\prime}
, that is,
t\cong^{o}u
. Thus,
\bm{\mathcal{H}}^{*}_{{\tt dCBV}}\vdash t\doteq u
if and only if

t\cong^{o}u
.

Therefore, as
\cong^{o}\,\subseteq\,\cong
, if
\bm{\mathcal{H}}^{*}_{{\tt dCBV}}\vdash t\doteq u
then
t\cong u
, in particular
\cong
is sensible (since so is
\bm{\mathcal{H}}^{*}_{{\tt dCBV}}
,
Corollary

6.6
).
As
\cong
is a consistent (clearly,

\mathtt{I}\not\cong\Omega
)
\lambda
-theory (trivial), one
has that
\bm{\mathcal{H}}^{*}_{{\tt dCBV}}\vdash t\doteq u
if and only if
t\cong u
, by maximality of
\bm{\mathcal{H}}^{*}_{{\tt dCBV}}

(
Corollary

6.8
).
∎

Corollaries

6.6
,
6.8
and
6.9
are among our
main contributions
.
In particular, the fact that
\bm{\mathcal{H}}^{*}_{{\tt dCBV}}=\ \cong
means that two different approaches to define a semantics in
dCBV
actually coincide.
This further backs up the idea that what we call
dCBV
-meaningfulness approppriately represents meaningfuness in
dCBV
.

6.3.
dCBN
Theories

We now instantiate the generic theories of

Section

6.1
to the

dCBN
-calculus: let
\bm{\mathcal{H}}_{{\tt dCBN}}
(resp.
\bm{\mathcal{H}}^{*}_{{\tt dCBN}}
) be the instantiation of
\bm{\mathcal{H}}
(resp.
\bm{\mathcal{H}}^{*}
) obtained by replacing “meaningful” with
dCBN
-meaningful (see
Definition

5.4
) Thus, we prove that
\bm{\mathcal{H}}_{{\tt dCBN}}
(resp.
\bm{\mathcal{H}}^{*}_{{\tt dCBN}}
) is
consistent (resp. consistent and sensible).

Theorem 6.10.

The
dCBN
-calculus verifies

F
,
G
,
E
,
H
,
I
and
J
.

Proof.

For
F
see
(
Bucciarelli et al. 2023
)
.
For

G
, it is a special case of
Theorem

5.11
.
For
H
is proved using
Full Subject Reduction and Expansion in system
\mathcal{N}

(
Bucciarelli et al. 2020
)
and

Lemma

5.5
. For

I
we use

Theorem

5.7
. For

J
: the term
\Omega
is
\rightarrow_{\mathrm{n}_{0}}

divergent thus by
Lemma

5.5
,
one concludes that it is
dCBN
-meaningless.
∎

Corollary 6.11.

(1)

The
\lambda_{{\tt dCBN}}
-theory
\bm{\mathcal{H}}_{{\tt dCBN}}
is consistent.

(2)

The theory
\bm{\mathcal{H}}^{*}_{{\tt dCBN}}
is a sensible and
consistent
\lambda_{{\tt dCBN}}
-theory.

Using arguments similar to
dCBV
, we can establish that the theory

\bm{\mathcal{H}}^{*}_{{\tt dCBN}}
is the unique maximal consistent
\lambda_{{\tt dCBN}}
-theory extending

\bm{\mathcal{H}}_{{\tt dCBN}}
and coincides with the observational equivalence where the observable is being
\mathrm{n}_{0}
-normalizing, analogously to what is well-known for the pure

\lambda
-calculus
(
Hyland 1975
;
Wadsworth 1976
;
Barendregt 1984
)
.

7.
Related Works and Conclusion

Related Works

There are at least six different methods to prove (full)
genericity
in CbN.

•

Barendregt
(
Barendregt 1984
, Ch. 14.3)
uses a
tree-topology on
terms based on Böhm trees, and continuity of term application with respect to it.

•

Takahashi’s
(
Takahashi 1994
)
operational proof exploits the fact that
the solvable terms are precisely the
ones having a head normal form.

•

Kuper’s
(
Kuper 1995
)
operational proof is based on leftmost reduction and
can also be extended to other calculi (
e.g.
with types, PCF).

•

Kennaway
et al.

(
Kennaway et al. 1999
)
abstractly provide three axioms in order for a set of
\lambda
-terms to be coherently equated and satisfy genericity.

•

Ghilezan
(
Ghilezan01
)
exploits a topology induced by an intersection type system and, as in
(
Barendregt 1984
)
, continuity of application with respect to it.

•

Barbarossa and Manzonetto
(
Barbarossa and Manzonetto 2020
)
prove that genericity follows from the commutation of normalization and Taylor expansion.

Let us see why the existing methods to prove CbN genericity cannot be
easily adapted to CbV. Barendregt’s method does not easily lift to CbV
because CbV
Böhm trees
and their properties are still to be
explored; a first attempt is in
(
Kerinec et al. 2020
;
Kerinec et al. 2021
)
. Takahashi’s method is
based on
lifting
genericity with respect to substitutions to
genericity with respect to
full contexts
: such a method breaks
in CbV, because it relies on adding abstractions, which in CbV (and
not in CbN) can turn a meaningless term into a meaningful one.
Kuper’s approach relies on
leftmost
reduction, which is a
normalizing strategy for CbN; the equivalent normalizing strategy for
CbV
(
Accattoli et al. 2021
;
Accattoli et al. 2023
)
is
involuted and not really leftmost in a syntactic sense, so this method
would not be handy. Kennaway et al.’s
axiomatics
targets
first-order term rewriting systems and CbN
\lambda
-calculus, but it
is unclear how to adapt the technique to
higher-order

reduction, in particular to reduction at a distance.
Ghilezan’s approach can only work with
idempotent
intersection types, and this makes impossible to extract any quantitative information as we do.
Finally, it is
tedious to adapt Barbarossa and Manzonetto’s method to
CbV
Taylor expansion
, whose underlying properties are still not well
explored: some preliminary attempts appear in
(
Ehrhard 2012
;
Carraro and Guerrieri 2014
;
Kerinec et al. 2020
;
Kerinec et al. 2021
)
.
Moreover the Taylor expansion exploits some kind of
non-determinism that in fact is not needed to prove genericity.
Indeed, our approach still uses some kind of
approximations of
\lambda
-terms, but contrary to the Taylor
expansion, its reduction rules
are not non-deterministic
.

Genericity and
\lambda
-theories of CbV was scarcely approached in
the literature on CbV. The non-adequacy problem mentioned
in
Section

2
for Plotkin’s CbV was already noticed by Paolini,
Ronchi Della Rocca
et
al.

(
Paolini and Ronchi Della Rocca 1999
;
Rocca and Paolini 2004
;
Accattoli and Paolini 2012
;
Carraro and Guerrieri 2014
;
Guerrieri et al. 2017
)
,
who studied different properties of CbV solvability.
As mentioned before, this notion does not yield a consistent
associated theory.

Garcia-Perez and Nogueira
(
García-Pérez and Nogueira 2016
)
proposed a
different

notion of solvability for Plotkin’s CbV that takes into account
the
order
of a term (number of arguments it can take), for which they prove
genericity. The
\lambda
-theory generated by their notion of
solvability is different from
\bm{\mathcal{H}}_{{\tt dCBV}}
and it can also be proved to be
consistent. However, the original calculus still suffers from

non-adequacy
.

Independently, Accattoli and Lancelot
(
Accattoli and Lancelot 2024
)
have recently
developed an alternative study of what we call
surface

genericity in this paper, for both CbV and CbN, via different tools
(
e.g.
bisimulations). As far as we know, the challenging proof of

full
genericity for
dCBV
-meaningless terms has never been
proved before.

The consistency of the theory
\bm{\mathcal{H}}_{{\tt dCBV}}
is already proved in the
literature —or can be easily derived— from the consistency of some
related

\lambda_{v}
-theories
(
Egidi et al. 1992
;
Paolini and Ronchi Della Rocca 1999
;
Carraro and Guerrieri 2014
;
Accattoli and Guerrieri 2022
;
Accattoli and Lancelot 2024
)
.
However, this paper provides the first proof of consistency of the
theory
\bm{\mathcal{H}}_{{\tt dCBV}}
that is
direct
—not based on consistency of some
related theories— and
operational
—not based on semantical
methods.

It would also be reasonable to study
\lambda
-theories that are consistent but non sensible, as it is done for CbN, see
e.g.

(
Barendregt92a
)
.

Conclusion

We introduce a novel technique to prove
stratified
genericity,
yielding both
surface
and
full
genericity as special
cases. Remarkably, this technique is specified by an
axiomatic

approach which applies to both
call-by-value
and

call-by-name
. Moreover, our genericity theorems are not only

qualitative
(as usual in the literature) but also

quantitative
, capturing the fact that replacing a meaningless
subterm in a normalizing term
t
does not affect the number of steps
to reach the normal form of
t
.

Furthermore, we establish the
first direct
and

operational
proof that the smallest congruence relation
resulting from equating all meaningless
\lambda
-terms is

consistent
. We substantiate this proof by introducing an

axiomatic
approach, which applies to both
call-by-value

and
call-by-name
paradigms. Moreover, we show that these two
theories admit a unique
maximal
consistent extension which
coincides with well-known
observational equivalences
.

Alternatively, our methodology based on meaningful approximants can be
replaced by a non-idempotent intersection type system characterizing
full normalization, as
(
Bucciarelli et al. 2017
;
Kesner and Ventura 2014
)
for
dCBN
and
(
Accattoli et al. 2023
)
for
dCBV
. The obtained
result would however be weaker, since such typing systems only
characterize normalizability of terms, but do not provide any
information about the form of the resulting normal forms. Some ideas
coming from
(
Bernadet and Lengrand 2011
)
will be studied in this direction.

Through the tools developed in this paper, we aim to extend our
approach to establish genericity within a more encompassing unifying
framework, such as call-by-push-value
(
Levy 1999
)
or the bang
calculus
(
Ehrhard 2016
;
Ehrhard and Guerrieri 2016
;
Guerrieri and Manzonetto 2018
;
Bucciarelli et al. 2020
;
Bucciarelli et al. 2023
;
ArrialGuerrieriKesner23
)
.
These frameworks, by explicitly distinguishing between values and
computations, have the capability to subsume both call-by-value and
call-by-name at the same time.

An equally challenging model of computation, still unexplored in terms
of meaningfulness and genericity, is
call-by-need
(
Ariola and Felleisen 1997
;
Ariola et al. 1995
)
We believe that by
stratifying the notion of call-by-need contexts and reduction, we can
construct a well-defined notion of meaningfulness and then achieve a
corresponding genericity result.

We are also interested in understanding the relation between the notion of CbV meaningless studied here and the one of
unsolvability of order
0
introduced by
(
García-Pérez and Nogueira 2016
)
for Plotkin’s CbV.

The notion of meaningfulness in CbN (solvability) is strictly related to the one of Böhm tree, which represents a term as a possible infinite tree that is the sup of its approximations.
Our notion of meaningful approximation yields a notion of Böhm tree in CbV, which slightly differs from the one introduced in
(
Kerinec et al. 2020
;
Kerinec et al. 2021
)
(because of their different notion of approximation). It would be interesting to compare the two notions of CbV Böhm trees and characterize the
\lambda
-theories they generate in a denotational or operational way.

We believe that our approach to approximation and stratification are powerful tools that can be used not only to prove genericity but also other important results in
\lambda
-calculus such as Scott’s continuity, Berry’s stability or Kahn and Plotkin’s sequentiality theory, simplifing the proofs provided in
(
Barbarossa and Manzonetto 2020
)
via the notion of Taylor expansion.

An open question in CbV was the existence of an appropriate notion of meaningfulness and its associated
\lambda
-theory fulfilling
all
the basic properties and criteria we discussed in
Section

1
, which are well-known for the theory
\bm{\mathcal{H}}
in CbN.
This paper fills the gap by answering positively to this fundamental question, through a new approach that is robust as it works uniformly for CbN and CbV.

,

\begin{array}[]{c}\begin{array}[]{rcl}{\tt vr}_{\mathrm{v}_{k}}&\coloneqq&x\;|\;{\tt vr}_{\mathrm{v}_{k}}[x\backslash{\tt ne}_{\mathrm{v}_{k}}]\\[5.69046pt]
{\tt ne}_{\mathrm{v}_{k}}&\coloneqq&{\tt vr}_{\mathrm{v}_{k}}\,{\tt no}_{\mathrm{v}_{k}}\;|\;{\tt ne}_{\mathrm{v}_{k}}\,{\tt no}_{\mathrm{v}_{k}}\;|\;{\tt ne}_{\mathrm{v}_{k}}[x\backslash{\tt ne}_{\mathrm{v}_{k}}]\\[5.69046pt]

\Gamma_{1}+\Gamma_{2}\vdash s\mathtt{C}^{\prime}\!\left<u\right>:\sigma

\mathtt{C}=\mathtt{C}^{\prime}[x\backslash s]
: Then
\Pi
is
necessarily of the following form:

({\tt es})

\Pi_{1}\triangleright_{\mathcal{V}}\Gamma_{1},x:\mathcal{M}\vdash\mathtt{C}^{\prime}\!\left<t\right>:\sigma
\Pi_{2}\triangleright_{\mathcal{V}}\Gamma_{2}\vdash s:\mathcal{M}
\Gamma_{1}+\Gamma_{2}\vdash\mathtt{C}^{\prime}\!\left<t\right>[x\backslash s]:\sigma

with
\Gamma=\Gamma_{1}+\Gamma_{2}
. By
i.h.
on
\Pi_{1}
, one has

\Pi^{\prime}_{1}\triangleright_{\mathcal{V}}\Gamma_{1},x:\mathcal{M}\vdash\mathtt{C}^{\prime}\!\left<u\right>:\sigma

and we set
\Pi^{\prime}
as the following derivation:

({\tt es})

\Pi^{\prime}_{1}\triangleright_{\mathcal{V}}\Gamma_{1},x:\mathcal{M}\vdash\mathtt{C}^{\prime}\!\left<u\right>:\sigma
\Pi_{2}\triangleright_{\mathcal{V}}\Gamma_{2}\vdash s:\mathcal{M}
\Gamma_{1}+\Gamma_{2}\vdash\mathtt{C}^{\prime}\!\left<u\right>[x\backslash s]:\sigma

\mathtt{C}=s[x\backslash\mathtt{C}^{\prime}]
: Then
\Pi
is
necessarily of the following form:

({\tt es})

\Pi_{1}\triangleright_{\mathcal{V}}\Gamma_{1},x:\mathcal{M}\vdash s:\sigma
\Pi_{2}\triangleright_{\mathcal{V}}\Gamma_{2}\vdash\mathtt{C}^{\prime}\!\left<t\right>:\mathcal{M}
\Gamma_{1}+\Gamma_{2}\vdash s[x\backslash\mathtt{C}^{\prime}\!\left<t\right>]:\sigma

with
\Gamma=\Gamma_{1}+\Gamma_{2}
. By
i.h.
on
\Pi_{2}
, one has

\Pi_{1}\triangleright_{\mathcal{V}}\Gamma_{1},x:\mathcal{M}\vdash\mathtt{C}^{\prime}\!\left<u\right>:\sigma

and we set
\Pi^{\prime}
as the following derivation:

({\tt es})

\Pi_{1}\triangleright_{\mathcal{V}}\Gamma_{1},x:\mathcal{M}\vdash s:\sigma
\Pi^{\prime}_{2}\triangleright_{\mathcal{V}}\Gamma_{2}\vdash\mathtt{C}^{\prime}\!\left<u\right>:\mathcal{M}
\Gamma_{1}+\Gamma_{2}\vdash s[x\backslash\mathtt{C}^{\prime}\!\left<u\right>]:\sigma

∎

Appendix B
Proofs of Section
4

B.1.
Technical Lemmas

Lemma B.1.

Let
\mathbf{t}_{1},\mathbf{t}_{2}\in\mathbf{\Lambda}
and
\mathbf{v}_{1},\mathbf{v}_{2}\in\mathbf{\Upsilon}
such that
\mathbf{t}_{1}\leq\mathbf{t}_{2}
and
\mathbf{v}_{1}\leq\mathbf{v}_{2}
, then

\mathbf{t}_{1}\{x\backslash\mathbf{v}_{1}\}\leq\mathbf{t}_{2}\{x\backslash\mathbf{v}_{2}\}
.

Proof.

By induction on
\mathbf{t}_{1}
:

\mathbf{t}_{1}=\bot
: Then

\mathbf{t}_{1}\{x\backslash\mathbf{v}_{1}\}=\bot\leq\mathbf{t}_{2}\{x\backslash\mathbf{v}_{2}\}
.

\mathbf{t}_{1}=y
: Then
\mathbf{t}_{2}=y
and we distinguish
two cases:

x=y
: Thus
\mathbf{t}_{1}\{x\backslash\mathbf{v}_{1}\}=\mathbf{v}_{1}\leq\mathbf{v}_{2}=\mathbf{t}_{2}\{x\backslash\mathbf{v}_{2}\}
.

x\neq y
: Thus
\mathbf{t}_{1}\{x\backslash\mathbf{v}_{1}\}=y=\mathbf{t}_{2}\{x\backslash\mathbf{v}_{2}\}
, thus
\mathbf{t}_{1}\{x\backslash\mathbf{v}_{1}\}\leq\mathbf{t}_{2}\{x\backslash\mathbf{v}_{2}\}
by reflexivity of

\leq
.

\mathbf{t}_{1}=\lambda y.\mathbf{t}^{\prime}_{1}
: Then

\mathbf{t}_{1}\{x\backslash\mathbf{v}_{1}\}=\lambda y.\mathbf{t}^{\prime}_{1}\{x\backslash\mathbf{v}_{1}\}
(by supposing
y\notin{\tt fv}(\mathbf{v}_{1})\cup{\tt fv}(\mathbf{v}_{2})
without loss of
generality) and necessarily
\mathbf{t}_{2}=\lambda y.\mathbf{t}^{\prime}_{2}
with

\mathbf{t}^{\prime}_{1}\leq\mathbf{t}^{\prime}_{2}
thus
\mathbf{t}_{2}\{x\backslash\mathbf{v}_{2}\}=\lambda y.\mathbf{t}^{\prime}_{2}\{y\backslash\mathbf{v}_{2}\}
. By
i.h.
on
\mathbf{t}^{\prime}_{1}
,
one has that
\mathbf{t}^{\prime}_{1}\{x\backslash\mathbf{v}_{1}\}\leq\mathbf{t}^{\prime}_{2}\{x\backslash\mathbf{v}_{2}\}
hence
\mathbf{t}_{1}\{x\backslash\mathbf{v}_{1}\}=\lambda y.\mathbf{t}^{\prime}_{1}\{x\backslash\mathbf{v}_{1}\}\leq\lambda y.\mathbf{t}^{\prime}_{2}\{x\backslash\mathbf{v}_{2}\}=\mathbf{t}_{2}\{x\backslash\mathbf{v}_{2}\}

by contextual closure.

\mathbf{t}_{1}=\mathbf{t}_{1}^{1}\mathbf{t}_{1}^{2}
: Then

\mathbf{t}_{1}\{x\backslash\mathbf{v}_{1}\}=(\mathbf{t}_{1}^{1}\{x\backslash\mathbf{v}_{1}\})(\mathbf{t}_{1}^{2}\{x\backslash\mathbf{v}_{1}\})

and necessarily
\mathbf{t}_{2}=\mathbf{t}_{2}^{1}\,\mathbf{t}_{2}^{2}
with

\mathbf{t}_{1}^{1}\leq\mathbf{t}_{2}^{1}
and
\mathbf{t}_{1}^{2}\leq\mathbf{t}_{2}^{2}
, thus
\mathbf{t}_{2}\{y\backslash\mathbf{v}_{2}\}=(\mathbf{t}_{2}^{1}\{x\backslash\mathbf{v}_{2}\})(\mathbf{t}_{2}^{2}\{x\backslash\mathbf{v}_{2}\})
.
By
i.h.
on
\mathbf{t}_{1}^{1}
and
\mathbf{t}_{1}^{2}
, one obtains that

\mathbf{t}_{1}^{1}\{x\backslash\mathbf{v}_{1}\}\leq\mathbf{t}_{2}^{1}\{x\backslash\mathbf{v}_{2}\}
and
\mathbf{t}_{1}^{2}\{x\backslash\mathbf{v}_{1}\}\leq\mathbf{t}_{2}^{2}\{x\backslash\mathbf{v}_{2}\}
, hence

\mathbf{t}_{1}\{x\backslash\mathbf{v}_{1}\}=(\mathbf{t}_{1}^{1}\{x\backslash\mathbf{v}_{1}\})(\mathbf{t}_{1}^{2}\{x\backslash\mathbf{v}_{1}\})\leq(\mathbf{t}_{2}^{1}\{x\backslash\mathbf{v}_{2}\})(\mathbf{t}_{2}^{2}\{x\backslash\mathbf{v}_{2}\})=\mathbf{t}_{2}\{x\backslash\mathbf{v}_{2}\}
by contextual closure.

\mathbf{t}_{1}=\mathbf{t}_{1}^{1}[y\backslash\mathbf{t}_{1}^{2}]
: Then

\mathbf{t}_{1}\{x\backslash\mathbf{v}_{1}\}=\mathbf{t}_{1}^{1}\{x\backslash\mathbf{v}_{1}\}[y\backslash\mathbf{t}_{1}^{2}\{x\backslash\mathbf{v}_{1}\}]

and necessarily
\mathbf{t}_{2}=\mathbf{t}_{2}^{1}[y\backslash\mathbf{t}_{2}^{2}]
with

\mathbf{t}_{1}^{1}\leq\mathbf{t}_{2}^{1}
and
\mathbf{t}_{1}^{2}\leq\mathbf{t}_{2}^{2}
, thus
\mathbf{t}_{2}\{y\backslash\mathbf{v}_{2}\}=\mathbf{t}_{2}^{1}\{x\backslash\mathbf{v}_{2}\}[y\backslash\mathbf{t}_{2}^{2}\{x\backslash\mathbf{v}_{2}\}]
.
By
i.h.
on
\mathbf{t}_{1}^{1}
and
\mathbf{t}_{1}^{2}
, one has

\mathbf{t}_{1}^{1}\{x\backslash\mathbf{v}_{1}\}\leq\mathbf{t}_{2}^{1}\{x\backslash\mathbf{v}_{2}\}
and
\mathbf{t}_{1}^{2}\{x\backslash\mathbf{v}_{1}\}\leq\mathbf{t}_{2}^{2}\{x\backslash\mathbf{v}_{2}\}
, so by contextual
closure
\mathbf{t}_{1}\{x\backslash\mathbf{v}_{1}\}=\mathbf{t}_{1}^{1}\{x\backslash\mathbf{v}_{1}\}[y\backslash\mathbf{t}_{1}^{2}\{x\backslash\mathbf{v}_{1}\}]\leq\mathbf{t}_{2}^{1}\{x\backslash\mathbf{v}_{2}\}[y\backslash\mathbf{t}_{2}^{2}\{x\backslash\mathbf{v}_{2}\}]=\mathbf{t}_{2}\{x\backslash\mathbf{v}_{2}\}
.
∎

Definition B.2.

The partial order
\leq
is extended to
dCBV
contexts
by setting
\diamond\leq\diamond
.

For example,

\diamond[x\backslash\lambda y.\bot](z\bot)\leq\diamond[x\backslash\lambda y.(zz)](z\bot)
.

Lemma B.3.

Let
\mathbf{L}_{1}\leq\mathbf{L}_{2}
and
\mathbf{t}_{1}\leq\mathbf{t}_{2}
for some list contexts
\mathbf{L}_{1},\mathbf{L}_{2}
and some
\mathbf{t}_{1},\mathbf{t}_{2}\in\mathbf{\Lambda}
, then
\mathbf{L}_{1}\!\left<\mathbf{t}_{1}\right>\leq\mathbf{L}_{2}\!\left<\mathbf{t}_{2}\right>
.

Proof.

By induction on
\mathbf{L}_{1}
:

\mathbf{L}_{1}=\diamond
: Then necessarily

\mathbf{L}_{2}=\diamond
thus
\mathbf{L}_{1}\!\left<\mathbf{t}_{1}\right>=\mathbf{t}_{1}\leq\mathbf{t}_{2}=\mathbf{L}_{2}\!\left<\mathbf{t}_{2}\right>
.

\mathbf{L}_{1}=\mathbf{L}^{\prime}_{1}[x\backslash\mathbf{u}_{1}]
:
Then necessarily
\mathbf{L}_{2}=\mathbf{L}^{\prime}_{2}[x\backslash\mathbf{u}_{2}]
with
\mathbf{L}^{\prime}_{1}\leq\mathbf{L}^{\prime}_{2}
and
\mathbf{u}_{1}\leq\mathbf{u}_{2}
.
By
i.h.
on
\mathbf{L}^{\prime}_{1}
, one has that

\mathbf{L}^{\prime}_{1}\!\left<\mathbf{t}_{1}\right>\leq\mathbf{L}^{\prime}_{2}\!\left<\mathbf{t}_{2}\right>
thus
\mathbf{L}_{1}\!\left<\mathbf{t}_{1}\right>=\mathbf{L}^{\prime}_{1}\!\left<\mathbf{t}_{1}\right>[x\backslash\mathbf{u}_{1}]\leq\mathbf{L}^{\prime}_{2}\!\left<\mathbf{t}_{2}\right>[x\backslash\mathbf{u}_{2}]=\mathbf{L}_{2}\!\left<\mathbf{t}_{2}\right>
by contextual closure.
∎

Lemma B.4.

Let
t,u\in\Lambda
such that
t\rightarrow_{\mathrm{v}_{k}}u
.
Then,
t
is
dCBV
-meaningful if and only if
u
is

dCBV
-meaningful.

Proof.

According to qualitative subject reduction and expansion
(
Accattoli and Guerrieri 2022
, Prop.
7.4)
, given
t\rightarrow_{\mathrm{v}_{\omega}}u
,
there is a derivation
\Pi\triangleright_{\mathcal{V}}\Gamma\vdash t:\sigma
if and
only if there is a derivation
\Pi^{\prime}\triangleright_{\mathcal{V}}\Gamma\vdash u:\sigma
. As
\rightarrow_{\mathrm{v}_{k}}\subseteq\rightarrow_{\mathrm{v}_{\omega}}

for all
k\in\mathbb{N}
, the same equivalence also holds if
we replace the hypothesis
t\rightarrow_{\mathrm{v}_{\omega}}u
with
t\rightarrow_{\mathrm{v}_{k}}u
, for all
k\in\mathbb{N}
. We
conclude thanks to the type-theoretical characterization of

dCBV
-meaningfulness
(
Lemma

2.5
).
∎

Lemma B.5.

Let
t,u\in\Lambda
and
v\in\Upsilon
. Then

(1)

If
tu
is
dCBV
-meaningful then
t
and
u
are

dCBV
-meaningful.

(2)

If
t[x\backslash u]
is
dCBV
-meaningful then
t
and
u

are
dCBV
-meaningful.

(3)

If
t\{x\backslash v\}
is
dCBV
-meaningful then
t
is

dCBV
-meaningful.

Proof.

(1)

Let
tu
be
dCBV
-meaningful and suppose by absurd
that
t
is
dCBV
-meaningless. Using

Lemma

2.5
, we know that

tu
surface normalizes. Since
t
is

dCBV
-meaningless, there exists a
(t_{i})_{i\in\mathbb{N}}

with
t_{0}=t
and
t_{i}\rightarrow_{\mathrm{v}_{0}}t_{i+1}
yielding the
infinite path
tu\rightarrow_{\mathrm{v}_{0}}t_{1}u\rightarrow_{\mathrm{v}_{0}}t_{2}u\rightarrow_{\mathrm{v}_{0}}\dots
obtained by always reducing
t
.
Since surface reduction is diamond, this contradicts the fact that

tu
is surface normalizing. And similarly for

ut
.

(2)

Same principle as the previous item.

(3)

Let
t\{x\backslash v\}
be
dCBV
-meaningful. By induction on
t
,
one can show that if
t\rightarrow_{\mathrm{v}_{0}}u
then
t\{x\backslash v\}\rightarrow_{\mathrm{v}_{0}}u\{x\backslash v\}
. Using this, one applied the same
principles as the two previous points.

∎

Definition B.6.

Meaningful approximation is extended to list contexts
\mathtt{L}

inductively as follows:

\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt V}}\!\left(\diamond\right)\coloneqq\diamond\hskip 56.9055pt\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt V}}\!\left(\mathtt{L}[x\backslash s]\right)\coloneqq\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt V}}\!\left(\mathtt{L}\right)[x\backslash\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt V}}\!\left(s\right)]

Corollary B.7.

Let
\mathtt{L}\!\left<t\right>
be
dCBV
-meaningful, then

\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt V}}\!\left(\mathtt{L}\!\left<t\right>\right)=\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt V}}\!\left(\mathtt{L}\right)\!\left<\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt V}}\!\left(t\right)\right>
.

Proof.

By induction on
\mathtt{L}
:

\mathtt{L}=\diamond
: Then
\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt V}}\!\left(\mathtt{L}\!\left<t\right>\right)=\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt V}}\!\left(t\right)=\diamond\!\left<\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt V}}\!\left(t\right)\right>=\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt V}}\!\left(\diamond\right)\!\left<\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt V}}\!\left(t\right)\right>
.

\mathtt{L}=\mathtt{L}^{\prime}[x\backslash u]
: Then
\mathtt{L}\!\left<t\right>=\mathtt{L}^{\prime}\!\left<t\right>[x\backslash u]
is
dCBV
-meaningful so that

\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt V}}\!\left(\mathtt{L}\!\left<t\right>\right)=\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt V}}\!\left(\mathtt{L}^{\prime}\!\left<t\right>\right)[x\backslash\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt V}}\!\left(u\right)]
. Moreover,
using

Lemma

B.5
.(
2
),
one has that
\mathtt{L}^{\prime}\!\left<t\right>
is
dCBV
-meaningful. By
i.h.
on

\mathtt{L}^{\prime}
, one obtains that
\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt V}}\!\left(\mathtt{L}^{\prime}\!\left<t\right>\right)=\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt V}}\!\left(\mathtt{L}^{\prime}\right)\!\left<\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt V}}\!\left(t\right)\right>
. Then:

\begin{array}[]{r cl cl}\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt V}}\!\left(\mathtt{L}\!\left<t\right>\right)&=&\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt V}}\!\left(\mathtt{L}^{\prime}\!\left<t\right>\right)[x\backslash\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt V}}\!\left(u\right)]\\
&\overset{\textit{i.h.}}{=}&\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt V}}\!\left(\mathtt{L}^{\prime}\right)\!\left<\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt V}}\!\left(t\right)\right>[x\backslash\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt V}}\!\left(u\right)]\\
&=&\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt V}}\!\left(\mathtt{L}^{\prime}\right)[x\backslash\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt V}}\!\left(u\right)]\!\left<\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt V}}\!\left(t\right)\right>\\
&=&\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt V}}\!\left(\mathtt{L}^{\prime}[x\backslash u]\right)\!\left<\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt V}}\!\left(t\right)\right>&=&\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt V}}\!\left(\mathtt{L}\right)\!\left<\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt V}}\!\left(t\right)\right>\end{array}

∎

Lemma B.8.

Let
t\in\Lambda
and
v\in\Upsilon
, then

\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt V}}\!\left(t\{x\backslash v\}\right)\leq\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt V}}\!\left(t\right)\{x\backslash\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt V}}\!\left(v\right)\}
.

Proof.

Let
t\in\Lambda
and
v\in\Upsilon
. Cases:

t\{x\backslash v\}
is
dCBV
-meaningless: Then

\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt V}}\!\left(t\{x\backslash v\}\right)=\bot\leq\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt V}}\!\left(t\right)\{x\backslash\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt V}}\!\left(v\right)\}
.

t\{x\backslash v\}
is
dCBV
-meaningful:
Using
Lemma

B.5
.(
3
),
one deduces that
t
is
dCBV
-meaningful. We proceed by
induction on
t
. Cases:

t=y
: Then
\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt V}}\!\left(t\right)=y
and we
distinguish two cases:

\bullet

y=x
: Then
\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt V}}\!\left(t\{x\backslash v\}\right)=\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt V}}\!\left(v\right)=x\{x\backslash\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt V}}\!\left(v\right)\}=\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt V}}\!\left(t\right)\{x\backslash\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt V}}\!\left(v\right)\}
.

\bullet

y\neq x
: Then
\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt V}}\!\left(t\{x\backslash v\}\right)=\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt V}}\!\left(y\right)=y=y\{x\backslash\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt V}}\!\left(v\right)\}=\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt V}}\!\left(t\right)\{x\backslash\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt V}}\!\left(v\right)\}
.

t=\lambda y.t^{\prime}
: Then
\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt V}}\!\left(t\right)=\lambda y.\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt V}}\!\left(t^{\prime}\right)
. By
i.h.
on
t^{\prime}
, one has that

\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt V}}\!\left(t^{\prime}\{x\backslash v\}\right)\leq\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt V}}\!\left(t^{\prime}\right)\{x\backslash\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt V}}\!\left(v\right)\}
. Therefore:

\begin{array}[]{r cl cl}\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt V}}\!\left(t\{x\backslash v\}\right)&=&\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt V}}\!\left(\lambda y.(t^{\prime}\{x\backslash v\})\right)\\
&=&\lambda y.\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt V}}\!\left(t^{\prime}\{x\backslash v\}\right)\\
&\overset{\textit{i.h.}}{\leq}&\lambda y.\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt V}}\!\left(t^{\prime}\right)\{x\backslash\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt V}}\!\left(v\right)\}\\
&=&(\lambda y.\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt V}}\!\left(t^{\prime}\right))\{x\backslash\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt V}}\!\left(v\right)\}&=&\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt V}}\!\left(t\right)\{x\backslash\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt V}}\!\left(v\right)\}\end{array}

t=t_{1}t_{2}
: Thus
\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt V}}\!\left(t\right)=\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt V}}\!\left(t_{1}\right)\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt V}}\!\left(t_{2}\right)
. By
i.h.
on
t_{1}
and

t_{2}
, one has that
\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt V}}\!\left(t_{1}\{x\backslash v\}\right)\leq\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt V}}\!\left(t_{1}\right)\{x\backslash\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt V}}\!\left(v\right)\}
and

\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt V}}\!\left(t_{2}\{x\backslash v\}\right)\leq\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt V}}\!\left(t_{2}\right)\{x\backslash\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt V}}\!\left(v\right)\}
. Therefore:

\begin{array}[]{r cl cl}\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt V}}\!\left(t\{x\backslash v\}\right)&=&\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt V}}\!\left(t_{1}\{x\backslash v\}t_{2}\{x\backslash v\}\right)\\
&=&\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt V}}\!\left(t_{1}\{x\backslash v\}\right)\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt V}}\!\left(t_{2}\{x\backslash v\}\right)\\
&\overset{\textit{i.h.}}{\leq}&(\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt V}}\!\left(t_{1}\right)\{x\backslash\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt V}}\!\left(v\right)\})(\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt V}}\!\left(t_{2}\right)\{x\backslash\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt V}}\!\left(v\right)\})\\
&=&(\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt V}}\!\left(t_{1}\right)\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt V}}\!\left(t_{2}\right))\{x\backslash\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt V}}\!\left(v\right)\}&=&\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt V}}\!\left(t\right)\{x\backslash\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt V}}\!\left(v\right)\}\end{array}

t=t_{1}[y\backslash t_{2}]
: Thus
\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt V}}\!\left(t\right)=\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt V}}\!\left(t_{1}\right)[y\backslash\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt V}}\!\left(t_{2}\right)]
. By
i.h.
on
t_{1}

and
t_{2}
, one has that
\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt V}}\!\left(t_{1}\{x\backslash v\}\right)\leq\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt V}}\!\left(t_{1}\right)\{x\backslash\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt V}}\!\left(v\right)\}
and

\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt V}}\!\left(t_{2}\{x\backslash v\}\right)\leq\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt V}}\!\left(t_{2}\right)\{x\backslash\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt V}}\!\left(v\right)\}
. Therefore:

\begin{array}[]{r cl cl}\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt V}}\!\left(t\{x\backslash v\}\right)&=&\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt V}}\!\left(t_{1}\{x\backslash v\}[y\backslash t_{2}\{x\backslash v\}]\right)\\
&=&\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt V}}\!\left(t_{1}\{x\backslash v\}\right)[y\backslash\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt V}}\!\left(t_{2}\{x\backslash v\}\right)]\\
&\overset{\textit{i.h.}}{\leq}&\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt V}}\!\left(t_{1}\right)\{x\backslash\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt V}}\!\left(v\right)\}[y\backslash\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt V}}\!\left(t_{2}\right)\{x\backslash\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt V}}\!\left(v\right)\}]\\
&=&(\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt V}}\!\left(t_{1}\right)[y\backslash\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt V}}\!\left(t_{2}\right)])\{x\backslash\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt V}}\!\left(v\right)\}&=&\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt V}}\!\left(t\right)\{x\backslash\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt V}}\!\left(v\right)\}\end{array}

∎

B.2.
Proofs of Subsection

4.2

See
4.3

Proof.

We distinguish two cases:

t
is meaningless: Then
\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt V}}\!\left(t\right)=\bot\leq t
.

t
is meaningful: We proceed by induction on
t
.
Subcases:

t=x
: Then
\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt V}}\!\left(t\right)=x\leq x=t
.

t=\lambda x.t^{\prime}
: By
i.h.
on
t^{\prime}
, one has that

\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt V}}\!\left(t^{\prime}\right)\leq t^{\prime}
thus
\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt V}}\!\left(t\right)=\lambda x.\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt V}}\!\left(t^{\prime}\right)\leq\lambda x.t^{\prime}=t
.

t=t_{1}t_{2}
: By
i.h.
on
t_{1}
and
t_{2}
, one
has that
\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt V}}\!\left(t_{1}\right)\leq t_{1}
and

\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt V}}\!\left(t_{2}\right)\leq t_{2}
so that
\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt V}}\!\left(t\right)=\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt V}}\!\left(t_{1}\right)\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt V}}\!\left(t_{2}\right)\leq t_{1}t_{2}=t
.

t=t_{1}[x\backslash t_{2}]
: By
i.h.
on
t_{1}
and
t_{2}
,

\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt V}}\!\left(t_{1}\right)\leq t_{1}
and
\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt V}}\!\left(t_{2}\right)\leq t_{2}
, hence
\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt V}}\!\left(t\right)=\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt V}}\!\left(t_{1}\right)[x\backslash\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt V}}\!\left(t_{2}\right)]\leq t_{1}[x\backslash t_{2}]=t
.
∎

See
4.4

Proof.

Let
\mathtt{C}
be a full context and
t,u\in\Lambda
be such
that
t
is
dCBV
-meaningless. We distinguish two cases:

\mathtt{C}\!\left<t\right>
is
dCBV
-meaningless: Then

\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt V}}\!\left(\mathtt{C}\!\left<t\right>\right)=\bot\leq\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt V}}\!\left(\mathtt{C}\!\left<u\right>\right)
.

\mathtt{C}\!\left<t\right>
is meaningful: Then by surface genericity
(
Theorem

2.8
), one has that

\mathtt{C}\!\left<u\right>
is also meaningful. We proceed by induction on

\mathtt{C}
:

\mathtt{C}=\diamond
: This case is impossible because
it would mean
\mathtt{C}\!\left<t\right>=t
is
dCBV
-meaningless (by
hypothesis) and meaningful at the same time.

\mathtt{C}=\lambda x.\mathtt{C}^{\prime}
: By
i.h.
on

\mathtt{C}^{\prime}
, one has
\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt V}}\!\left(\mathtt{C}^{\prime}\!\left<t\right>\right)\leq\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt V}}\!\left(\mathtt{C}^{\prime}\!\left<u\right>\right)
, thus
\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt V}}\!\left(\mathtt{C}\!\left<t\right>\right)=\lambda x.\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt V}}\!\left(\mathtt{C}^{\prime}\!\left<t\right>\right)\overset{\textit{i.h.}}{\leq}\lambda x.\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt V}}\!\left(\mathtt{C}^{\prime}\!\left<u\right>\right)=\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt V}}\!\left(\mathtt{C}\!\left<u^{\prime}\right>\right)
by contextual closure.

\mathtt{C}=\mathtt{C}^{\prime}s
: By
i.h.
on

\mathtt{C}^{\prime}
, one has
\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt V}}\!\left(\mathtt{C}^{\prime}\!\left<t\right>\right)\leq\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt V}}\!\left(\mathtt{C}^{\prime}\!\left<u\right>\right)
, thus
\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt V}}\!\left(\mathtt{C}\!\left<t\right>\right)=\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt V}}\!\left(\mathtt{C}^{\prime}\!\left<t\right>\right)\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt V}}\!\left(s\right)\overset{\textit{i.h.}}{\leq}\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt V}}\!\left(\mathtt{C}^{\prime}\!\left<u\right>\right)\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt V}}\!\left(s\right)\allowbreak=\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt V}}\!\left(\mathtt{C}\!\left<u^{\prime}\right>\right)
by contextual closure.

\mathtt{C}=s\,\mathtt{C}^{\prime}
: By
i.h.
on

\mathtt{C}^{\prime}
, one has
\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt V}}\!\left(\mathtt{C}^{\prime}\!\left<t\right>\right)\leq\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt V}}\!\left(\mathtt{C}^{\prime}\!\left<u\right>\right)
, thus
\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt V}}\!\left(\mathtt{C}\!\left<t\right>\right)=\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt V}}\!\left(s\right)\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt V}}\!\left(\mathtt{C}^{\prime}\!\left<t\right>\right)\overset{\textit{i.h.}}{\leq}\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt V}}\!\left(s\right)\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt V}}\!\left(\mathtt{C}^{\prime}\!\left<u\right>\right)\allowbreak=\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt V}}\!\left(\mathtt{C}\!\left<u^{\prime}\right>\right)
by contextual closure.

\mathtt{C}=\mathtt{C}^{\prime}[x\backslash s]
: By
i.h.
on

\mathtt{C}^{\prime}
, one has
\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt V}}\!\left(\mathtt{C}^{\prime}\!\left<t\right>\right)\leq\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt V}}\!\left(\mathtt{C}^{\prime}\!\left<u\right>\right)
, and hence

\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt V}}\!\left(\mathtt{C}\!\left<t\right>\right)=\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt V}}\!\left(\mathtt{C}^{\prime}\!\left<t\right>\right)[x\backslash\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt V}}\!\left(s\right)]\overset{\textit{i.h.}}{\leq}\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt V}}\!\left(\mathtt{C}^{\prime}\!\left<u\right>\right)[x\backslash\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt V}}\!\left(s\right)]=\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt V}}\!\left(\mathtt{C}\!\left<u^{\prime}\right>\right)
by contextual closure.

\mathtt{C}=s[x\backslash\mathtt{C}^{\prime}]
: By
i.h.
on

\mathtt{C}^{\prime}
, one has
\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt V}}\!\left(\mathtt{C}^{\prime}\!\left<t\right>\right)\leq\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt V}}\!\left(\mathtt{C}^{\prime}\!\left<u\right>\right)
, and hence

\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt V}}\!\left(\mathtt{C}\!\left<t\right>\right)=\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt V}}\!\left(s\right)[x\backslash\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt V}}\!\left(\mathtt{C}^{\prime}\!\left<t\right>\right)]\overset{\textit{i.h.}}{\leq}\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt V}}\!\left(s\right)[x\backslash\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt V}}\!\left(\mathtt{C}^{\prime}\!\left<u\right>\right)]=\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt V}}\!\left(\mathtt{C}\!\left<u^{\prime}\right>\right)
by contextual closure.
∎

See
4.6

Proof.

We distinguish two cases:

t
is
dCBV
-meaningless: Then
u
is necessarily

dCBV
-meaningless by

Lemma

B.4
, thus

\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt V}}\!\left(t\right)=\bot
and
\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt V}}\!\left(u\right)=\bot
.
We set
\mathbf{u}\coloneqq\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt V}}\!\left(u\right)
, therefore by reflexivity
of both relations
\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt V}}\!\left(t\right)\rightarrow^{=}_{\textbf{v}_{k}}\mathbf{u}\geq\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt V}}\!\left(u\right)
.

t
is
dCBV
-meaningful: Then
u
is necessarily

dCBV
-meaningful by

Lemma

B.4
. Let
t,u\in\Lambda
such that
t\rightarrow_{\mathrm{v}_{k}}u
. Then
there exists a context
\mathtt{V}_{k}
and
t^{\prime},u^{\prime}\in\Lambda
such that
t=\mathtt{V}_{k}\!\left<t^{\prime}\right>
,
u=\mathtt{V}_{k}\!\left<u^{\prime}\right>
and
t^{\prime}\mapsto_{\mathcal{R}}u^{\prime}
for some
\mathcal{R}\in\{{\tt dB},{\tt sV}\}
. We reason by induction on

\mathtt{V}_{k}
:

\mathtt{V}_{k}=\diamond
: Then
t=t^{\prime}
and
u=u^{\prime}
. We distinguish two cases:

\bullet

\mathcal{R}={\tt dB}
: Then
t^{\prime}=\mathtt{L}\!\left<\lambda x.s_{1}\right>s_{2}
and
u^{\prime}=\mathtt{L}\!\left<s_{1}[x\backslash s_{2}]\right>
for some list context

\mathtt{L}
and some

. Since

is
dCBV
-meaningful, then using

Lemma

B.5
.(
1
),
one deduces in particular that
\mathtt{L}\!\left<\lambda x.s_{1}\right>

is
dCBV
-meaningful. Since
u^{\prime}
is
dCBV
-meaningful,
then using

Lemma

B.5
.(
2
),
one deduces in particular that
s_{1}[x\backslash s_{2}]
is

dCBV
-meaningful. Thus, using

Corollary

B.7

\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt V}}\!\left(t^{\prime}\right)=\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt V}}\!\left(\mathtt{L}\!\left<\lambda x.s_{1}\right>\right)\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt V}}\!\left(s_{2}\right)=\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt V}}\!\left(\mathtt{L}\right)\!\left<\lambda x.\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt V}}\!\left(s_{1}\right)\right>\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt V}}\!\left(s_{2}\right)

and
\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt V}}\!\left(u^{\prime}\right)=\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt V}}\!\left(\mathtt{L}\!\left<s_{1}[x\backslash s_{2}]\right>\right)=\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt V}}\!\left(\mathtt{L}\right)\!\left<\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt V}}\!\left(s_{1}[x\backslash s_{2}]\right)\right>=\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt V}}\!\left(\mathtt{L}\right)\!\left<\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt V}}\!\left(s_{1}\right)[x\backslash\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt V}}\!\left(s_{2}\right)]\right>
.

\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt V}}\!\left(t\right)=\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt V}}\!\left(t^{\prime}\right)\mapsto_{{\tt dB}}\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt V}}\!\left(u^{\prime}\right)=\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt V}}\!\left(u\right)
.
We set
\mathbf{u}:=\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt V}}\!\left(u\right)
, therefore by
reflexivity
\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt V}}\!\left(t\right)\rightarrow_{\textbf{v}_{k}}\mathbf{u}\geq\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt V}}\!\left(u\right)
.

\bullet

\mathcal{R}={\tt sV}
: Then
t^{\prime}=s[x\backslash\mathtt{L}\!\left<v\right>]
and

for some list context

\mathtt{L}
, and some

and

. Since

is
dCBV
-meaningful, then

\mathtt{L}\!\left<v\right>
is
dCBV
-meaningful by

Lemma

B.5
.(
2
).
Since
u^{\prime}
is
dCBV
-meaningful, then using

B.5
.(
2
),
one deduces that
s\{x\backslash v\}
is
dCBV
-meaningful.
Using

B.7

and

,
one has that
\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt V}}\!\left(t^{\prime}\right)=\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt V}}\!\left(s\right)[x\backslash\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt V}}\!\left(\mathtt{L}\right)\!\left<\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt V}}\!\left(v\right)\right>]

and
\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt V}}\!\left(u^{\prime}\right)\leq\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt V}}\!\left(\mathtt{L}\right)\!\left<\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt V}}\!\left(s\right)\{x\backslash\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt V}}\!\left(v\right)\}\right>
.
Thus
\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt V}}\!\left(t\right)=\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt V}}\!\left(t^{\prime}\right)\mapsto_{{\tt sV}}\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt V}}\!\left(\mathtt{L}\right)\!\left<\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt V}}\!\left(s\right)\{x\backslash\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt V}}\!\left(v\right)\}\right>\geq\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt V}}\!\left(u^{\prime}\right)=\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt V}}\!\left(u\right)
. We set

\mathbf{u}\coloneqq\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt V}}\!\left(\mathtt{L}\right)\!\left<\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt V}}\!\left(s\right)\{x\backslash\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt V}}\!\left(v\right)\}\right>
,
therefore
\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt V}}\!\left(t\right)\rightarrow_{\textbf{v}_{k}}\mathbf{u}\geq\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt V}}\!\left(u\right)
.

\mathtt{V}_{k}=\lambda x.\mathtt{V}_{k-1}

with
k>0
or
k=\omega
: By
i.h.
on

\mathtt{V}_{k-1}
, there is
\mathbf{u}^{\prime}
such that

\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt V}}\!\left(\mathtt{V}_{k-1}\!\left<t^{\prime}\right>\right)\rightarrow^{=}_{\textbf{v}_{k-1}}\mathbf{u}^{\prime}\geq\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt V}}\!\left(\mathtt{V}_{k-1}\!\left<u^{\prime}\right>\right)
. We set

.

\mathtt{V}_{k}=\mathtt{V}^{\prime}_{k}s
:
Thus

\mathtt{V}^{\prime}_{k}

\mathtt{V}^{\prime}_{k}

See
4.7

Proof.

Let
\mathbf{t},\mathbf{u},\mathbf{t}^{\prime}\in\mathbf{\Lambda}
such that
\mathbf{t}\leq\mathbf{t}^{\prime}
and
\mathbf{t}\rightarrow_{\textbf{v}_{k}}\mathbf{u}
. Then

\mathbf{t}=\mathbf{V}_{k}\!\left<\mathbf{t}_{r}\right>
and
\mathbf{u}=\mathbf{V}_{k}\!\left<\mathbf{u}_{r}\right>
with
\mathbf{t}_{r}\mapsto_{\mathcal{R}}\mathbf{u}_{r}
for
some
\mathbf{t}_{r},\mathbf{u}_{r}\in\mathbf{\Lambda}
and
\mathcal{R}\in\{{\tt dB},{\tt sV}\}
. Let us proceed by induction on

\mathbf{V}_{k}
:

\mathbf{V}_{k}=\diamond
: Then
\mathbf{t}=\mathbf{t}_{r}

and
\mathbf{u}=\mathbf{u}_{r}
. We distinguish two cases:

\mathcal{R}={\tt dB}
: Then
\mathbf{t}_{r}=\mathbf{L}\!\left<\lambda x.\mathbf{s}_{1}\right>\mathbf{s}_{2}
and

\mathbf{u}_{r}=\mathbf{L}\!\left<\mathbf{s}_{1}[x\backslash\mathbf{s}_{2}]\right>
for
some list context
\mathbf{L}
and
\mathbf{s}_{1},\mathbf{s}_{2}\in\mathbf{\Lambda}
. Since
\mathbf{t}\leq\mathbf{t}^{\prime}
, by
induction on
\mathbf{L}
, one can prove that
\mathbf{t}^{\prime}=\mathbf{L}^{\prime}\!\left<\lambda x.\mathbf{s}^{\prime}_{1}\right>\mathbf{s}^{\prime}_{2}
with

\mathbf{L}\leq\mathbf{L}^{\prime}
,
\mathbf{s}_{1}\leq\mathbf{s}^{\prime}_{1}
and
\mathbf{s}_{2}\leq\mathbf{s}^{\prime}_{2}

for some list context
\mathbf{L}^{\prime}
and
\mathbf{s}^{\prime}_{1},\mathbf{s}^{\prime}_{2}\in\mathbf{\Lambda}
. Taking
\mathbf{u}^{\prime}=\mathbf{L}^{\prime}\!\left<\mathbf{s}^{\prime}_{1}[x\backslash\mathbf{s}^{\prime}_{2}]\right>
concludes this
case since
\mathbf{t}^{\prime}=\mathbf{L}^{\prime}\!\left<\lambda x.\mathbf{s}^{\prime}_{1}\right>\mathbf{s}^{\prime}_{2}\rightarrow_{\textbf{v}_{k}}\mathbf{L}^{\prime}\!\left<\mathbf{s}^{\prime}_{1}[x\backslash\mathbf{s}^{\prime}_{2}]\right>=\mathbf{u}^{\prime}
and by
transitivity
\mathbf{s}_{1}[x\backslash\mathbf{s}_{2}]\leq\mathbf{s}^{\prime}_{1}[x\backslash\mathbf{s}^{\prime}_{2}]
thus

\mathbf{L}\!\left<\mathbf{s}_{1}[x\backslash\mathbf{s}_{2}]\right>\leq\mathbf{L}^{\prime}\!\left<\mathbf{s}^{\prime}_{1}[x\backslash\mathbf{s}^{\prime}_{2}]\right>=\mathbf{u}^{\prime}
using

Lemma

B.3
.

\mathcal{R}={\tt sV}
: Then
\mathbf{t}_{r}=\mathbf{s}[x\backslash\mathbf{L}\!\left<\mathbf{v}\right>]
and
\mathbf{u}_{r}=\mathbf{L}\!\left<\mathbf{s}\{x\backslash\mathbf{v}\}\right>
for some list context

\mathbf{L}
,
\mathbf{s}\in\mathbf{\Lambda}
and
\mathbf{v}\in\mathbf{\Upsilon}
. Since
\mathbf{t}\leq\mathbf{t}^{\prime}
, by
induction on
\mathbf{L}
, one can prove that
\mathbf{t}^{\prime}=\mathbf{s}^{\prime}[x\backslash\mathbf{L}^{\prime}\!\left<\mathbf{v}^{\prime}\right>]
with
\mathbf{L}\leq\mathbf{L}^{\prime}
,
\mathbf{s}\leq\mathbf{s}^{\prime}
and

\mathbf{v}\leq\mathbf{v}^{\prime}
for some list context

\mathbf{L}^{\prime}
,
\mathbf{s}^{\prime}\in\mathbf{\Lambda}
and
\mathbf{v}^{\prime}\in\mathbf{\Upsilon}
. Taking
\mathbf{u}^{\prime}=\mathbf{L}^{\prime}\!\left<\mathbf{s}^{\prime}\{x\backslash\mathbf{v}^{\prime}\}\right>
concludes this case
since
\mathbf{t}^{\prime}=\mathbf{s}^{\prime}[x\backslash\mathbf{L}^{\prime}\!\left<\mathbf{v}^{\prime}\right>]\rightarrow_{\textbf{v}_{k}}\mathbf{L}^{\prime}\!\left<\mathbf{s}^{\prime}\{x\backslash\mathbf{v}^{\prime}\}\right>
and by

Lemma

B.1

\mathbf{s}\{x\backslash\mathbf{v}\}\leq\mathbf{s}^{\prime}\{x\backslash\mathbf{v}^{\prime}\}
,
thus
\mathbf{L}\!\left<\mathbf{s}\{x\backslash\mathbf{v}\}\right>\leq\mathbf{L}^{\prime}\!\left<\mathbf{s}^{\prime}\{x\backslash\mathbf{v}^{\prime}\}\right>=\mathbf{u}^{\prime}
using

Lemma

B.3
.

\mathbf{V}_{k}=\lambda x.\mathbf{V}^{\prime}_{m}
with
either
k>0
and
m=k-1
, or
k=m=\omega
: Then
\mathbf{t}=\lambda x.\mathbf{V}^{\prime}_{m}\!\left<\mathbf{t}_{r}\right>
and
\mathbf{u}=\lambda x.\mathbf{V}^{\prime}_{m}\!\left<\mathbf{u}_{r}\right>
thus
\mathbf{t}^{\prime}=\lambda x.\mathbf{t}^{\prime}_{1}
for some
\mathbf{t}^{\prime}_{1}\in\mathbf{\Lambda}

such that
\mathbf{V}^{\prime}_{m}\!\left<\mathbf{t}_{r}\right>\leq\mathbf{t}^{\prime}_{1}
.
Since
\mathbf{V}^{\prime}_{m}\!\left<\mathbf{t}_{r}\right>\rightarrow_{\textbf{v}_{m}}\mathbf{V}^{\prime}_{m}\!\left<\mathbf{u}_{r}\right>
, by
i.h.
on
\mathbf{V}^{\prime}_{m}
, one
obtains
\mathbf{u}^{\prime}_{1}\in\mathbf{\Lambda}
such that
\mathbf{t}^{\prime}_{1}\rightarrow_{\textbf{v}_{m}}\mathbf{u}^{\prime}_{1}
and
\mathbf{V}^{\prime}_{m}\!\left<\mathbf{u}_{r}\right>\leq\mathbf{u}^{\prime}_{1}
. Taking
\mathbf{u}^{\prime}=\lambda x.\mathbf{u}^{\prime}_{1}

concludes this case since
\mathbf{t}^{\prime}=\lambda x.\mathbf{t}^{\prime}_{1}\rightarrow_{\textbf{v}_{k}}\lambda x.\mathbf{u}^{\prime}_{1}=\mathbf{u}^{\prime}
and
\mathbf{u}=\lambda x.\mathbf{V}^{\prime}_{k}\!\left<\mathbf{u}_{r}\right>\leq\lambda x.\mathbf{u}^{\prime}_{1}=\mathbf{u}^{\prime}
.

\mathbf{V}_{k}=\mathbf{V}^{\prime}_{k}\,\mathbf{s}
: Then
\mathbf{t}=\mathbf{V}^{\prime}_{k}\!\left<\mathbf{t}_{r}\right>\mathbf{s}
and
\mathbf{u}=\mathbf{V}^{\prime}_{k}\!\left<\mathbf{u}_{r}\right>\mathbf{s}
thus
\mathbf{t}^{\prime}=\mathbf{t}^{\prime}_{1}\,\mathbf{s}^{\prime}
for some
\mathbf{t}^{\prime}_{1},\mathbf{s}\in\mathbf{\Lambda}
such that
\mathbf{V}^{\prime}_{k}\!\left<\mathbf{t}_{r}\right>\leq\mathbf{t}^{\prime}_{1}
and
\mathbf{s}\leq\mathbf{s}^{\prime}
. Since

\mathbf{V}^{\prime}_{k}\!\left<\mathbf{t}_{r}\right>\rightarrow_{\textbf{v}_{k}}\mathbf{V}^{\prime}_{k}\!\left<\mathbf{u}_{r}\right>
, by
i.h.
on

\mathbf{V}^{\prime}_{k}
, one obtains
\mathbf{u}^{\prime}_{1}\in\mathbf{\Lambda}
such that
\mathbf{t}^{\prime}_{1}\rightarrow_{\textbf{v}_{k}}\mathbf{u}^{\prime}_{1}
and
\mathbf{V}^{\prime}_{k}\!\left<\mathbf{u}_{r}\right>\leq\mathbf{u}^{\prime}_{1}
. Taking
\mathbf{u}^{\prime}=\mathbf{u}^{\prime}_{1}\mathbf{s}^{\prime}
concludes
this case since
\mathbf{t}^{\prime}=\mathbf{t}^{\prime}_{1}\mathbf{s}^{\prime}\rightarrow_{\textbf{v}_{k}}\mathbf{u}^{\prime}_{1}\mathbf{s}^{\prime}=\mathbf{u}^{\prime}
and

\mathbf{u}=\mathbf{V}^{\prime}_{k}\!\left<\mathbf{u}_{r}\right>\mathbf{s}\leq\mathbf{u}^{\prime}_{1}\mathbf{s}^{\prime}=\mathbf{u}^{\prime}
by transitivity.

\mathbf{V}_{k}=\mathbf{s}\,\mathbf{V}^{\prime}_{k}
: Then
\mathbf{t}=\mathbf{s}\,\mathbf{V}^{\prime}_{k}\!\left<\mathbf{t}_{r}\right>
and
\mathbf{u}=\mathbf{s}\,\mathbf{V}^{\prime}_{k}\!\left<\mathbf{u}_{r}\right>
thus
\mathbf{t}^{\prime}=\mathbf{s}^{\prime}\,\mathbf{t}^{\prime}_{1}
for some
\mathbf{s},\mathbf{t}^{\prime}_{1}\in\mathbf{\Lambda}
such that
\mathbf{s}\leq\mathbf{s}^{\prime}
and

\mathbf{V}^{\prime}_{k}\!\left<\mathbf{t}_{r}\right>\leq\mathbf{t}^{\prime}_{1}
. Since

\mathbf{V}^{\prime}_{k}\!\left<\mathbf{t}_{r}\right>\rightarrow_{\textbf{v}_{k}}\mathbf{V}^{\prime}_{k}\!\left<\mathbf{u}_{r}\right>
, by
i.h.
on

\mathbf{V}^{\prime}_{k}
, one obtains
\mathbf{u}^{\prime}_{1}\in\mathbf{\Lambda}
such that
\mathbf{t}^{\prime}_{1}\rightarrow_{\textbf{v}_{k}}\mathbf{u}^{\prime}_{1}
and
\mathbf{V}^{\prime}_{k}\!\left<\mathbf{u}_{r}\right>\leq\mathbf{u}^{\prime}_{1}
. Taking
\mathbf{u}^{\prime}=\mathbf{s}^{\prime}\mathbf{u}^{\prime}_{1}
concludes
this case since
\mathbf{t}^{\prime}=\mathbf{s}^{\prime}\mathbf{t}^{\prime}_{1}\rightarrow_{\textbf{v}_{k}}\mathbf{s}^{\prime}\mathbf{u}^{\prime}_{1}=\mathbf{u}^{\prime}
and

\mathbf{u}=\mathbf{s}\,\mathbf{V}^{\prime}_{k}\!\left<\mathbf{u}_{r}\right>\leq\mathbf{s}^{\prime}\mathbf{u}^{\prime}_{1}=\mathbf{u}^{\prime}
by transitivity.

\mathbf{V}_{k}=\mathbf{V}^{\prime}_{k}[x\backslash\mathbf{s}]
: Then
\mathbf{t}=\mathbf{V}^{\prime}_{k}\!\left<\mathbf{t}_{r}\right>[x\backslash\mathbf{s}]
and
\mathbf{u}=\mathbf{V}^{\prime}_{k}\!\left<\mathbf{u}_{r}\right>[x\backslash\mathbf{s}]
thus
\mathbf{t}^{\prime}=\mathbf{t}^{\prime}_{1}[x\backslash\mathbf{s}^{\prime}]
for some
\mathbf{t}^{\prime}_{1},\mathbf{s}\in\mathbf{\Lambda}
such that
\mathbf{V}^{\prime}_{k}\!\left<\mathbf{t}_{r}\right>\leq\mathbf{t}^{\prime}_{1}
and
\mathbf{s}\leq\mathbf{s}^{\prime}
. Since

\mathbf{V}^{\prime}_{k}\!\left<\mathbf{t}_{r}\right>\rightarrow_{\textbf{v}_{k}}\mathbf{V}^{\prime}_{k}\!\left<\mathbf{u}_{r}\right>
, by
i.h.
on

\mathbf{V}^{\prime}_{k}
, one obtains
\mathbf{u}^{\prime}_{1}\in\mathbf{\Lambda}
such that
\mathbf{t}^{\prime}_{1}\rightarrow_{\textbf{v}_{k}}\mathbf{u}^{\prime}_{1}
and
\mathbf{V}^{\prime}_{k}\!\left<\mathbf{u}_{r}\right>\leq\mathbf{u}^{\prime}_{1}
. Taking
\mathbf{u}^{\prime}=\mathbf{u}^{\prime}_{1}[x\backslash\mathbf{s}^{\prime}]

concludes this case since
\mathbf{t}^{\prime}=\mathbf{t}^{\prime}_{1}[x\backslash\mathbf{s}^{\prime}]\rightarrow_{\textbf{v}_{k}}\mathbf{u}^{\prime}_{1}[x\backslash\mathbf{s}^{\prime}]=\mathbf{u}^{\prime}
and

\mathbf{u}=\mathbf{V}^{\prime}_{k}\!\left<\mathbf{u}_{r}\right>[x\backslash\mathbf{s}]\leq\mathbf{u}^{\prime}_{1}[x\backslash\mathbf{s}^{\prime}]=\mathbf{u}^{\prime}
by transitivity.

\mathbf{V}_{k}=\mathbf{s}[x\backslash\mathbf{V}^{\prime}_{k}]
: Then
\mathbf{t}=\mathbf{s}[x\backslash\mathbf{V}^{\prime}_{k}\!\left<\mathbf{t}_{r}\right>]
and
\mathbf{u}=\mathbf{s}[x\backslash\mathbf{V}^{\prime}_{k}\!\left<\mathbf{u}_{r}\right>]
thus
\mathbf{t}^{\prime}=\mathbf{s}^{\prime}[x\backslash\mathbf{t}^{\prime}_{1}]
for some
\mathbf{s},\mathbf{t}^{\prime}_{1}\in\mathbf{\Lambda}
such that
\mathbf{s}\leq\mathbf{s}^{\prime}
and

\mathbf{V}^{\prime}_{k}\!\left<\mathbf{t}_{r}\right>\leq\mathbf{t}^{\prime}_{1}
. Since

\mathbf{V}^{\prime}_{k}\!\left<\mathbf{t}_{r}\right>\rightarrow_{\textbf{v}_{k}}\mathbf{V}^{\prime}_{k}\!\left<\mathbf{u}_{r}\right>
, by
i.h.
on

\mathbf{V}^{\prime}_{k}
, one obtains
\mathbf{u}^{\prime}_{1}\in\mathbf{\Lambda}
such that
\mathbf{t}^{\prime}_{1}\rightarrow_{\textbf{v}_{k}}\mathbf{u}^{\prime}_{1}
and
\mathbf{V}^{\prime}_{k}\!\left<\mathbf{u}_{r}\right>\leq\mathbf{u}^{\prime}_{1}
. Taking
\mathbf{u}^{\prime}=\mathbf{s}^{\prime}[x\backslash\mathbf{u}^{\prime}_{1}]

concludes this case since
\mathbf{t}^{\prime}=\mathbf{s}^{\prime}[x\backslash\mathbf{t}^{\prime}_{1}]\rightarrow_{\textbf{v}_{k}}\mathbf{s}^{\prime}[x\backslash\mathbf{u}^{\prime}_{1}]=\mathbf{u}^{\prime}
and

\mathbf{u}=\mathbf{s}[x\backslash\mathbf{V}^{\prime}_{k}\!\left<\mathbf{u}_{r}\right>]\leq\mathbf{s}^{\prime}[x\backslash\mathbf{u}^{\prime}_{1}]=\mathbf{u}^{\prime}
by transitivity.
∎

Let us recall mutual inductive definitions of
{\tt bvr}_{\textbf{v}_{k}}
,

{\tt bne}_{\textbf{v}_{k}}
and
{\tt bno}_{\textbf{v}_{k}}
for
k\in\mathbb{N}^{\omega}
:

\begin{array}[]{rcl}{\tt bvr}_{\textbf{v}_{k}}&\coloneqq&x\;|\;{\tt bvr}_{\textbf{v}_{k}}[x\backslash{\tt bne}_{\textbf{v}_{k}}]\\[5.69046pt]
{\tt bne}_{\textbf{v}_{k}}&\coloneqq&{\tt bvr}_{\textbf{v}_{k}}\,{\tt bno}_{\textbf{v}_{k}}\;|\;{\tt bne}_{\textbf{v}_{k}}\,{\tt bno}_{\textbf{v}_{k}}\;|\;{\tt bne}_{\textbf{v}_{k}}[x\backslash{\tt bne}_{\textbf{v}_{k}}]\\[5.69046pt]
{\tt bno}_{\textbf{v}_{0}}&\coloneqq&\lambda x.\mathbf{t}\;|\;{\tt bvr}_{\textbf{v}_{0}}\;|\;{\tt bne}_{\textbf{v}_{0}}\;|\;{\tt bno}_{\textbf{v}_{0}}[x\backslash{\tt bne}_{\textbf{v}_{0}}]\\
{\tt bno}_{\textbf{v}_{i+1}}&\coloneqq&\lambda x.{\tt bno}_{\textbf{v}_{i}}\;|\;{\tt bvr}_{\textbf{v}_{i+1}}\;|\;{\tt bne}_{\textbf{v}_{i+1}}\;|\;{\tt bno}_{\textbf{v}_{i+1}}[x\backslash{\tt bne}_{\textbf{v}_{i+1}}]\qquad(i\in\mathbb{N})\\
{\tt bno}_{\textbf{v}_{\omega}}&\coloneqq&\lambda x.{\tt bno}_{\textbf{v}_{\omega}}\;|\;{\tt bvr}_{\textbf{v}_{\omega}}\;|\;{\tt bne}_{\textbf{v}_{\omega}}\;|\;{\tt bno}_{\textbf{v}_{\omega}}[x\backslash{\tt bne}_{\textbf{v}_{\omega}}]\end{array}

See
4.8

Proof.

We strengthen the induction hypothesis:

If
t\in{\tt vr}_{\mathrm{v}_{k}}
then
\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt V}}\!\left(t\right)\in{\tt bvr}_{\textbf{v}_{k}}
.

If
t\in{\tt ne}_{\mathrm{v}_{k}}
then
\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt V}}\!\left(t\right)\in{\tt bne}_{\textbf{v}_{k}}
.

If
t\in{\tt no}_{\mathrm{v}_{k}}
then
\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt V}}\!\left(t\right)\in{\tt bno}_{\textbf{v}_{k}}
.

Since
t\in{\tt no}_{\mathrm{n}_{k}}
then in particular
t\in{\tt no}_{\mathrm{n}_{0}}

thus
t
is
dCBV
-meaningful. By mutual induction on
t\in{\tt vr}_{\mathrm{v}_{k}}
,
t\in{\tt ne}_{\mathrm{v}_{k}}
and
t\in{\tt no}_{\mathrm{v}_{k}}
.
Cases:

t\in{\tt vr}_{\mathrm{v}_{k}}
: We distinguish two cases:

t=x
: Then
\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt V}}\!\left(t\right)=x\in{\tt bvr}_{\textbf{v}_{k}}
.

t=t_{1}[x\backslash t_{2}]
with
t_{1}\in{\tt vr}_{\mathrm{v}_{k}}
and
t_{2}\in{\tt ne}_{\mathrm{v}_{k}}
: By
i.h.
on

t_{1}
and
t_{2}
, one has that
\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt V}}\!\left(t_{1}\right)\in{\tt bvr}_{\textbf{v}_{k}}
and
\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt V}}\!\left(t_{2}\right)\in{\tt bne}_{\textbf{v}_{k}}
thus
\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt V}}\!\left(t\right)=\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt V}}\!\left(t_{1}\right)[x\backslash\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt V}}\!\left(t_{2}\right)]\subseteq{\tt bvr}_{\textbf{v}_{k}}[x\backslash{\tt bne}_{\textbf{v}_{k}}]\subseteq{\tt bvr}_{\textbf{v}_{k}}
.

t\in{\tt ne}_{\mathrm{v}_{k}}
: We distinguish three cases:

t=t_{1}t_{2}
with
t_{1}\in{\tt vr}_{\mathrm{v}_{k}}

and
t_{2}\in{\tt no}_{\mathrm{v}_{k}}
: Then by
i.h.
on
t_{1}
and
t_{2}
,
one has that
\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt V}}\!\left(t_{1}\right)\in{\tt bvr}_{\textbf{v}_{k}}
and

\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt V}}\!\left(t_{2}\right)\in{\tt bno}_{\textbf{v}_{k}}
thus

\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt V}}\!\left(t\right)=\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt V}}\!\left(t_{1}\right)\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt V}}\!\left(t_{2}\right)\in{\tt bvr}_{\textbf{v}_{k}}{\tt bno}_{\textbf{v}_{k}}\subseteq{\tt bne}_{\textbf{v}_{k}}
.

t=t_{1}t_{2}

: Then by
i.h.
on
t_{1}
and
t_{2}
,
one has that
\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt V}}\!\left(t_{1}\right)\in{\tt bne}_{\textbf{v}_{k}}
and

\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt V}}\!\left(t_{2}\right)\in{\tt bno}_{\textbf{v}_{k}}
thus

\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt V}}\!\left(t\right)=\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt V}}\!\left(t_{1}\right)\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt V}}\!\left(t_{2}\right)\in{\tt bne}_{\textbf{v}_{k}}{\tt bno}_{\textbf{v}_{k}}\subseteq{\tt bne}_{\textbf{v}_{k}}

and

and

\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt V}}\!\left(t_{2}\right)\in{\tt bne}_{\textbf{v}_{k}}
thus

.

t\in{\tt no}_{\mathrm{v}_{k}}
: We distinguish four cases:

t=\lambda x.t^{\prime}
: We distinguish three cases:

\bullet

k=0
: Then
t=\lambda x.t^{\prime}
and

\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt V}}\!\left(t\right)=\lambda x.\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt V}}\!\left(t^{\prime}\right)\in{\tt bno}_{\textbf{v}_{0}}

and

.
