Let us recall inductive definitions of stratified equality, where

k\in\mathbb{N}^{\omega}
and
i\in\mathbb{N}
:

\begin{array}[]{c}\hskip-5.69046pt\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\vbox{\hbox{\hskip 8.76215pt\hbox{}}\vbox{\hbox{\hbox{\lower 3.21385pt\vbox{\vskip 3.01385pt\hrule height=0.4pt\vskip 3.01385pt\par}}\enskip\hbox{\lower 2.15277pt\hbox{\!\scriptsize$({\tt var})$}}}\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\hbox{$\hfil\lx@intercol$}}\hbox{$x\overset{\scriptscriptstyle k}{\approx}x$}}\par}\par}}}$}\hskip 11.38092pt\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\vbox{\hbox{\hskip 1.65398pt\hbox{}}\vbox{\hbox{\hbox{\lower 3.21385pt\vbox{\vskip 3.01385pt\hrule height=0.4pt\vskip 3.01385pt\par}}\enskip\hbox{\lower 2.15277pt\hbox{\!\scriptsize$({\tt app})$}}}\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\hbox{\hbox{}\hskip 15.00002pt\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\hbox{$t_{1}\overset{\scriptscriptstyle k}{\approx}u_{1}$}}\hbox{$t_{2}\overset{\scriptscriptstyle k}{\approx}u_{2}$}}}}\hbox{$t_{1}t_{2}\overset{\scriptscriptstyle k}{\approx}u_{1}u_{2}$}}\par}\par}}}$}\hskip 11.38092pt\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\vbox{\hbox{\hskip 22.36917pt\hbox{}}\vbox{\hbox{\hbox{\lower 3.21385pt\vbox{\vskip 3.01385pt\hrule height=0.4pt\vskip 3.01385pt\par}}\enskip\hbox{\lower 2.15277pt\hbox{\!\scriptsize$({\tt es})$}}}\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\hbox{\hbox{}\hskip 15.00002pt\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\hbox{$t_{1}\overset{\scriptscriptstyle k}{\approx}u_{1}$}}\hbox{$t_{2}\overset{\scriptscriptstyle k}{\approx}u_{2}$}}}}\hbox{$t_{1}[x\backslash t_{2}]\overset{\scriptscriptstyle k}{\approx}u_{1}[x\backslash u_{2}]$}}\par}\par}}}$}\\[11.38092pt]
\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\vbox{\hbox{\hskip 23.24655pt\hbox{}}\vbox{\hbox{\hbox{\lower 3.21385pt\vbox{\vskip 3.01385pt\hrule height=0.4pt\vskip 3.01385pt\par}}\enskip\hbox{\lower 2.15277pt\hbox{\!\scriptsize$({\tt abs})$}}}\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\hbox{$\hfil\lx@intercol$}}\hbox{$\lambda x.t\overset{\scriptscriptstyle 0}{\approx}\lambda y.u$}}\par}\par}}}$}\hskip 22.76228pt\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\vbox{\hbox{\hskip 22.48611pt\hbox{}}\vbox{\hbox{\hbox{\lower 3.21385pt\vbox{\vskip 3.01385pt\hrule height=0.4pt\vskip 3.01385pt\par}}\enskip\hbox{\lower 2.15277pt\hbox{\!\scriptsize$({\tt abs})$}}}\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\hbox{$t\overset{\scriptscriptstyle i}{\approx}u$}}\hbox{$\lambda x.t\overset{\scriptscriptstyle i+1}{\approx}\lambda x.u$}}\par}\par}}}$}\hskip 22.76228pt\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\vbox{\hbox{\hskip 15.99304pt\hbox{}}\vbox{\hbox{\hbox{\lower 3.21385pt\vbox{\vskip 3.01385pt\hrule height=0.4pt\vskip 3.01385pt\par}}\enskip\hbox{\lower 2.15277pt\hbox{\!\scriptsize$({\tt abs})$}}}\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\hbox{$t\overset{\scriptscriptstyle\omega}{\approx}u$}}\hbox{$\lambda x.t\overset{\scriptscriptstyle\omega}{\approx}\lambda x.u$}}\par}\par}}}$}\end{array}

See
4.10

Proof.

Let
\mathbf{t},\mathbf{u}\in\mathbf{\Lambda}
such that
\mathbf{t}\leq\mathbf{u}
We strengthen the induction hypothesis:

If
\mathbf{t}\in{\tt bvr}_{\textbf{v}_{k}}
then
\mathbf{u}\in{\tt bvr}_{\textbf{v}_{k}}
.

If
\mathbf{t}\in{\tt bne}_{\textbf{v}_{k}}
then
\mathbf{u}\in{\tt bne}_{\textbf{v}_{k}}
.

If
\mathbf{t}\in{\tt bno}_{\textbf{v}_{k}}
then
\mathbf{u}\in{\tt bno}_{\textbf{v}_{k}}
.

Moreover,
\mathbf{t}\overset{\scriptscriptstyle k}{\approx}\mathbf{u}
.

We prove it by mutual induction on
\mathbf{t}\in{\tt bvr}_{\textbf{v}_{k}}
,

\mathbf{t}\in{\tt bne}_{\textbf{v}_{k}}
and
\mathbf{t}\in{\tt bno}_{\textbf{v}_{k}}
:

\mathbf{t}\in{\tt bvr}_{\textbf{v}_{k}}
:

\mathbf{t}=x
: Then necessarily
\mathbf{u}=x
thus

\mathbf{u}\in{\tt bvr}_{\textbf{v}_{k}}
and
\mathbf{t}\overset{\scriptscriptstyle k}{\approx}\mathbf{u}
using
({\tt var})
.

\mathbf{t}=\mathbf{t}_{1}[x\backslash\mathbf{t}_{2}]
with
\mathbf{t}_{1}\in{\tt bvr}_{\textbf{v}_{k}}
and
\mathbf{t}_{2}\in{\tt bne}_{\textbf{v}_{k}}
. Then necessarilyn
\mathbf{u}=\mathbf{u}_{1}[x\backslash\mathbf{u}_{2}]
with
\mathbf{t}_{1}\leq\mathbf{u}_{1}
and
\mathbf{t}_{2}\leq\mathbf{u}_{2}
for some

\mathbf{u}_{1},\mathbf{u}_{2}\in\mathbf{\Lambda}
. By
i.h.
on

\mathbf{t}_{1}
and
\mathbf{t}_{2}
, one has that
\mathbf{u}_{1}\in{\tt bvr}_{\textbf{v}_{k}}
,
\mathbf{u}_{2}\in{\tt bne}_{\textbf{v}_{k}}
,

\mathbf{t}_{1}\overset{\scriptscriptstyle k}{\approx}\mathbf{u}_{1}
and
\mathbf{t}_{2}\overset{\scriptscriptstyle k}{\approx}\mathbf{u}_{2}
. Thus
\mathbf{u}=\mathbf{u}_{1}[x\backslash\mathbf{u}_{2}]\in{\tt bvr}_{\textbf{v}_{k}}[x\backslash{\tt bne}_{\textbf{v}_{k}}]\subseteq{\tt bvr}_{\textbf{v}_{k}}
and
\mathbf{t}\overset{\scriptscriptstyle k}{\approx}\mathbf{u}

using
({\tt es})
.

\mathbf{t}\in{\tt bne}_{\textbf{v}_{k}}
:

\mathbf{t}=\mathbf{t}_{1}\mathbf{t}_{2}
with
\mathbf{t}_{1}\in{\tt bvr}_{\textbf{v}_{k}}
and
\mathbf{t}_{2}\in{\tt bno}_{\textbf{v}_{k}}
: Necessarily
\mathbf{u}=\mathbf{u}_{1}\mathbf{u}_{2}
with
\mathbf{t}_{1}\leq\mathbf{u}_{1}

and
\mathbf{t}_{2}\leq\mathbf{u}_{2}
. By
i.h.
on
\mathbf{t}_{1}
and

\mathbf{t}_{2}
, one has
\mathbf{u}_{1}\in{\tt bvr}_{\textbf{v}_{k}}
,

\mathbf{u}_{2}\in{\tt bno}_{\textbf{v}_{k}}
,
\mathbf{t}_{1}\overset{\scriptscriptstyle k}{\approx}\mathbf{u}_{1}
and
\mathbf{t}_{2}\overset{\scriptscriptstyle k}{\approx}\mathbf{u}_{2}
. Thus

\mathbf{u}=\mathbf{u}_{1}\mathbf{u}_{2}\in{\tt bvr}_{\textbf{v}_{k}}\,{\tt bno}_{\textbf{v}_{k}}\subseteq{\tt bne}_{\textbf{v}_{k}}
and
\mathbf{t}\overset{\scriptscriptstyle k}{\approx}\mathbf{u}

using
({\tt app})
.

\mathbf{t}=\mathbf{t}_{1}\mathbf{t}_{2}
with
\mathbf{t}_{1}\in{\tt bne}_{\textbf{v}_{k}}
and
\mathbf{t}_{2}\in{\tt bno}_{\textbf{v}_{k}}
: Then necessarily
\mathbf{u}=\mathbf{u}_{1}\mathbf{u}_{2}
with
\mathbf{t}_{1}\leq\mathbf{u}_{1}

and
\mathbf{t}_{2}\leq\mathbf{u}_{2}
. By
i.h.
on
\mathbf{t}_{1}
and

\mathbf{t}_{2}
, one has that
\mathbf{u}_{1}\in{\tt bne}_{\textbf{v}_{k}}
,

\mathbf{u}_{2}\in{\tt bno}_{\textbf{v}_{k}}
,
\mathbf{t}_{1}\overset{\scriptscriptstyle k}{\approx}\mathbf{u}_{1}
and
\mathbf{t}_{2}\overset{\scriptscriptstyle k}{\approx}\mathbf{u}_{2}
. Thus

\mathbf{u}=\mathbf{u}_{1}\mathbf{u}_{2}\in{\tt bne}_{\textbf{v}_{k}}{\tt bno}_{\textbf{v}_{k}}\subseteq{\tt bne}_{\textbf{v}_{k}}
and
\mathbf{t}\overset{\scriptscriptstyle k}{\approx}\mathbf{u}

using
({\tt app})
.

\mathbf{t}=\mathbf{t}_{1}[x\backslash\mathbf{t}_{2}]
with

\mathbf{t}_{1},\mathbf{t}_{2}\in{\tt bne}_{\textbf{v}_{k}}
: Then
necessarily
\mathbf{u}=\mathbf{u}_{1}[x\backslash\mathbf{u}_{2}]
with

\mathbf{t}_{1}\leq\mathbf{u}_{1}
and
\mathbf{t}_{2}\leq\mathbf{u}_{2}
. By
i.h.
on
\mathbf{t}_{1}
and
\mathbf{t}_{2}
, one has that

\mathbf{u}_{1}\in{\tt bne}_{\textbf{v}_{k}}
,
\mathbf{u}_{2}\in{\tt bne}_{\textbf{v}_{k}}
,
\mathbf{t}_{1}\overset{\scriptscriptstyle k}{\approx}\mathbf{u}_{1}
and

\mathbf{t}_{2}\overset{\scriptscriptstyle k}{\approx}\mathbf{u}_{2}
. Thus
\mathbf{u}=\mathbf{u}_{1}[x\backslash\mathbf{u}_{2}]\in{\tt bne}_{\textbf{v}_{k}}[x\backslash{\tt bne}_{\textbf{v}_{k}}]\subseteq{\tt bne}_{\textbf{v}_{k}}
and
\mathbf{t}\overset{\scriptscriptstyle k}{\approx}\mathbf{u}

using
({\tt es})
.

\mathbf{t}\in{\tt bno}_{\textbf{v}_{k}}
:

\mathbf{t}=\lambda x.\mathbf{t}^{\prime}
: Then necessarily

\mathbf{u}=\lambda x.\mathbf{u}^{\prime}
with
\mathbf{t}^{\prime}\leq\mathbf{u}^{\prime}
. We distinguish three cases:

\bullet

k=0
: Then
\mathbf{u}=\lambda x.\mathbf{u}^{\prime}\in{\tt bno}_{\textbf{v}_{0}}
.

\bullet

k=i+1
for some
i\in\mathbb{N}
: Then
\mathbf{t}^{\prime}\in{\tt bno}_{\textbf{v}_{i}}
and
by
i.h.
on
\mathbf{t}^{\prime}
one has
\mathbf{u}^{\prime}\!\in{\tt bno}_{\textbf{v}_{i}}
and
\mathbf{t}^{\prime}\overset{\scriptscriptstyle i}{\approx}\mathbf{u}^{\prime}
. Thus
\mathbf{u}=\lambda x.\mathbf{u}^{\prime}\!\in\lambda x.{\tt bno}_{\textbf{v}_{i}}\subseteq{\tt bno}_{\textbf{v}_{k}}
and
\mathbf{t}\overset{\scriptscriptstyle k}{\approx}\mathbf{u}

using
({\tt abs})
.

\bullet

k=\omega
: Then
\mathbf{t}^{\prime}\in{\tt bno}_{\textbf{v}_{\omega}}
and by
i.h.
on
\mathbf{t}^{\prime}
one has

\mathbf{u}^{\prime}\!\in{\tt bno}_{\textbf{v}_{\omega}}
, so
\mathbf{u}=\lambda x.\mathbf{u}^{\prime}\!\in\lambda x.{\tt bno}_{\textbf{v}_{\omega}}\subseteq{\tt bno}_{\textbf{v}_{k}}
and
\mathbf{t}\overset{\scriptscriptstyle\omega}{\approx}\mathbf{u}
using
({\tt abs})
.

\mathbf{t}\in{\tt bvr}_{\textbf{v}_{k}}
: By
i.h.
on

\mathbf{t}
, one has that
\mathbf{u}\in{\tt bvr}_{\textbf{v}_{k}}\subseteq{\tt bno}_{\textbf{v}_{k}}
and
\mathbf{t}\overset{\scriptscriptstyle k}{\approx}\mathbf{u}
.

\mathbf{t}\in{\tt bne}_{\textbf{v}_{k}}
: By
i.h.
on

\mathbf{t}
, one has that
\mathbf{u}\in{\tt bne}_{\textbf{v}_{k}}\subseteq{\tt bno}_{\textbf{v}_{k}}
and
\mathbf{t}\overset{\scriptscriptstyle k}{\approx}\mathbf{u}
.

\mathbf{t}=\mathbf{t}_{1}[x\backslash\mathbf{t}_{2}]
with
\mathbf{t}_{1}\in{\tt bno}_{\textbf{v}_{k}}
and
\mathbf{t}_{2}\in{\tt bne}_{\textbf{v}_{k}}
: Then necessarily
\mathbf{u}=\mathbf{u}_{1}[x\backslash\mathbf{u}_{2}]
with
\mathbf{t}_{1}\leq\mathbf{u}_{1}
and
\mathbf{t}_{2}\leq\mathbf{u}_{2}
. By
i.h.
on

\mathbf{t}_{1}
and
\mathbf{t}_{2}
, one has that
\mathbf{u}_{1}\in{\tt bno}_{\textbf{v}_{k}}
,
\mathbf{u}_{2}\in{\tt bne}_{\textbf{v}_{k}}
,

\mathbf{t}_{1}\overset{\scriptscriptstyle k}{\approx}\mathbf{u}_{1}
and
\mathbf{t}_{2}\overset{\scriptscriptstyle k}{\approx}\mathbf{u}_{2}
. Thus
\mathbf{u}=\mathbf{u}_{1}[x\backslash\mathbf{u}_{2}]\in{\tt bno}_{\textbf{v}_{k}}[x\backslash{\tt bne}_{\textbf{v}_{k}}]\subseteq{\tt bno}_{\textbf{v}_{k}}
and
\mathbf{t}\overset{\scriptscriptstyle k}{\approx}\mathbf{u}

using
({\tt es})
.
∎

Appendix C
Proof of Section
5

C.1.
Proof of Subsection
5.1

Let us recall the mutual inductive definitions of
{\tt ne}_{\mathrm{n}_{k}}

and
{\tt no}_{\mathrm{n}_{k}}
:

\begin{array}[]{c}\begin{array}[]{rcl}{\tt ne}_{\mathrm{n}_{0}}&\coloneqq&x\;|\;{\tt ne}_{\mathrm{n}_{0}}t\\
{\tt ne}_{\mathrm{n}_{i+1}}&\coloneqq&x\;|\;{\tt ne}_{\mathrm{n}_{i+1}}{\tt no}_{\mathrm{n}_{i}}\qquad(i\in\mathbb{N})\\
{\tt ne}_{\mathrm{n}_{\omega}}&\coloneqq&x\;|\;{\tt ne}_{\mathrm{n}_{\omega}}{\tt no}_{\mathrm{n}_{\omega}}\\[5.69046pt]
{\tt no}_{\mathrm{n}_{k}}&\coloneqq&\lambda x.{\tt no}_{\mathrm{n}_{k}}\;|\;{\tt ne}_{\mathrm{n}_{k}}\qquad(k\in\mathbb{N}^{\omega})\end{array}\end{array}

See
5.3

Proof.

Let
k\in\mathbb{N}^{\omega}
and
t\in\Lambda
. We strengthen
the property as follows:

t\in{\tt ne}_{\mathrm{n}_{k}}
if and only if
t
is a

\mathrm{n}_{k}
-normal form and
\neg{\tt Abs}\!\left(t\right)
.

t\in{\tt no}_{\mathrm{n}_{k}}
if and only if
t
is a

\mathrm{n}_{k}
-normal form.

By double implication:

(\Rightarrow)

By mutual induction on
t\in{\tt ne}_{\mathrm{n}_{k}}

and
t\in{\tt no}_{\mathrm{n}_{k}}
:

t\in{\tt ne}_{\mathrm{n}_{k}}
: We distinguish two cases:

t=x
: Then
t
is a

\mathrm{n}_{k}
-normal form and

\neg{\tt Abs}\left(t\right)
.

t=t_{1}t_{2}
with
t_{1}\in{\tt ne}_{\mathrm{n}_{k}}
: Then, by
i.h.
on
t_{1}
, one has that

t_{1}
is a
\mathrm{n}_{k}
-normal form and

\neg{\tt Abs}\left(t_{1}\right)
thus
t=t_{1}t_{2}
is not a
redex. Moreover,
\neg{\tt Abs}\left(t\right)
. We distinguish
three cases:

\bullet

k=0
: Then
t=t_{1}t_{2}
is

\mathrm{n}_{0}
-normal form.

\bullet

k=i+1
for some
i\in\mathbb{N}
: Then
t_{2}\in{\tt no}_{\mathrm{n}_{i}}
thus
by
i.h.
, one has that
t_{2}
is a

\mathrm{n}_{i}
-normal form. Since
t
is
not a redex then it is a

\mathrm{n}_{k}
-normal form.

\bullet

k=\omega
: Then
t_{2}\in{\tt no}_{\mathrm{n}_{\omega}}
thus by
i.h.
, one has that
t_{2}

is a
\mathrm{n}_{\omega}
-normal form. Since

t
is not a redex then it is a

\mathrm{n}_{\omega}
-normal form.

t\in{\tt no}_{\mathrm{n}_{k}}
: We distinguish two cases:

t=\lambda x.t^{\prime}
with
t^{\prime}\in{\tt no}_{\mathrm{n}_{k}}
:
By
i.h.
on
t^{\prime}
, one has that
t^{\prime}
is a

\mathrm{n}_{k}
-normal form thus
\lambda x.t^{\prime}

is also a
\mathrm{n}_{k}
-normal form.

t\in{\tt ne}_{\mathrm{n}_{k}}
: Then by
i.h.
on
t
, one
deduces that
t
is a
\mathrm{n}_{k}
-normal
form.

(\Leftarrow)

By induction on
t
:

t=x
: Then
\neg{\tt Abs}\left(t\right)
and
t\in{\tt ne}_{\mathrm{n}_{k}}
.

t=\lambda x.t^{\prime}
: Then
{\tt Abs}\left(t\right)
and
t^{\prime}
is
necessarily a
\mathrm{n}_{k}
-normal form. By
i.h.
on

t^{\prime}
, one has that
t^{\prime}\in{\tt no}_{\mathrm{n}_{k}}
thus
t=\lambda x.t^{\prime}\in\lambda x.{\tt no}_{\mathrm{n}_{k}}\subseteq{\tt no}_{\mathrm{n}_{k}}
.

t=t_{1}t_{2}
: Then
\neg{\tt Abs}\left(t\right)
and
necessarily,
\neg{\tt Abs}\!\left(t\right)
and
t_{1}
is a

\mathrm{n}_{k}
-normal form. By
i.h.
on
t_{1}
, one
deduces that
t_{1}\in{\tt ne}_{\mathrm{n}_{k}}
. We distinguish three
cases:

k=0
: Then
t=t_{1}t_{2}\in{\tt ne}_{\mathrm{n}_{0}}t_{2}\subseteq{\tt ne}_{\mathrm{n}_{0}}
.

k=i+1
for some
i\in\mathbb{N}
: Then
t_{2}\in{\tt no}_{\mathrm{n}_{i}}
. By
i.h.
on

t_{2}
, one has that
t_{2}\in{\tt no}_{\mathrm{n}_{i}}
thus
t=t_{1}t_{2}\in{\tt ne}_{\mathrm{n}_{k}}{\tt no}_{\mathrm{n}_{i}}\subseteq{\tt ne}_{\mathrm{n}_{k}}
.

k=\omega
: Then
t_{2}\in{\tt no}_{\mathrm{n}_{\omega}}
. By
i.h.
on
t_{2}
, one has that
t_{2}\in{\tt no}_{\mathrm{n}_{\omega}}
thus
t=t_{1}t_{2}\in{\tt ne}_{\mathrm{n}_{\omega}}{\tt no}_{\mathrm{n}_{\omega}}\subseteq{\tt ne}_{\mathrm{n}_{\omega}}
.

t=t_{1}[x\backslash t_{2}]
: Impossible since it
contradict the fact that
t
is a
\mathrm{n}_{k}
.

∎

See
5.6

Proof.

By induction on
\mathtt{C}
:

\mathtt{C}=\diamond
: Then
\mathtt{C}\!\left<t\right>=t
is
meaningless, which is impossible since it contradicts

Lemma

5.5
.

\mathtt{C}=\lambda x.\mathtt{C}^{\prime}
: Then
\Pi
is
necessarily of the following form:

({\tt abs})

\Phi\triangleright_{\mathcal{N}}\Gamma,x:\mathcal{M}\vdash\mathtt{C}^{\prime}\!\left<t\right>:\tau
\Gamma\vdash\lambda x.\mathtt{C}^{\prime}\!\left<t\right>:\mathcal{M}\Rightarrow\tau\quad

with
\sigma=\mathcal{M}\Rightarrow\tau
. By
i.h.
on
\Phi
, one has

\Phi^{\prime}\triangleright_{\mathcal{N}}\Gamma,x:\mathcal{M}\triangleright_{\mathcal{N}}\mathtt{C}^{\prime}\!\left<u\right>:\tau

and we set
\Pi^{\prime}
as the following derivation:

({\tt abs})

\Phi^{\prime}\triangleright_{\mathcal{N}}\Gamma,x:\mathcal{M}\vdash\mathtt{C}^{\prime}\!\left<u\right>:\tau
\Gamma\vdash\lambda x.\mathtt{C}^{\prime}\!\left<u\right>:\mathcal{M}\Rightarrow\tau\quad

\mathtt{C}=\mathtt{C}^{\prime}s
: Then
\Pi
is
necessarily of the following form:

({\tt app})

\left(\vrule height=8.26085pt,depth=2.5pt,width=0.0pt\right.
\left.\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\hbox{$\Pi_{1}\triangleright_{\mathcal{N}}\Gamma_{1}\vdash\mathtt{C}^{\prime}\!\left<t\right>:\left[\tau_{i}\right]_{i\in I}\Rightarrow\sigma$}}\hbox{$\Pi_{2}^{i}\triangleright_{\mathcal{N}}\Gamma_{2}^{i}\vdash s:\tau_{i}$}}}$}\right)_{i\in I}
\Gamma_{1}+_{i\in I}\Gamma_{2}^{i}\mathrel{}
\mathrel{}\vdash\mathtt{C}^{\prime}\!\left<t\right>s:\sigma\hskip 71.13188pt

with
I
finite and
\Gamma=\Gamma_{1}+_{i\in I}\Gamma_{2}^{i}
. By

i.h.
on
\Pi_{1}
, one has
\Pi^{\prime}_{1}\triangleright_{\mathcal{N}}\Gamma_{1}\vdash\mathtt{C}^{\prime}\!\left<u\right>:\left[\tau_{i}\right]_{i\in I}\Rightarrow\sigma
and we
set
\Pi^{\prime}
as the following derivation:

({\tt app})

\left(\vrule height=8.26085pt,depth=2.5pt,width=0.0pt\right.
\left.\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\hbox{$\Pi^{\prime}_{1}\triangleright_{\mathcal{N}}\Gamma_{1}\vdash\mathtt{C}^{\prime}\!\left<u\right>:\left[\tau_{i}\right]_{i\in I}\Rightarrow\sigma$}}\hbox{$\Pi_{2}^{i}\triangleright_{\mathcal{N}}\Gamma_{2}^{i}\vdash s:\tau_{i}$}}}$}\right)_{i\in I}
\Gamma_{1}+_{i\in I}\Gamma_{2}^{i}\mathrel{}
\mathrel{}\vdash\mathtt{C}^{\prime}\!\left<t\right>s:\sigma\hskip 71.13188pt

\mathtt{C}=s\,\mathtt{C}^{\prime}
: Then
\Pi
is
necessarily of the following form:

({\tt app})

\left(\vrule height=8.26085pt,depth=2.5pt,width=0.0pt\right.
\left.\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\hbox{$\Pi_{1}\triangleright_{\mathcal{N}}\Gamma_{1}\vdash s:\left[\tau_{i}\right]_{i\in I}\Rightarrow\sigma$}}\hbox{$\Pi_{2}^{i}\triangleright_{\mathcal{N}}\Gamma_{2}^{i}\vdash\mathtt{C}^{\prime}\!\left<t\right>:\tau_{i}$}}}$}\right)_{i\in I}
\Gamma_{1}+_{i\in I}\Gamma_{2}^{i}\mathrel{}
\mathrel{}\vdash s\,\mathtt{C}^{\prime}\!\left<t\right>:\sigma\hskip 71.13188pt

with
I
finite and
\Gamma=\Gamma_{1}+_{i\in I}\Gamma_{2}^{i}
.
For each
i\in I
, by
i.h.
on
\Pi_{2}^{i}
, one has
{\Pi_{2}^{i}}^{\prime}\triangleright_{\mathcal{N}}\Gamma_{2}^{i}\vdash\mathtt{C}^{\prime}\!\left<u\right>:\tau_{i}
and we set

\Pi^{\prime}
as the following derivation:

({\tt app})

\left(\vrule height=8.26085pt,depth=2.5pt,width=0.0pt\right.
\left.\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\hbox{$\Pi_{1}\triangleright_{\mathcal{N}}\Gamma_{1}\vdash s:\left[\tau_{i}\right]_{i\in I}\Rightarrow\sigma$}}\hbox{${\Pi_{2}^{i}}^{\prime}\triangleright_{\mathcal{N}}\Gamma_{2}^{i}\vdash\mathtt{C}^{\prime}\!\left<u\right>:\tau_{i}$}}}$}\right)_{i\in I}
\Gamma_{1}+_{i\in I}\Gamma_{2}^{i}\mathrel{}
\mathrel{}\vdash s\,\mathtt{C}^{\prime}\!\left<u\right>:\sigma\hskip 71.13188pt

\mathtt{C}=\mathtt{C}^{\prime}[x\backslash s]
: Then
\Pi
is
necessarily of the following form:

({\tt es})

\left(\vrule height=8.26085pt,depth=2.5pt,width=0.0pt\right.
\left.\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\hbox{$\Pi_{1}\triangleright_{\mathcal{N}}\Gamma_{1},x:\left[\tau_{i}\right]_{i\in I}\vdash\mathtt{C}^{\prime}\!\left<t\right>:\sigma$}}\hbox{$\Pi_{2}^{i}\triangleright_{\mathcal{N}}\Gamma_{2}^{i}\vdash s:\tau_{i}$}}}$}\right)_{i\in I}
\Gamma_{1}+_{i\in I}\Gamma_{2}^{i}\mathrel{}
\mathrel{}\vdash\mathtt{C}^{\prime}\!\left<t\right>[x\backslash s]:\sigma\hskip 54.06006pt

with
I
finite and
\Gamma=\Gamma_{1}+_{i\in I}\Gamma_{2}^{i}
. By

i.h.
on
\Pi_{1}
, one has
\Pi^{\prime}_{1}\triangleright_{\mathcal{N}}\Gamma_{1},x:\left[\tau_{i}\right]_{i\in I}\vdash\mathtt{C}^{\prime}\!\left<u\right>:\sigma
and we set

\Pi^{\prime}
as the following derivation:

({\tt es})

\left(\vrule height=8.26085pt,depth=2.5pt,width=0.0pt\right.
\left.\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\hbox{$\Pi^{\prime}_{1}\triangleright_{\mathcal{N}}\Gamma_{1},x:\left[\tau_{i}\right]_{i\in I}\vdash\mathtt{C}^{\prime}\!\left<u\right>:\sigma$}}\hbox{$\Pi_{2}^{i}\triangleright_{\mathcal{N}}\Gamma_{2}^{i}\vdash s:\tau_{i}$}}}$}\right)_{i\in I}
\Gamma_{1}+_{i\in I}\Gamma_{2}^{i}\mathrel{}
\mathrel{}\vdash\mathtt{C}^{\prime}\!\left<u\right>[x\backslash s]:\sigma\hskip 54.06006pt

\mathtt{C}=s[x\backslash\mathtt{C}^{\prime}]
: Then
\Pi
is
necessarily of the following form:

({\tt es})

\left(\vrule height=8.26085pt,depth=2.5pt,width=0.0pt\right.
\left.\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\hbox{$\Pi_{1}\triangleright_{\mathcal{N}}\Gamma_{1},x:\left[\tau_{i}\right]_{i\in I}\vdash s:\sigma$}}\hbox{$\Pi_{2}^{i}\triangleright_{\mathcal{N}}\Gamma_{2}^{i}\vdash\mathtt{C}^{\prime}\!\left<t\right>:\tau_{i}$}}}$}\right)_{i\in I}
\Gamma_{1}+_{i\in I}\Gamma_{2}^{i}\mathrel{}
\mathrel{}\vdash s[x\backslash\mathtt{C}^{\prime}\!\left<t\right>]:\sigma\hskip 54.06006pt

with
I
finite and
\Gamma=\Gamma_{1}+_{i\in I}\Gamma_{2}^{i}
.
For each
i\in I
, by
i.h.
on
\Pi_{2}^{i}
, one has
\Pi_{2}^{i}\triangleright_{\mathcal{N}}\Gamma_{2}^{i}\vdash\mathtt{C}^{\prime}\!\left<u\right>:\tau_{i}
and we set

\Pi^{\prime}
as the following derivation:

({\tt es})

\left(\vrule height=8.26085pt,depth=2.5pt,width=0.0pt\right.
\left.\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\hbox{$\Pi_{1}\triangleright_{\mathcal{N}}\Gamma_{1},x:\left[\tau_{i}\right]_{i\in I}\vdash s:\sigma$}}\hbox{${\Pi_{2}^{i}}^{\prime}\triangleright_{\mathcal{N}}\Gamma_{2}^{i}\vdash\mathtt{C}\!\left<u\right>:\tau_{i}$}}}$}\right)_{i\in I}
\Gamma_{1}+_{i\in I}\Gamma_{2}^{i}\mathrel{}
\mathrel{}\vdash s[x\backslash\mathtt{C}\!\left<u\right>]:\sigma\hskip 54.06006pt

∎

See
5.7

Proof.

Let
t\in\Lambda
such that
t
is
dCBN
-meaningless and

\mathtt{C}\!\left<t\right>
is
dCBN
-meaningful. Using

Lemma

5.5
, one has
\Pi\triangleright_{\mathcal{N}}\Gamma\vdash\mathtt{C}\!\left<t\right>:\sigma
for some context

\Gamma
and some type
\sigma
. By

Lemma

5.6
, there is

\Pi^{\prime}\triangleright_{\mathcal{N}}\Gamma\vdash\mathtt{C}\!\left<u\right>:\sigma
, thus
using
Lemma

5.5
again, one
concludes that
\mathtt{C}\!\left<u\right>
is
dCBN
-meaningful.
∎

C.2.
Technical Lemmas

Lemma C.1.

Let
\mathbf{t}_{1},\mathbf{t}_{2},\mathbf{u}_{1},\mathbf{u}_{2}\in\mathbf{\Lambda}

such that
\mathbf{t}_{1}\leq\mathbf{t}_{2}
and
\mathbf{u}_{1}\leq\mathbf{u}_{2}
, then
\mathbf{t}_{1}\{x\backslash\mathbf{u}_{1}\}\leq\mathbf{t}_{2}\{x\backslash\mathbf{u}_{2}\}
.

Proof.

By induction on
\mathbf{t}_{1}
:

\mathbf{t}_{1}=\bot
: Then

\mathbf{t}_{1}\{x\backslash\mathbf{u}_{1}\}=\bot\leq\mathbf{t}_{2}\{x\backslash\mathbf{u}_{2}\}
.

\mathbf{t}_{1}=y
: Then
\mathbf{t}_{2}=y
and we distinguish
two cases:

x=y
: Thus
\mathbf{t}_{1}\{x\backslash\mathbf{u}_{1}\}=\mathbf{u}_{1}\leq\mathbf{u}_{2}=\mathbf{t}_{2}\{x\backslash\mathbf{u}_{2}\}
.

x\neq y
: Thus
\mathbf{t}_{1}\{x\backslash\mathbf{u}_{1}\}=y=\mathbf{t}_{2}\{x\backslash\mathbf{u}_{2}\}
, thus
\mathbf{t}_{1}\{x\backslash\mathbf{u}_{1}\}\leq\mathbf{t}_{2}\{x\backslash\mathbf{u}_{2}\}
by reflexivity of

\leq
.

\mathbf{t}_{1}=\lambda y.\mathbf{t}^{\prime}_{1}
: Then

\mathbf{t}_{1}\{x\backslash\mathbf{u}_{1}\}=\lambda y.\mathbf{t}^{\prime}_{1}\{x\backslash\mathbf{u}_{1}\}
(by supposing
y\notin{\tt fv}(\mathbf{u}_{1})\cup{\tt fv}(\mathbf{u}_{2})
without loss of
generality) and necessarily
\mathbf{t}_{2}=\lambda y.\mathbf{t}^{\prime}_{2}
with

\mathbf{t}^{\prime}_{1}\leq\mathbf{t}^{\prime}_{2}
thus
\mathbf{t}_{2}\{x\backslash\mathbf{u}_{2}\}=\lambda y.\mathbf{t}^{\prime}_{2}\{y\backslash\mathbf{u}_{2}\}
. By
i.h.
on
\mathbf{t}^{\prime}_{1}
,
one has that
\mathbf{t}^{\prime}_{1}\{x\backslash\mathbf{u}_{1}\}\leq\mathbf{t}^{\prime}_{2}\{x\backslash\mathbf{u}_{2}\}
hence
\mathbf{t}_{1}\{x\backslash\mathbf{u}_{1}\}=\lambda y.\mathbf{t}^{\prime}_{1}\{x\backslash\mathbf{u}_{1}\}\leq\lambda y.\mathbf{t}^{\prime}_{2}\{x\backslash\mathbf{u}_{2}\}=\mathbf{t}_{2}\{x\backslash\mathbf{u}_{2}\}

by contextual closure.

\mathbf{t}_{1}=\mathbf{t}_{1}^{1}\mathbf{t}_{1}^{2}
: Then

\mathbf{t}_{1}\{x\backslash\mathbf{u}_{1}\}=(\mathbf{t}_{1}^{1}\{x\backslash\mathbf{u}_{1}\})(\mathbf{t}_{1}^{2}\{x\backslash\mathbf{u}_{1}\})

and necessarily
\mathbf{t}_{2}=\mathbf{t}_{2}^{1}\mathbf{t}_{2}^{2}
with

\mathbf{t}_{1}^{1}\leq\mathbf{t}_{2}^{1}
and
\mathbf{t}_{1}^{2}\leq\mathbf{t}_{2}^{2}
, thus
\mathbf{t}_{2}\{y\backslash\mathbf{u}_{2}\}=(\mathbf{t}_{2}^{1}\{x\backslash\mathbf{u}_{2}\})(\mathbf{t}_{2}^{2}\{x\backslash\mathbf{u}_{2}\})
.
By
i.h.
on
\mathbf{t}_{1}^{1}
and
\mathbf{t}_{1}^{2}
, one obtains that

\mathbf{t}_{1}^{1}\{x\backslash\mathbf{u}_{1}\}\leq\mathbf{t}_{2}^{1}\{x\backslash\mathbf{u}_{2}\}
and
\mathbf{t}_{1}^{2}\{x\backslash\mathbf{u}_{1}\}\leq\mathbf{t}_{2}^{2}\{x\backslash\mathbf{u}_{2}\}
, hence

\mathbf{t}_{1}\{x\backslash\mathbf{u}_{1}\}=(\mathbf{t}_{1}^{1}\{x\backslash\mathbf{u}_{1}\})(\mathbf{t}_{1}^{2}\{x\backslash\mathbf{u}_{1}\})\leq(\mathbf{t}_{2}^{1}\{x\backslash\mathbf{u}_{2}\})(\mathbf{t}_{2}^{2}\{x\backslash\mathbf{u}_{2}\})=\mathbf{t}_{2}\{x\backslash\mathbf{u}_{2}\}
by contextual closure.

\mathbf{t}_{1}=\mathbf{t}_{1}^{1}[y\backslash\mathbf{t}_{1}^{2}]
: Then

\mathbf{t}_{1}\{x\backslash\mathbf{u}_{1}\}=\mathbf{t}_{1}^{1}\{x\backslash\mathbf{u}_{1}\}[y\backslash\mathbf{t}_{1}^{2}\{x\backslash\mathbf{u}_{1}\}]

and necessarily
\mathbf{t}_{2}=\mathbf{t}_{2}^{1}[y\backslash\mathbf{t}_{2}^{2}]
with

\mathbf{t}_{1}^{1}\leq\mathbf{t}_{2}^{1}
and
\mathbf{t}_{1}^{2}\leq\mathbf{t}_{2}^{2}
, thus
\mathbf{t}_{2}\{y\backslash\mathbf{u}_{2}\}=\mathbf{t}_{2}^{1}\{x\backslash\mathbf{u}_{2}\}[y\backslash\mathbf{t}_{2}^{2}\{x\backslash\mathbf{u}_{2}\}]
.
By
i.h.
on
\mathbf{t}_{1}^{1}
and
\mathbf{t}_{1}^{2}
, one has

\mathbf{t}_{1}^{1}\{x\backslash\mathbf{u}_{1}\}\leq\mathbf{t}_{2}^{1}\{x\backslash\mathbf{u}_{2}\}
and
\mathbf{t}_{1}^{2}\{x\backslash\mathbf{u}_{1}\}\leq\mathbf{t}_{2}^{2}\{x\backslash\mathbf{u}_{2}\}
, so by contextual
closure
\mathbf{t}_{1}\{x\backslash\mathbf{u}_{1}\}=\mathbf{t}_{1}^{1}\{x\backslash\mathbf{u}_{1}\}[y\backslash\mathbf{t}_{1}^{2}\{x\backslash\mathbf{u}_{1}\}]\leq\mathbf{t}_{2}^{1}\{x\backslash\mathbf{u}_{2}\}[y\backslash\mathbf{t}_{2}^{2}\{x\backslash\mathbf{u}_{2}\}]=\mathbf{t}_{2}\{x\backslash\mathbf{u}_{2}\}
.
∎

Definition C.2.

The partial order
\leq
is extended to
dCBN
contexts
by setting
\diamond\leq\diamond
.

For example,

\diamond[x\backslash\lambda y.\bot](z\bot)\leq\diamond[x\backslash\lambda y.(zz)](z\bot)
.

Lemma C.3.

Let
\mathbf{L}_{1}\leq\mathbf{L}_{2}
and
\mathbf{t}_{1}\leq\mathbf{t}_{2}
for some list contexts
\mathbf{L}_{1},\mathbf{L}_{2}
and some
\mathbf{t}_{1},\mathbf{t}_{2}\in\mathbf{\Lambda}
, then
\mathbf{L}_{1}\!\left<\mathbf{t}_{1}\right>\leq\mathbf{L}_{2}\!\left<\mathbf{t}_{2}\right>
.

Proof.

By induction on
\mathbf{L}_{1}
:

\mathbf{L}_{1}=\diamond
: Then necessarily

\mathbf{L}_{2}=\diamond
thus
\mathbf{L}_{1}\!\left<\mathbf{t}_{1}\right>=\mathbf{t}_{1}\leq\mathbf{t}_{2}=\mathbf{L}_{2}\!\left<\mathbf{t}_{2}\right>
.

\mathbf{L}_{1}=\mathbf{L}^{\prime}_{1}[x\backslash\mathbf{u}_{1}]
:
Then necessarily
\mathbf{L}_{2}=\mathbf{L}^{\prime}_{2}[x\backslash\mathbf{u}_{2}]
with
\mathbf{L}^{\prime}_{1}\leq\mathbf{L}^{\prime}_{2}
and
\mathbf{u}_{1}\leq\mathbf{u}_{2}
.
By
i.h.
on
\mathbf{L}^{\prime}_{1}
, one has that

\mathbf{L}^{\prime}_{1}\!\left<\mathbf{t}_{1}\right>\leq\mathbf{L}^{\prime}_{2}\!\left<\mathbf{t}_{2}\right>
thus
\mathbf{L}_{1}\!\left<\mathbf{t}_{1}\right>=\mathbf{L}^{\prime}_{1}\!\left<\mathbf{t}_{1}\right>[x\backslash\mathbf{u}_{1}]\leq\mathbf{L}^{\prime}_{2}\!\left<\mathbf{t}_{2}\right>[x\backslash\mathbf{u}_{2}]=\mathbf{L}_{2}\!\left<\mathbf{t}_{2}\right>
by contextual closure.
∎

Lemma C.4.

Let
t,u\in\Lambda
such that
t\rightarrow_{\mathrm{n}_{k}}u
.
Then,
t
is
dCBN
-meaningful if and only if
u
is

dCBN
-meaningful.

Proof.

According to qualitative subject reduction and expansion

(
deCarvalho18
)
, given
t\rightarrow_{\mathrm{n}_{\omega}}u
, there is a
derivation
\Pi\triangleright_{\mathcal{N}}\Gamma\vdash t:\sigma
if and only if
there is a derivation
\Pi^{\prime}\triangleright_{\mathcal{N}}\Gamma\vdash u:\sigma
. As

\rightarrow_{\mathrm{n}_{k}}\subseteq\rightarrow_{\mathrm{n}_{\omega}}
for all

k\in\mathbb{N}
, the same equivalence also holds if we
replace the hypothesis
t\rightarrow_{\mathrm{n}_{\omega}}u
with
t\rightarrow_{\mathrm{n}_{k}}u
, for all
k\in\mathbb{N}
. We
conclude thanks to the type-theoretical characterization of

dCBN
-meaningfulness
(
Lemma

5.5
).
∎

Lemma C.5.

Let
t,u\in\Lambda
. Then

(1)

If
tu
is
dCBN
-meaningful then
t
is

dCBN
-meaningful.

(2)

If
t[x\backslash u]
is
dCBN
-meaningful then
t
is

dCBN
-meaningful.

(3)

If
t\{x\backslash u\}
is
dCBN
-meaningful then
t
is

dCBN
-meaningful.

Proof.

(1)

Let
tu
be
dCBN
-meaningful and suppose by absurd
that
t
is
dCBN
-meaningless. Using

Lemma

5.5
, we know that

tu
surface normalizes. Since
t
is

dCBN
-meaningless, there exists a
(t_{i})_{i\in\mathbb{N}}

with
t_{0}=t
and
t_{i}\rightarrow_{\mathrm{v}_{0}}t_{i+1}
yielding the
infinite path
tu\rightarrow_{\mathrm{v}_{0}}t_{1}u\rightarrow_{\mathrm{v}_{0}}t_{2}u\rightarrow_{\mathrm{v}_{0}}\dots
obtained by always reducing
t
.
Since surface reduction is diamond, this contradicts the fact that

tu
is surface normalizing.

(2)

Same principle as the previous item.

(3)

Let
t\{x\backslash v\}
be
dCBN
-meaningful. By induction on
t
,
one can show that if
t\rightarrow_{\mathrm{v}_{0}}u
then
t\{x\backslash v\}\rightarrow_{\mathrm{v}_{0}}u\{x\backslash v\}
. Using this, one applied the same
principles as the two previous points.

∎

Definition C.6.

Meaningful approximation is extended to list contexts
\mathtt{L}

inductively as follows:

\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(\diamond\right)\coloneqq\diamond\hskip 56.9055pt\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt V}}\!\left(\mathtt{L}[x\backslash s]\right)\coloneqq\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(\mathtt{L}\right)[x\backslash\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(s\right)]

Corollary C.7.

Let
\mathtt{L}\!\left<t\right>
be
dCBN
-meaningful, then

\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(\mathtt{L}\!\left<t\right>\right)=\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(\mathtt{L}\right)\!\left<\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(t\right)\right>
.

Proof.

By induction on
\mathtt{L}
. Cases:

\mathtt{L}=\diamond
: Then
\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(\mathtt{L}\!\left<t\right>\right)=\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(t\right)=\diamond\!\left<\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(t\right)\right>=\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(\diamond\right)\!\left<\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(t\right)\right>
.

\mathtt{L}=\mathtt{L}^{\prime}[x\backslash u]
: Then
\mathtt{L}\!\left<t\right>=\mathtt{L}^{\prime}\!\left<t\right>[x\backslash u]
is
dCBN
-meaningful, hence by

Lemma

C.5
.(
2
)

\mathtt{L}^{\prime}\!\left<t\right>
is
dCBN
-meaningful. By
i.h.
on
\mathtt{L}^{\prime}
,
one obtains that
\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(\mathtt{L}^{\prime}\!\left<t\right>\right)=\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(\mathtt{L}^{\prime}\right)\!\left<\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(t\right)\right>
, thus:

\begin{array}[]{r cl cl}\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(\mathtt{L}\!\left<t\right>\right)&=&\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(\mathtt{L}^{\prime}\!\left<t\right>[x\backslash u]\right)\\
&=&\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(\mathtt{L}^{\prime}\!\left<t\right>\right)[x\backslash\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(u\right)]\\
&\overset{\textit{i.h.}}{=}&\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(\mathtt{L}^{\prime}\right)\!\left<\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(t\right)\right>[x\backslash\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(u\right)]\\
&=&\big(\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(\mathtt{L}^{\prime}\right)[x\backslash\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(u\right)]\big)\!\left<\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(t\right)\right>\\
&=&\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(\mathtt{L}^{\prime}[x\backslash u]\right)\!\left<\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(t\right)\right>&=&\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(\mathtt{L}\right)\!\left<\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(t\right)\right>\end{array}

∎

Lemma C.8.

Let
t,u\in\Lambda
, then
\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(t\{x\backslash u\}\right)\leq\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(t\right)\{x\backslash\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(u\right)\}
.

Proof.

We distinguish two cases:

t\{x\backslash u\}
is
dCBN
-meaningless. Then

\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(t\{x\backslash u\}\right)=\bot\leq\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(t\right)\{x\backslash\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(u\right)\}
.

t\{x\backslash u\}
is
dCBN
-meaningful.
By
Lemma

C.5
.(
3
),

t
is
dCBN
-meaningful. We reason by induction on
t
. Cases:

t=y
: Then
\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(t\right)=y
and we
distinguish two subcases:

\bullet

y=x
: Then
t\{x\backslash u\}=u
and so

\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(t\{x\backslash u\}\right)=\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(u\right)=x\{x\backslash\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(u\right)\}=\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(t\right)\{x\backslash\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(u\right)\}
.

\bullet

y\neq x
: Then
t\{x\backslash u\}=y
and so

\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(t\{x\backslash u\}\right)=\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(y\right)=y=y\{x\backslash\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(u\right)\}=\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(t\right)\{x\backslash\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(u\right)\}
.

t=\lambda y.t^{\prime}
: Then
\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(t\right)=\lambda y.\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(t^{\prime}\right)
and
t\{x\backslash u\}=\lambda y.(t^{\prime}\{x\backslash u\})
(we suppose without loss of generality
that
y\notin{\tt fv}(u)\cup\{x\}
). By
i.h.
on
t^{\prime}
, one
has
\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(t^{\prime}\{x\backslash u\}\right)\leq\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(t^{\prime}\right)\{x\backslash\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(u\right)\}
. Thus:

\begin{array}[]{r cl cl}\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(t\{x\backslash u\}\right)&=&\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(\lambda y.(t^{\prime}\{x\backslash u\})\right)\\
&=&\lambda y.\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(t^{\prime}\{x\backslash u\}\right)\\
&\overset{\textit{i.h.}}{\leq}&\lambda y.\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(t^{\prime}\right)\{x\backslash\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(u\right)\}\\
&=&(\lambda y.\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(t^{\prime}\right))\{x\backslash\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(u\right)\}\\
&=&\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(\lambda y.t^{\prime}\right)\{x\backslash\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(u\right)\}&=&\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(t\right)\{x\backslash\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(u\right)\}\end{array}

t=t_{1}t_{2}
: Then
\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(t\right)=\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(t_{1}\right)\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(t_{2}\right)
and
t\{x\backslash u\}=(t_{1}\{x\backslash u\})[y\backslash t_{2}\{x\backslash u\}]
(we suppose without
loss of generality that
y\notin{\tt fv}(u)\cup\{x\}
). By

i.h.
on
t_{1}
and
t_{2}
, one has
\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(t_{1}\{x\backslash u\}\right)\leq\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(t_{1}\right)\{x\backslash\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(u\right)\}
and

\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(t_{2}\{x\backslash u\}\right)\leq\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(t_{2}\right)\{x\backslash\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(u\right)\}
. Thus:

\begin{array}[]{r cl cl}\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(t\{x\backslash u\}\right)&=&\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(t_{1}\{x\backslash u\}t_{2}\{x\backslash u\}\right)\\
&=&\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(t_{1}\{x\backslash u\}\right)\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(t_{2}\{x\backslash u\}\right)\\
&\overset{\textit{i.h.}}{\leq}&(\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(t_{1}\right)\{x\backslash\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(u\right)\})(\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(t_{2}\right)\{x\backslash\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(u\right)\})\\
&=&(\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(t_{1}\right)\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(t_{2}\right))\{x\backslash\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(u\right)\}\\
&=&\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(t_{1}t_{2}\right)\{x\backslash\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(u\right)\}&=&\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(t\right)\{x\backslash\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(u\right)\}\end{array}

t=t_{1}[y\backslash t_{2}]
: Thus
\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(t\right)=\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(t_{1}\right)[y\backslash\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(t_{2}\right)]
and
t\{x\backslash u\}=(t_{1}\{x\backslash u\})[y\backslash t_{2}\{x\backslash u\}]
(we suppose
without loss of generality that
y\notin{\tt fv}(u)\cup\{x\}
). By
i.h.
on
t_{1}
and
t_{2}
, one has

\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(t_{1}\{x\backslash u\}\right)\leq\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(t_{1}\right)\{x\backslash\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(u\right)\}
and

\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(t_{2}\{x\backslash u\}\right)\leq\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(t_{2}\right)\{x\backslash\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(u\right)\}
. Thus:

\begin{array}[]{r cl cl}\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(t\{x\backslash u\}\right)&=&\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(t_{1}\{x\backslash u\}[y\backslash t_{2}\{x\backslash u\}]\right)\\
&=&\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(t_{1}\{x\backslash u\}\right)[y\backslash\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(t_{2}\{x\backslash u\}\right)]\\
&\overset{\textit{i.h.}}{\leq}&(\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(t_{1}\right)\{x\backslash\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(u\right)\})[y\backslash\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(t_{2}\right)\{x\backslash\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(u\right)\}]\\
&=&(\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(t_{1}\right)[y\backslash\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(t_{2}\right)])\{x\backslash\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(u\right)\}\\
&=&\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(t_{1}[y\backslash t_{2}]\right)\{x\backslash\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(u\right)\}&=&\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(t\right)\{x\backslash\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(u\right)\}\end{array}

∎

C.3.
Proofs of Section
5.2

See
5.9

Proof.

We distinguish two cases:

t
is meaningless. Then
\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(t\right)=\bot\leq t
.

t
is meaningful. We then reason by cases on
t
:

t=x
: Then
\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(t\right)=x\leq x=t
.

t=\lambda x.t^{\prime}
: By
i.h.
on
t^{\prime}
, one has that

\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(t^{\prime}\right)\leq t^{\prime}
thus
\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(t\right)=\lambda x.\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(t^{\prime}\right)\leq\lambda x.t^{\prime}=t
.

t=t_{1}t_{2}
: By
i.h.
on
t_{1}
and
t_{2}
,
one has that
\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(t_{1}\right)\leq t_{1}
and

\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(t_{2}\right)\leq t_{2}
so that
\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(t\right)=\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(t_{1}\right)\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(t_{2}\right)\leq t_{1}t_{2}=t
.

t=t_{1}[x\backslash t_{2}]
: By
i.h.
on
t_{1}
and

t_{2}
,
\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(t_{1}\right)\leq t_{1}
and

\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(t_{2}\right)\leq t_{2}
. So,
\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(t\right)=\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(t_{1}\right)[x\backslash\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(t_{2}\right)]\leq t_{1}[x\backslash t_{2}]=t
.
∎

See
5.10

Proof.

Let
\mathtt{C}
be a full context and
t,u\in\Lambda
be such
that
t
is meaningless. We distinguish two cases:

\mathtt{C}\!\left<t\right>
is meaningless. Then

\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(\mathtt{C}\!\left<t\right>\right)=\bot\leq\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(\mathtt{C}\!\left<u\right>\right)
.

\mathtt{C}\!\left<t\right>
is meaningful: Then by surface genericity
(
Theorem

5.7
), one has that

\mathtt{C}\!\left<u\right>
is also meaningful. We reason by induction on

\mathtt{C}
:

\mathtt{C}=\diamond
: This case is impossible because
it would mean
\mathtt{C}\!\left<t\right>=t
is meaningless (by
hypothesis) and meaningful at the same time.

\mathtt{C}=\lambda x.\mathtt{C}^{\prime}
: By
i.h.
on

\mathtt{C}^{\prime}
, one has
\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(\mathtt{C}^{\prime}\!\left<t\right>\right)\leq\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(\mathtt{C}^{\prime}\!\left<u\right>\right)
, thus
\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(\mathtt{C}\!\left<t\right>\right)=\lambda x.\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(\mathtt{C}^{\prime}\!\left<t\right>\right)\overset{\textit{i.h.}}{\leq}\lambda x.\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(\mathtt{C}^{\prime}\!\left<u\right>\right)=\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(\mathtt{C}\!\left<u^{\prime}\right>\right)
by contextual closure.

\mathtt{C}=\mathtt{C}^{\prime}s
: By
i.h.
on

\mathtt{C}^{\prime}
, one has
\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(\mathtt{C}^{\prime}\!\left<t\right>\right)\leq\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(\mathtt{C}^{\prime}\!\left<u\right>\right)
, thus
\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(\mathtt{C}\!\left<t\right>\right)=\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(\mathtt{C}^{\prime}\!\left<t\right>\right)\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(s\right)\overset{\textit{i.h.}}{\leq}\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(\mathtt{C}^{\prime}\!\left<u\right>\right)\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(s\right)\allowbreak=\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(\mathtt{C}\!\left<u^{\prime}\right>\right)
by contextual closure.

\mathtt{C}=s\,\mathtt{C}^{\prime}
: By
i.h.
on

\mathtt{C}^{\prime}
, one has
\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(\mathtt{C}^{\prime}\!\left<t\right>\right)\leq\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(\mathtt{C}^{\prime}\!\left<u\right>\right)
, thus
\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(\mathtt{C}\!\left<t\right>\right)=\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(s\right)\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(\mathtt{C}^{\prime}\!\left<t\right>\right)\overset{\textit{i.h.}}{\leq}\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(s\right)\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(\mathtt{C}^{\prime}\!\left<u\right>\right)\allowbreak=\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(\mathtt{C}\!\left<u^{\prime}\right>\right)
by contextual closure.

\mathtt{C}=\mathtt{C}^{\prime}[x\backslash s]
: By
i.h.
on

\mathtt{C}^{\prime}
, one has
\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(\mathtt{C}^{\prime}\!\left<t\right>\right)\leq\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(\mathtt{C}^{\prime}\!\left<u\right>\right)
, and hence

\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(\mathtt{C}\!\left<t\right>\right)=\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(\mathtt{C}^{\prime}\!\left<t\right>\right)[x\backslash]{\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(s\right)}\overset{\textit{i.h.}}{\leq}\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(\mathtt{C}^{\prime}\!\left<u\right>\right)[x\backslash]{\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(s\right)}=\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(\mathtt{C}\!\left<u^{\prime}\right>\right)
by contextual closure.

\mathtt{C}=s[x\backslash\mathtt{C}^{\prime}]
: By
i.h.
on

\mathtt{C}^{\prime}
, one has
\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(\mathtt{C}^{\prime}\!\left<t\right>\right)\leq\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(\mathtt{C}^{\prime}\!\left<u\right>\right)
, and hence

\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(\mathtt{C}\!\left<t\right>\right)=\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(s\right)[x\backslash\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(\mathtt{C}^{\prime}\!\left<t\right>\right)]\overset{\textit{i.h.}}{\leq}\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(s\right)[x\backslash\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(\mathtt{C}^{\prime}\!\left<u\right>\right)]=\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(\mathtt{C}\!\left<u^{\prime}\right>\right)
by contextual closure.
∎

Dynamic Approximation and Lifting.

We now prove the instance of

A
for
dCBN
.

Lemma C.9 (
dCBN
Dynamic Approximation).

Let
k\in\mathbb{N}^{\omega}
and
t,u\in\Lambda
such
that
t\rightarrow_{\mathrm{n}_{k}}u
, then there exists
\mathbf{u}\in\mathbf{\Lambda}
such that
\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(t\right)\rightarrow^{=}_{\mathbf{n}_{k}}\mathbf{u}\geq\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(u\right)
.

Proof.

Let
t,u\in\Lambda
such that
t\rightarrow_{\mathrm{n}_{k}}u
. We
distinguish two cases:

t

dCBN
-meaningless: Then
u
is
dCBN
-meaningful
using
Lemma

C.4

thus
\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(t\right)=\bot
and
\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(u\right)=\bot
. We set
\mathbf{u}\coloneqq\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(u\right)
therefore
by reflexivity of both relations,
\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(t\right)\rightarrow^{=}_{\mathbf{n}_{k}}\mathbf{u}\geq\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(u\right)
.

t

dCBN
-meaningful: Then
u
is
dCBN
-meaningful
using
Lemma

C.4
. By
definition, there exist a context
\mathtt{N}_{k}
and
t^{\prime},u^{\prime}\in\Lambda
such that
t=\mathtt{N}_{k}\!\left<t^{\prime}\right>
,
u=\mathtt{N}_{k}\!\left<u^{\prime}\right>
and
t^{\prime}\mapsto_{\mathcal{R}}u^{\prime}
for some
\mathcal{R}\in\{{\tt dB},{\tt s}\}
. We reason by induction on

\mathtt{N}_{k}
:

\mathtt{N}_{k}=\diamond
: Then
t=t^{\prime}
and
u=u^{\prime}
. We distinguish two cases:

\bullet

\mathcal{R}={\tt dB}
: Then
t^{\prime}=\mathtt{L}\!\left<\lambda x.s_{1}\right>s_{2}
and
u^{\prime}=\mathtt{L}\!\left<s_{1}[x\backslash s_{2}]\right>
for some list context

\mathtt{L}
and some
s_{1},s_{2}\in\Lambda
. Since

t^{\prime}
is
dCBN
-meaningful, then using

Lemma

C.5
.(
1
),
one deduces in particular that
\mathtt{L}\!\left<\lambda x.s_{1}\right>

is
dCBN
-meaningful. Since
u^{\prime}
is
dCBN
-meaningful,
then using

Lemma

C.5
.(
2
),
one deduces in particular that
s_{1}[x\backslash s_{2}]
is

dCBN
-meaningful. Thus, by

Corollary

C.7
, one has

\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(t^{\prime}\right)=\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(\mathtt{L}\!\left<\lambda x.s_{1}\right>\right)\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(s_{2}\right)=\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(\mathtt{L}\right)\!\left<\lambda x.\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(s_{1}\right)\right>\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(s_{2}\right)

and
\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(u^{\prime}\right)=\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(\mathtt{L}\!\left<s_{1}[x\backslash s_{2}]\right>\right)=\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(\mathtt{L}\right)\!\left<\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(s_{1}[x\backslash s_{2}]\right)\right>=\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(\mathtt{L}\right)\!\left<\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(s_{1}\right)[x\backslash\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(s_{2}\right)]\right>
.
Hence,
\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(t\right)=\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(t^{\prime}\right)\mapsto_{{\tt dB}}\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(u^{\prime}\right)=\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(u\right)
.
We set
\mathbf{u}\coloneqq\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(u\right)
, therefore by
reflexivity
\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(t\right)\rightarrow_{\mathbf{n}_{k}}\mathbf{u}\geq\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(u\right)
.

\bullet

\mathcal{R}={\tt s}
: Then
t^{\prime}=s_{1}[x\backslash s_{2}]
and
u^{\prime}=s_{1}\{x\backslash s_{2}\}
for some

s_{1},s_{2}\in\Lambda
. Using

Lemma

C.8
, one has
that
\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(t^{\prime}\right)=\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(s_{1}\right)[x\backslash\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(s_{2}\right)]
and

\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(u^{\prime}\right)\leq\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(s_{1}\right)\{x\backslash\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(s_{2}\right)\}
. Thus

\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(t\right)=\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(t^{\prime}\right)\mapsto_{{\tt s}}\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(s_{1}\right)\{x\backslash\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(s_{2}\right)\}\geq\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(u^{\prime}\right)=\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(u\right)
. We set
\mathbf{u}\coloneqq\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(s_{1}\right)\{x\backslash\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(s_{2}\right)\}
,
therefore
\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(t\right)\rightarrow_{\mathbf{n}_{k}}\mathbf{u}\geq\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(u\right)
.

\mathtt{N}_{k}=\lambda x.\mathtt{N}^{\prime}_{k}
:
By
i.h.
on
\mathtt{N}^{\prime}_{k}
, one has
\mathbf{u}^{\prime}\in\mathbf{\Lambda}
such that

\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(\mathtt{N}^{\prime}_{k}\!\left<t^{\prime}\right>\right)\rightarrow^{=}_{\mathbf{n}_{k}}\mathbf{u}^{\prime}\geq\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(\mathtt{N}^{\prime}_{k}\!\left<u^{\prime}\right>\right)
. We
set
\mathbf{u}\coloneqq\lambda x.\mathbf{u}^{\prime}
, therefore

\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(\mathtt{N}_{k}\!\left<t^{\prime}\right>\right)=\lambda x.\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(\mathtt{N}^{\prime}_{k}\!\left<t^{\prime}\right>\right)\rightarrow^{=}_{\mathbf{n}_{k}}\mathbf{u}\geq\lambda x.\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(\mathtt{N}^{\prime}_{k}\!\left<u^{\prime}\right>\right)=\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(\mathtt{N}_{k}\!\left<u^{\prime}\right>\right)
.

\mathtt{N}_{k}=\mathtt{N}^{\prime}_{k}\,s
: By
i.h.
on

\mathtt{N}^{\prime}_{k}
, one has
\mathbf{u}^{\prime}\in\mathbf{\Lambda}

such that
\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(\mathtt{N}^{\prime}_{k}\!\left<t^{\prime}\right>\right)\rightarrow^{=}_{\mathbf{n}_{k}}\mathbf{u}^{\prime}\geq\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(\mathtt{N}^{\prime}_{k}\!\left<u^{\prime}\right>\right)
. We set
\mathbf{u}\coloneqq\mathbf{u}^{\prime}\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(s\right)
, therefore

\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(\mathtt{N}_{k}\!\left<t^{\prime}\right>\right)=\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(\mathtt{N}^{\prime}_{k}\!\left<t^{\prime}\right>\right)\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(s\right)\rightarrow^{=}_{\mathbf{n}_{k}}\mathbf{u}\geq\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(\mathtt{N}^{\prime}_{k}\!\left<u^{\prime}\right>\right)\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(s\right)=\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(\mathtt{N}_{k}\!\left<u^{\prime}\right>\right)
.

\mathtt{N}_{k}=s\,\mathtt{N}_{m}
with
either
k>0
and
m=k-1
, or
k=m=\omega
: By
i.h.
on
\mathtt{N}^{\prime}_{m}
, one has
\mathbf{u}^{\prime}\in\mathbf{\Lambda}
such that
\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(\mathtt{N}_{m}\!\left<t^{\prime}\right>\right)\rightarrow^{=}_{\mathbf{n}_{m}}\mathbf{u}^{\prime}\geq\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(\mathtt{N}_{m}\!\left<u^{\prime}\right>\right)
. We set
\mathbf{u}\coloneqq\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(s\right)\mathbf{u}^{\prime}
, therefore

\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(\mathtt{N}_{k}\!\left<t^{\prime}\right>\right)=\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(s\right)\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(\mathtt{N}_{m}\!\left<t^{\prime}\right>\right)\rightarrow^{=}_{\mathbf{n}_{k}}\mathbf{u}\geq\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(s\right)\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(\mathtt{N}_{m}\!\left<u^{\prime}\right>\right)=\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(\mathtt{N}_{k}\!\left<u^{\prime}\right>\right)
.

\mathtt{N}_{k}=\mathtt{N}^{\prime}_{k}[x\backslash s]
:
By
i.h.
on
\mathtt{N}^{\prime}_{k}
, one has
\mathbf{u}^{\prime}\in\mathbf{\Lambda}
such that

\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(\mathtt{N}^{\prime}_{k}\!\left<t^{\prime}\right>\right)\rightarrow^{=}_{\mathbf{n}_{k}}\mathbf{u}^{\prime}\geq\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(\mathtt{N}^{\prime}_{k}\!\left<u^{\prime}\right>\right)
. We
set
\mathbf{u}\coloneqq\mathbf{u}^{\prime}[x\backslash\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(s\right)]
,
therefore
\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(\mathtt{N}_{k}\!\left<t^{\prime}\right>\right)=\mathbf{u}^{\prime}[x\backslash\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(s\right)]\rightarrow^{=}_{\mathbf{n}_{k}}\mathbf{u}\geq\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(\mathtt{N}^{\prime}_{k}\!\left<u^{\prime}\right>\right)[x\backslash\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(s\right)]=\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(\mathtt{N}_{k}\!\left<u^{\prime}\right>\right)
.

\mathtt{N}_{k}=s[x\backslash\mathtt{N}_{m}]
with
either
k>0
and
m=k-1
, or
k=m=\omega
: By
i.h.
on
\mathtt{N}_{m}
, one has
\mathbf{u}^{\prime}\in\mathbf{\Lambda}
such that
\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(\mathtt{N}_{m}\!\left<t^{\prime}\right>\right)\rightarrow^{=}_{\mathbf{n}_{m}}\mathbf{u}^{\prime}\geq\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(\mathtt{N}_{m}\!\left<u^{\prime}\right>\right)
. We set
\mathbf{u}\coloneqq\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(s\right)[x\backslash\mathbf{u}^{\prime}]
, therefore

\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(\mathtt{N}_{k}\!\left<t^{\prime}\right>\right)=\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(s\right)[x\backslash\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(\mathtt{N}_{m}\!\left<t^{\prime}\right>\right)]\rightarrow^{=}_{\mathbf{n}_{k}}\mathbf{u}\geq\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(s\right)[x\backslash\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(\mathtt{N}_{m}\!\left<u^{\prime}\right>\right)]=\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(\mathtt{N}_{k}\!\left<u^{\prime}\right>\right)
.
∎

We now prove the instance of

B
for

dCBN
.

Lemma C.10 (
dCBN
Dynamic Partial Lift).

lemma
Let
k\in\mathbb{N}^{\omega}
and
\mathbf{t},\mathbf{u},\mathbf{t}^{\prime}\in\mathbf{\Lambda}
. If
\mathbf{t}\rightarrow_{\mathbf{n}_{k}}\mathbf{u}
and

\mathbf{t}\leq\mathbf{t}^{\prime}
, then there is
\mathbf{u}^{\prime}\in\mathbf{\Lambda}
such that
\mathbf{t}^{\prime}\rightarrow_{\mathbf{n}_{k}}\mathbf{u}^{\prime}
and
\mathbf{u}\leq\mathbf{u}^{\prime}
.

Proof.

Let
\mathbf{t},\mathbf{u},\mathbf{t}^{\prime}\in\mathbf{\Lambda}
such that
\mathbf{t}\leq\mathbf{t}^{\prime}
and
\mathbf{t}\rightarrow_{\mathbf{n}_{k}}\mathbf{u}
. Then

\mathbf{t}=\mathbf{N}_{k}\!\left<\mathbf{t}_{r}\right>
and
\mathbf{u}=\mathbf{N}_{k}\!\left<\mathbf{u}_{r}\right>
with
\mathbf{t}_{r}\mapsto_{\mathcal{R}}\mathbf{u}_{r}
for some
\mathbf{t}_{r},\mathbf{u}_{r}\in\mathbf{\Lambda}
and
\mathcal{R}\in\{{\tt dB},{\tt s}\}
. Let us proceed by induction on

\mathbf{N}_{k}
:

\mathbf{N}_{k}=\diamond
: Then
\mathbf{t}=\mathbf{t}_{r}

and
\mathbf{u}=\mathbf{u}_{r}
. We distinguish two cases:

\mathcal{R}={\tt dB}
: Then
\mathbf{t}_{r}=\mathbf{L}\!\left<\lambda x.\mathbf{s}_{1}\right>\mathbf{s}_{2}
and

\mathbf{u}_{r}=\mathbf{L}\!\left<\mathbf{s}_{1}[x\backslash\mathbf{s}_{2}]\right>
for
some list context
\mathbf{L}
and some
\mathbf{s}_{1},\mathbf{s}_{2}\in\mathbf{\Lambda}
. Since
\mathbf{t}\leq\mathbf{t}^{\prime}
, by induction on
\mathbf{L}
, one has that

\mathbf{t}=\mathbf{L}^{\prime}\!\left<\lambda x.\mathbf{s}^{\prime}_{1}\right>\mathbf{s}^{\prime}_{2}

with
\mathbf{L}\leq\mathbf{L}^{\prime}
,
\mathbf{s}_{1}\leq\mathbf{s}^{\prime}_{1}
and
\mathbf{s}_{2}\leq\mathbf{s}^{\prime}_{2}

for some list context
\mathbf{L}^{\prime}
and some
\mathbf{s}^{\prime}_{1},\mathbf{s}^{\prime}_{2}\in\mathbf{\Lambda}
. Taking
\mathbf{u}^{\prime}=\mathbf{L}^{\prime}\!\left<\mathbf{s}^{\prime}_{1}[x\backslash\mathbf{s}^{\prime}_{2}]\right>
concludes this
case since
\mathbf{t}^{\prime}=\mathbf{L}^{\prime}\!\left<\lambda x.\mathbf{s}^{\prime}_{1}\right>\mathbf{s}^{\prime}_{2}\rightarrow_{\mathbf{n}_{k}}\mathbf{L}^{\prime}\!\left<\mathbf{s}^{\prime}_{1}[x\backslash\mathbf{s}^{\prime}_{2}]\right>=\mathbf{u}^{\prime}
and by
transitivity
\mathbf{s}_{1}[x\backslash\mathbf{s}_{2}]\leq\mathbf{s}^{\prime}_{1}[x\backslash\mathbf{s}^{\prime}_{2}]
thus

\mathbf{L}\!\left<\mathbf{s}_{1}[x\backslash\mathbf{s}_{2}]\right>\leq\mathbf{L}^{\prime}\!\left<\mathbf{s}^{\prime}_{1}[x\backslash\mathbf{s}^{\prime}_{2}]\right>=\mathbf{u}^{\prime}
using

lemma

C.3
.

\mathcal{R}={\tt s}
: Then
\mathbf{t}_{r}=\mathbf{s}_{1}[x\backslash\mathbf{s}_{2}]
and
\mathbf{u}_{r}=\mathbf{s}_{1}\{x\backslash\mathbf{s}_{2}\}
for some partial terms
\mathbf{s}_{1},\mathbf{s}_{2}\in\mathbf{\Lambda}
. Since
\mathbf{t}\leq\mathbf{t}^{\prime}
, one has that
\mathbf{t}^{\prime}=\mathbf{s}^{\prime}_{1}[x\backslash\mathbf{s}^{\prime}_{2}]
with
\mathbf{s}_{1}\leq\mathbf{s}^{\prime}_{1}
and
\mathbf{s}_{2}\leq\mathbf{s}^{\prime}_{2}
for some some
partial terms
\mathbf{s}^{\prime}_{1},\mathbf{s}^{\prime}_{2}\in\mathbf{\Lambda}
.
Taking
\mathbf{u}^{\prime}=\mathbf{s}^{\prime}_{1}\{x\backslash\mathbf{s}^{\prime}_{2}\}
concludes this
case since
\mathbf{t}^{\prime}=\mathbf{s}^{\prime}_{1}[x\backslash\mathbf{s}^{\prime}_{2}]\rightarrow_{\mathbf{n}_{k}}\mathbf{s}^{\prime}_{1}\{x\backslash\mathbf{s}^{\prime}_{2}\}
and
using

lemma

C.1
,
one has that
\mathbf{s}_{1}\{x\backslash\mathbf{s}_{2}\}\leq\mathbf{s}^{\prime}_{1}\{x\backslash\mathbf{s}^{\prime}_{2}\}
.

\mathbf{N}_{k}=\lambda x.\mathbf{N}^{\prime}_{k}
: Then
\mathbf{t}=\lambda x.\mathbf{N}^{\prime}_{k}\!\left<\mathbf{t}_{r}\right>
and
\mathbf{u}=\lambda x.\mathbf{N}^{\prime}_{k}\!\left<\mathbf{u}_{r}\right>
thus
\mathbf{t}^{\prime}=\lambda x.\mathbf{t}^{\prime}_{1}
for some
\mathbf{t}^{\prime}_{1}\in\mathbf{\Lambda}
such
that
\mathbf{N}^{\prime}_{k}\!\left<\mathbf{t}_{r}\right>\leq\mathbf{t}^{\prime}_{1}
.
Since
\mathbf{N}^{\prime}_{k}\!\left<\mathbf{t}_{r}\right>\rightarrow_{\mathbf{n}_{k}}\mathbf{N}^{\prime}_{k}\!\left<\mathbf{u}_{r}\right>
, by
i.h.
on

\mathbf{N}^{\prime}_{k}
, one obtains
\mathbf{u}^{\prime}_{1}\in\mathbf{\Lambda}
such that
\mathbf{t}^{\prime}_{1}\rightarrow_{\mathbf{n}_{k}}\mathbf{u}^{\prime}_{1}
and
\mathbf{N}^{\prime}_{k}\!\left<\mathbf{u}_{r}\right>\leq\mathbf{u}^{\prime}_{1}
. Taking
\mathbf{u}^{\prime}=\lambda x.\mathbf{u}^{\prime}_{1}
concludes this
case since
\mathbf{t}^{\prime}=\lambda x.\mathbf{t}^{\prime}_{1}\rightarrow_{\mathbf{n}_{k}}\lambda x.\mathbf{u}^{\prime}_{1}=\mathbf{u}^{\prime}
and
\mathbf{u}=\lambda x.\mathbf{N}^{\prime}_{k}\!\left<\mathbf{u}_{r}\right>\leq\lambda x.\mathbf{u}^{\prime}_{1}=\mathbf{u}^{\prime}
.

\mathbf{N}_{k}=\mathbf{N}^{\prime}_{k}\,\mathbf{s}
: Then
\mathbf{t}=\mathbf{N}^{\prime}_{k}\!\left<\mathbf{t}_{r}\right>\mathbf{s}
and
\mathbf{u}=\mathbf{N}^{\prime}_{k}\!\left<\mathbf{u}_{r}\right>\mathbf{s}
thus
\mathbf{t}^{\prime}=\mathbf{t}^{\prime}_{1}\,\mathbf{s}^{\prime}
for some partial term
\mathbf{t}^{\prime}_{1},\mathbf{s}\in\mathbf{\Lambda}
such that

\mathbf{N}^{\prime}_{k}\!\left<\mathbf{t}_{r}\right>\leq\mathbf{t}^{\prime}_{1}
and

\mathbf{s}\leq\mathbf{s}^{\prime}
. Since

\mathbf{N}^{\prime}_{k}\!\left<\mathbf{t}_{r}\right>\rightarrow_{\mathbf{n}_{k}}\mathbf{N}^{\prime}_{k}\!\left<\mathbf{u}_{r}\right>
, by
i.h.
on

\mathbf{N}^{\prime}_{k}
, one obtains
\mathbf{u}^{\prime}_{1}\in\mathbf{\Lambda}
such that
\mathbf{t}^{\prime}_{1}\rightarrow_{\mathbf{n}_{k}}\mathbf{u}^{\prime}_{1}
and
\mathbf{N}^{\prime}_{k}\!\left<\mathbf{u}_{r}\right>\leq\mathbf{u}^{\prime}_{1}
. Taking
\mathbf{u}^{\prime}=\mathbf{u}^{\prime}_{1}\mathbf{s}^{\prime}
concludes
this case since
\mathbf{t}^{\prime}=\mathbf{t}^{\prime}_{1}\mathbf{s}^{\prime}\rightarrow_{\mathbf{n}_{k}}\mathbf{u}^{\prime}_{1}\mathbf{s}^{\prime}=\mathbf{u}^{\prime}
and

\mathbf{u}=\mathbf{N}^{\prime}_{k}\!\left<\mathbf{u}_{r}\right>\mathbf{s}\leq\mathbf{u}^{\prime}_{1}\mathbf{s}^{\prime}=\mathbf{u}^{\prime}
by transitivity.

\mathbf{N}_{k}=\mathbf{s}\,\mathbf{N}^{\prime}_{m}
with either
k>0
and

m=k-1
, or
k=m=\omega
: Then
\mathbf{t}=\mathbf{s}\,\mathbf{N}^{\prime}_{m}\!\left<\mathbf{t}_{r}\right>
and
\mathbf{u}=\mathbf{s}\,\mathbf{N}^{\prime}_{m}\!\left<\mathbf{u}_{r}\right>
thus
\mathbf{t}^{\prime}=\mathbf{s}^{\prime}\,\mathbf{t}^{\prime}_{1}
for some
\mathbf{s},\mathbf{t}^{\prime}_{1}\in\mathbf{\Lambda}
such that
\mathbf{s}\leq\mathbf{s}^{\prime}
and

\mathbf{N}^{\prime}_{m}\!\left<\mathbf{t}_{r}\right>\leq\mathbf{t}^{\prime}_{1}
. Since

\mathbf{N}^{\prime}_{m}\!\left<\mathbf{t}_{r}\right>\rightarrow_{\mathbf{n}_{m}}\mathbf{N}^{\prime}_{m}\!\left<\mathbf{u}_{r}\right>
, by
i.h.
on
\mathbf{N}^{\prime}_{m}
, one
obtains
\mathbf{u}^{\prime}_{1}\in\mathbf{\Lambda}
such that
\mathbf{t}^{\prime}_{1}\rightarrow_{\mathbf{n}_{m}}\mathbf{u}^{\prime}_{1}
and
\mathbf{N}^{\prime}_{m}\!\left<\mathbf{u}_{r}\right>\leq\mathbf{u}^{\prime}_{1}
. Taking
\mathbf{u}^{\prime}=\mathbf{s}^{\prime}\mathbf{u}^{\prime}_{1}
concludes this case since
\mathbf{t}^{\prime}=\mathbf{s}^{\prime}\mathbf{t}^{\prime}_{1}\rightarrow_{\mathbf{n}_{k}}\mathbf{s}^{\prime}\mathbf{u}^{\prime}_{1}=\mathbf{u}^{\prime}
and
\mathbf{u}=\mathbf{s}\,\mathbf{N}^{\prime}_{k}\!\left<\mathbf{u}_{r}\right>\leq\mathbf{s}^{\prime}\mathbf{u}^{\prime}_{1}=\mathbf{u}^{\prime}
by transitivity.

\mathbf{N}_{k}=\mathbf{N}^{\prime}_{k}[x\backslash\mathbf{s}]
: Then
\mathbf{t}=\mathbf{N}^{\prime}_{k}\!\left<\mathbf{t}_{r}\right>[x\backslash\mathbf{s}]
and
\mathbf{u}=\mathbf{N}^{\prime}_{k}\!\left<\mathbf{u}_{r}\right>[x\backslash\mathbf{s}]
thus
\mathbf{t}^{\prime}=\mathbf{t}^{\prime}_{1}[x\backslash\mathbf{s}^{\prime}]
for some
\mathbf{t}^{\prime}_{1},\mathbf{s}\in\mathbf{\Lambda}
such that
\mathbf{N}^{\prime}_{k}\!\left<\mathbf{t}_{r}\right>\leq\mathbf{t}^{\prime}_{1}
and
\mathbf{s}\leq\mathbf{s}^{\prime}
. Since

\mathbf{N}^{\prime}_{k}\!\left<\mathbf{t}_{r}\right>\rightarrow_{\mathbf{n}_{k}}\mathbf{N}^{\prime}_{k}\!\left<\mathbf{u}_{r}\right>
, by
i.h.
on

\mathbf{N}^{\prime}_{k}
, one obtains
\mathbf{u}^{\prime}_{1}\in\mathbf{\Lambda}
such that
\mathbf{t}^{\prime}_{1}\rightarrow_{\mathbf{n}_{k}}\mathbf{u}^{\prime}_{1}
and
\mathbf{N}^{\prime}_{k}\!\left<\mathbf{u}_{r}\right>\leq\mathbf{u}^{\prime}_{1}
. Taking
\mathbf{u}^{\prime}=\mathbf{u}^{\prime}_{1}[x\backslash\mathbf{s}^{\prime}]

concludes this case since
\mathbf{t}^{\prime}=\mathbf{t}^{\prime}_{1}[x\backslash\mathbf{s}^{\prime}]\rightarrow_{\mathbf{n}_{k}}\mathbf{u}^{\prime}_{1}[x\backslash\mathbf{s}^{\prime}]=\mathbf{u}^{\prime}
and

\mathbf{u}=\mathbf{N}^{\prime}_{k}\!\left<\mathbf{u}_{r}\right>[x\backslash\mathbf{s}]\leq\mathbf{u}^{\prime}_{1}[x\backslash\mathbf{s}^{\prime}]=\mathbf{u}^{\prime}
by transitivity.

\mathbf{N}_{k}=\mathbf{s}[x\backslash\mathbf{N}^{\prime}_{m}]
with either
k>0
and
m=k-1
, or
k=m=\omega
: Then
\mathbf{t}=\mathbf{s}[x\backslash\mathbf{N}^{\prime}_{m}\!\left<\mathbf{t}_{r}\right>]
and
\mathbf{u}=\mathbf{s}[x\backslash\mathbf{N}^{\prime}_{m}\!\left<\mathbf{u}_{r}\right>]
thus
\mathbf{t}^{\prime}=\mathbf{s}^{\prime}[x\backslash\mathbf{t}^{\prime}_{1}]
for some
\mathbf{s},\mathbf{t}^{\prime}_{1}\in\mathbf{\Lambda}
such that
\mathbf{s}\leq\mathbf{s}^{\prime}
and

\mathbf{N}^{\prime}_{m}\!\left<\mathbf{t}_{r}\right>\leq\mathbf{t}^{\prime}_{1}
. Since

\mathbf{N}^{\prime}_{m}\!\left<\mathbf{t}_{r}\right>\rightarrow_{\mathbf{n}_{m}}\mathbf{N}^{\prime}_{m}\!\left<\mathbf{u}_{r}\right>
, by
i.h.
on
\mathbf{N}^{\prime}_{m}
, one
obtains
\mathbf{u}^{\prime}_{1}\in\mathbf{\Lambda}
such that
\mathbf{t}^{\prime}_{1}\rightarrow_{\mathbf{n}_{m}}\mathbf{u}^{\prime}_{1}
and
\mathbf{N}^{\prime}_{m}\!\left<\mathbf{u}_{r}\right>\leq\mathbf{u}^{\prime}_{1}
. Taking
\mathbf{u}^{\prime}=\mathbf{s}^{\prime}[x\backslash\mathbf{u}^{\prime}_{1}]
concludes this case since
\mathbf{t}^{\prime}=\mathbf{s}^{\prime}[x\backslash\mathbf{t}^{\prime}_{1}]\rightarrow_{\mathbf{n}_{k}}\mathbf{s}^{\prime}[x\backslash\mathbf{u}^{\prime}_{1}]=\mathbf{u}^{\prime}
and
\mathbf{u}=\mathbf{s}[x\backslash\mathbf{N}^{\prime}_{k}\!\left<\mathbf{u}_{r}\right>]\leq\mathbf{s}^{\prime}[x\backslash\mathbf{u}^{\prime}_{1}]=\mathbf{u}^{\prime}
by transitivity.
∎

Observability.

Let us recall the mutual
inductive definitions of
{\tt bne}_{\mathbf{n}_{k}}
and

{\tt bno}_{\mathbf{n}_{k}}
:

\begin{array}[]{rcl}{\tt bne}_{\mathbf{n}_{0}}&\coloneqq&x\;|\;{\tt bne}_{\mathbf{n}_{0}}\mathbf{t}\\
{\tt bne}_{\mathbf{n}_{i+1}}&\coloneqq&x\;|\;{\tt bne}_{\mathbf{n}_{i+1}}{\tt bno}_{\mathbf{n}_{i}}\qquad(i\in\mathbb{N})\\
{\tt bne}_{\mathbf{n}_{\omega}}&\coloneqq&x\;|\;{\tt bne}_{\mathbf{n}_{\omega}}{\tt bno}_{\mathbf{n}_{\omega}}\\[5.69046pt]
{\tt bno}_{\mathbf{n}_{k}}&\coloneqq&\lambda x.{\tt bno}_{\mathbf{n}_{k}}\;|\;{\tt bne}_{\mathbf{n}_{k}}\qquad(k\in\mathbb{N}^{\omega})\end{array}

We now prove the instance of

C
for
dCBN
.

Lemma C.11 (
dCBN
Observability of Normal Form Approximants).

Let
k\in\mathbb{N}^{\omega}
and
\mathbf{t}\in{\tt no}_{\mathrm{n}_{k}}
,
then
\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(t\right)\in{\tt bno}_{\mathbf{n}_{k}}
.

Proof.

We strengthen the induction hypothesis:

Let
t\in\Lambda
such that
{\tt NF}_{\mathrm{n}_{k}}(t)
, then

\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(t\right)\in{\tt bno}_{\mathbf{n}_{k}}
. And if, moreover,

\neg{\tt Abs}\left(t\right)
then
\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(t\right)\in{\tt bne}_{\mathbf{n}_{k}}
.

Since
{\tt NF}_{\mathrm{n}_{k}}(t)
, then in particular
{\tt NF}_{\mathrm{n}}(t)
,
so that
t
is meaningful. We reason by induction on
t\in\Lambda
:

t=x
: Then
\neg{\tt Abs}\left(t\right)
and
\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(t\right)=x\in{\tt bne}_{\mathbf{n}_{k}}
.

t=\lambda x.t^{\prime}
: Then
{\tt NF}_{\mathrm{n}_{k}}(t^{\prime})
and

{\tt Abs}\left(t\right)
, thus
\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(t^{\prime}\right)\in{\tt bno}_{\mathbf{n}_{k}}

by
i.h.
on
t^{\prime}
. Hence,
\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(t\right)=\lambda y.\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(t^{\prime}\right)\in\lambda y.{\tt bno}_{\mathbf{n}_{k}}\subseteq{\tt bno}_{\mathbf{n}_{k}}
.

t=t_{1}t_{2}
: Then
\neg{\tt Abs}\left(t\right)
,

{\tt NF}_{\mathrm{n}_{k}}(t_{1})
and
\neg{\tt Abs}\left(t_{1}\right)
. By
i.h.
on

t_{1}
, one has that
\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(t_{1}\right)\in{\tt bne}_{\mathbf{n}_{k}}
.
We distinguish two cases:

–

k=0
: Then
\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(t\right)=\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(t_{1}\right)\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(t_{2}\right)\in{\tt bne}_{\mathbf{n}_{0}}\mathbf{t}\subseteq{\tt bno}_{\mathbf{n}_{0}}
.

–

k\in\mathbb{N}\setminus\{0\}
(resp.
k=\omega
): Then
{\tt NF}_{\mathrm{n}_{m}}(t_{2})
with
m=k-1
(resp.
m=\omega
). By
i.h.
on
t_{2}
, one has that

\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(t_{2}\right)\in{\tt bno}_{\mathbf{n}_{m}}
thus
\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(t\right)=\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(t_{1}\right)\mathcal{M\!A}_{{\tt N}}\!\left(t_{2}\right)\in{\tt bne}_{\mathbf{n}_{k}}{\tt bno}_{\mathbf{n}_{m}}\subseteq{\tt bne}_{\mathbf{n}_{k}}
.

t=t_{1}[x\backslash t_{2}]
: Impossible since

{\tt NF}_{\mathrm{n}_{k}}(t)
.
∎

Let us recall the inductive definition of
dCBN
stratified
equality:

\begin{array}[]{c}\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\vbox{\hbox{\hskip 8.16028pt\hbox{}}\vbox{\hbox{\hbox{\lower 3.21385pt\vbox{\vskip 3.01385pt\hrule height=0.4pt\vskip 3.01385pt\par}}\enskip\hbox{\lower 2.15277pt\hbox{\!\!\scriptsize$({\tt var})$}}}\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\hbox{$\hfil\lx@intercol$}}\hbox{$x\overset{\scriptscriptstyle i}{\approx}x$}}\par}\par}}}$}\hskip 42.67912pt\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\vbox{\hbox{\hskip 15.99304pt\hbox{}}\vbox{\hbox{\hbox{\lower 3.21385pt\vbox{\vskip 3.01385pt\hrule height=0.4pt\vskip 3.01385pt\par}}\enskip\hbox{\lower 2.15277pt\hbox{\!\!\scriptsize$({\tt abs})$}}}\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\hbox{$t\overset{\scriptscriptstyle i}{\approx}u$}}\hbox{$\lambda x.t\overset{\scriptscriptstyle i}{\approx}\lambda x.u$}}\par}\par}}}$}\\[14.22636pt]
\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\vbox{\hbox{\enskip\hbox{}}\vbox{\hbox{\hbox{\lower 3.21385pt\vbox{\vskip 3.01385pt\hrule height=0.4pt\vskip 3.01385pt\par}}\enskip\hbox{\lower 2.15277pt\hbox{\!\!\scriptsize$({\tt app})$}}}\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\hbox{$t_{1}\overset{\scriptscriptstyle 0}{\approx}u_{1}\hfil\lx@intercol$}}\hbox{$t_{1}t_{2}\overset{\scriptscriptstyle 0}{\approx}u_{1}u_{2}$}}\par}\par}}}$}\hskip 14.22636pt\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\vbox{\hbox{\hskip 8.14703pt\hbox{}}\vbox{\hbox{\hbox{\lower 3.21385pt\vbox{\vskip 3.01385pt\hrule height=0.4pt\vskip 3.01385pt\par}}\enskip\hbox{\lower 2.15277pt\hbox{\!\!\scriptsize$({\tt app})$}}}\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\hbox{\hbox{}\hskip 15.00002pt\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\hbox{$t_{1}\overset{\scriptscriptstyle i+1}{\approx}u_{1}$}}\hbox{$t_{2}\overset{\scriptscriptstyle i}{\approx}u_{2}$}}}}\hbox{$t_{1}t_{2}\overset{\scriptscriptstyle i+1}{\approx}u_{1}u_{2}$}}\par}\par}}}$}\hskip 14.22636pt\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\vbox{\hbox{\hskip 1.65396pt\hbox{}}\vbox{\hbox{\hbox{\lower 3.21385pt\vbox{\vskip 3.01385pt\hrule height=0.4pt\vskip 3.01385pt\par}}\enskip\hbox{\lower 2.15277pt\hbox{\!\!\scriptsize$({\tt app})$}}}\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\hbox{\hbox{}\hskip 15.00002pt\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\hbox{$t_{1}\overset{\scriptscriptstyle\omega}{\approx}u_{1}$}}\hbox{$t_{2}\overset{\scriptscriptstyle\omega}{\approx}u_{2}$}}}}\hbox{$t_{1}t_{2}\overset{\scriptscriptstyle\omega}{\approx}u_{1}u_{2}$}}\par}\par}}}$}\\[14.22636pt]
\hskip-11.38092pt\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\vbox{\hbox{\hskip 25.7025pt\hbox{}}\vbox{\hbox{\hbox{\lower 3.21385pt\vbox{\vskip 3.01385pt\hrule height=0.4pt\vskip 3.01385pt\par}}\enskip\hbox{\lower 2.15277pt\hbox{\!\!\scriptsize$({\tt es})$}}}\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\hbox{$t_{1}\overset{\scriptscriptstyle 0}{\approx}u_{1}\hfil\lx@intercol$}}\hbox{$t_{1}[x\backslash t_{2}]\overset{\scriptscriptstyle 0}{\approx}u_{1}[x\backslash u_{2}]$}}\par}\par}}}$}\hskip 2.84544pt\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\vbox{\hbox{\hskip 28.86223pt\hbox{}}\vbox{\hbox{\hbox{\lower 3.21385pt\vbox{\vskip 3.01385pt\hrule height=0.4pt\vskip 3.01385pt\par}}\enskip\hbox{\lower 2.15277pt\hbox{\!\!\scriptsize$({\tt es})$}}}\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\hbox{\hbox{}\hskip 15.00002pt\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\hbox{$t_{1}\overset{\scriptscriptstyle i+1}{\approx}u_{1}$}}\hbox{$t_{2}\overset{\scriptscriptstyle i}{\approx}u_{2}$}}}}\hbox{$t_{1}[x\backslash t_{2}]\overset{\scriptscriptstyle i+1}{\approx}u_{1}[x\backslash u_{2}]$}}\par}\par}}}$}\hskip 2.84544pt\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\vbox{\hbox{\hskip 22.36916pt\hbox{}}\vbox{\hbox{\hbox{\lower 3.21385pt\vbox{\vskip 3.01385pt\hrule height=0.4pt\vskip 3.01385pt\par}}\enskip\hbox{\lower 2.15277pt\hbox{\!\!\scriptsize$({\tt es})$}}}\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\hbox{\hbox{}\hskip 15.00002pt\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\hbox{$t_{1}\overset{\scriptscriptstyle\omega}{\approx}u_{1}$}}\hbox{$t_{2}\overset{\scriptscriptstyle\omega}{\approx}u_{2}$}}}}\hbox{$t_{1}[x\backslash t_{2}]\overset{\scriptscriptstyle\omega}{\approx}u_{1}[x\backslash u_{2}]$}}\par}\par}}}$}\end{array}

We now prove the instance of

D
for
dCBN
.

Lemma C.12 (
dCBN
Stability of Meaningful Observables).

Let
k\in\mathbb{N}^{\omega}
and
\mathbf{t}\in{\tt bno}_{\mathbf{n}_{k}}
. Then for any
\mathbf{u}\in\mathbf{\Lambda}

such that
\mathbf{t}\leq\mathbf{u}
, one has that
\mathbf{u}\in{\tt bno}_{\mathbf{n}_{k}}
and
\mathbf{t}\overset{\scriptscriptstyle k}{\approx}\mathbf{u}
.

Proof.

Let
\mathbf{t},\mathbf{u}\in\mathbf{\Lambda}
such that
\mathbf{t}\leq\mathbf{u}
. We strengthen the induction hypothesis:

If
\mathbf{t}\in{\tt bne}_{\mathbf{n}_{k}}
then
\mathbf{u}\in{\tt bne}_{\mathbf{n}_{k}}
.

If
\mathbf{t}\in{\tt bno}_{\mathbf{n}_{k}}
then
\mathbf{u}\in{\tt bno}_{\mathbf{n}_{k}}
.

Moreover,
\mathbf{t}\overset{\scriptscriptstyle k}{\approx}\mathbf{u}
.

By mutual induction on
\mathbf{t}\in{\tt bno}_{\mathbf{n}_{k}}
and
\mathbf{t}\in{\tt bne}_{\mathbf{n}_{k}}
:

\mathbf{t}\in{\tt bne}_{\mathbf{n}_{k}}
:

\mathbf{t}=x
: Then
\mathbf{u}=x\in{\tt bne}_{\mathbf{n}}
and

\mathbf{t}\overset{\scriptscriptstyle k}{\approx}\mathbf{u}
using
({\tt var})
.

\mathbf{t}=\mathbf{t}_{1}\mathbf{t}_{2}
: Then
necessarily
\mathbf{u}=\mathbf{u}_{1}\,\mathbf{u}_{2}
with

\mathbf{t}_{1}\leq\mathbf{u}_{1}
and
\mathbf{t}_{2}\leq\mathbf{u}_{2}
. Moreover
\mathbf{t}_{1}\in{\tt bne}_{\mathbf{n}_{k}}
thus
by
i.h.
on
\mathbf{t}_{1}
, one has that
\mathbf{u}_{1}\in{\tt bne}_{\mathbf{n}_{k}}
and
\mathbf{t}_{1}\overset{\scriptscriptstyle k}{\approx}\mathbf{u}_{1}
.
We distinguish two cases:

\bullet

k=0
: Then
\mathbf{u}=\mathbf{u}_{1}\mathbf{u}_{2}\in{\tt bne}_{\mathbf{n}_{0}}\mathbf{t}\subseteq{\tt bne}_{\mathbf{n}_{0}}
and
\mathbf{t}\overset{\scriptscriptstyle k}{\approx}\mathbf{u}
using
({\tt app})
.

\bullet

k\in\mathbb{N}\setminus\{0\}
(resp.
k=\omega
): Then
m=k-1
(resp.
m=\omega
) and
\mathbf{t}_{2}\in{\tt bno}_{\mathbf{n}_{m}}
. By
i.h.
on
t_{2}
, one has that
\mathbf{u}_{2}\in{\tt bno}_{\mathbf{n}_{m}}
and

\mathbf{t}_{2}\overset{\scriptscriptstyle m}{\approx}\mathbf{u}_{2}
. Thus
\mathbf{u}=\mathbf{u}_{1}\mathbf{u}_{2}\in{\tt bne}_{\mathbf{n}_{k}}{\tt bno}_{\mathbf{n}_{m}}\subseteq{\tt bne}_{\mathbf{n}_{k}}
and
\mathbf{t}\overset{\scriptscriptstyle k}{\approx}\mathbf{u}

using
({\tt app})
.

\mathbf{t}\in{\tt bno}_{\mathbf{n}_{k}}
:

\mathbf{t}=\lambda x.\mathbf{t}^{\prime}
: Then
\mathbf{t}^{\prime}\in{\tt bno}_{\mathbf{n}_{k}}
and
\mathbf{u}=\lambda x.\mathbf{u}^{\prime}
with

\mathbf{t}^{\prime}\leq\mathbf{u}^{\prime}
. By
i.h.
on
\mathbf{t}^{\prime}
, one has

\mathbf{u}^{\prime}\in{\tt bno}_{\mathbf{n}_{k}}
and
\mathbf{t}^{\prime}\overset{\scriptscriptstyle k}{\approx}\mathbf{u}^{\prime}
. Thus
\mathbf{u}=\lambda x.\mathbf{u}^{\prime}\in\lambda x.{\tt bno}_{\mathbf{n}_{k}}\subseteq{\tt bno}_{\mathbf{n}_{k}}

and
\mathbf{t}\overset{\scriptscriptstyle k}{\approx}\mathbf{u}
using

({\tt abs})
.

\mathbf{t}\in{\tt bne}_{\mathbf{n}_{k}}
: By
i.h.
on

\mathbf{t}
, one has that
\mathbf{u}\in{\tt bne}_{\mathbf{n}_{k}}\subseteq{\tt bno}_{\mathbf{n}_{k}}
and
\mathbf{t}\overset{\scriptscriptstyle k}{\approx}\mathbf{u}
.
∎

Proof.

Appendix D
