D.1.
Proofs of Subsection
6.1

Let us recall the rules of the congruence
\mathcal{C}_{\mathsf{B}}
.

\begin{array}[]{c}\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\vbox{\hbox{\hskip 14.3055pt\hbox{}}\vbox{\hbox{\hbox{\lower 3.21385pt\vbox{\vskip 3.01385pt\hrule height=0.4pt\vskip 3.01385pt\par}}\enskip\hbox{\lower 2.15277pt\hbox{\!\!\scriptsize$({\tt ref})$}}}\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\hbox{}}\hbox{$t\doteq t\hfil\lx@intercol$}}\par}\par}}}$}\hskip 17.07182pt\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\vbox{\hbox{}\vbox{\hbox{\hbox{\lower 3.21385pt\vbox{\vskip 3.01385pt\hrule height=0.4pt\vskip 3.01385pt\par}}\enskip\hbox{\lower 2.15277pt\hbox{\!\!\scriptsize$({\tt sym})$}}}\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\hbox{$t\doteq u$}}\hbox{$u\doteq t$}}\par}\par}}}$}\hskip 17.07182pt\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\vbox{\hbox{}\vbox{\hbox{\hbox{\lower 3.21385pt\vbox{\vskip 3.01385pt\hrule height=0.4pt\vskip 3.01385pt\par}}\enskip\hbox{\lower 2.15277pt\hbox{\!\!\scriptsize$({\tt tra})$}}}\hbox{\hskip 8.03821pt\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\hbox{\hbox{}\hskip 15.00002pt\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\hbox{$t\doteq s$}}\hbox{$s\doteq u$}}}}\hbox{$t\doteq u$}}}\par}\par}}}$}\hskip 17.07182pt\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\vbox{\hbox{\hskip 16.36122pt\hbox{}}\vbox{\hbox{\hbox{\lower 3.21385pt\vbox{\vskip 3.01385pt\hrule height=0.4pt\vskip 3.01385pt\par}}\enskip\hbox{\lower 2.15277pt\hbox{\!\!\scriptsize$({\tt ctx})$}}}\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\hbox{$t\doteq u$}}\hbox{$\mathtt{C}\!\left<t\right>\doteq\mathtt{C}\!\left<u\right>$}}\par}\par}}}$}\hskip 17.07182pt\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\vbox{\hbox{}\vbox{\hbox{\hbox{\lower 3.21385pt\vbox{\vskip 3.01385pt\hrule height=0.4pt\vskip 3.01385pt\par}}\enskip\hbox{\lower 2.15277pt\hbox{\!\!\scriptsize$({\tt base})$}}}\hbox{\hskip 5.97232pt\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\hbox{$(t,u)\!\in\!\mathsf{B}$}}\hbox{$t\doteq u$}}}\par}\par}}}$}\end{array}

We define the theory
\bm{\mathcal{H}}^{\tt ax}
to be the set of equations generated
by the following rules.

\begin{array}[]{c}\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\vbox{\hbox{\hskip 14.4444pt\hbox{}}\vbox{\hbox{\hbox{\lower 3.21385pt\vbox{\vskip 3.01385pt\hrule height=0.4pt\vskip 3.01385pt\par}}\enskip\hbox{\lower 2.15277pt\hbox{\!\!\scriptsize$({\tt Refl})$}}}\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\hbox{}}\hbox{$t\approx t\hfil\lx@intercol$}}\par}\par}}}$}\hskip 14.22636pt\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\vbox{\hbox{}\vbox{\hbox{\hbox{\lower 3.21385pt\vbox{\vskip 3.01385pt\hrule height=0.4pt\vskip 3.01385pt\par}}\enskip\hbox{\lower 2.15277pt\hbox{\!\!\scriptsize$({\tt Base})$}}}\hbox{\hskip 7.70839pt\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\hbox{$(t,u\in\mathsf{H})$}}\hbox{$t\approx u$}}}\par}\par}}}$}\hskip 14.22636pt\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\vbox{\hbox{}\vbox{\hbox{\hbox{\lower 3.21385pt\vbox{\vskip 3.01385pt\hrule height=0.4pt\vskip 3.01385pt\par}}\enskip\hbox{\lower 2.15277pt\hbox{\!\!\scriptsize$({\tt Sym})$}}}\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\hbox{$t\approx u$}}\hbox{$u\approx t$}}\par}\par}}}$}\hskip 14.22636pt\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\vbox{\hbox{}\vbox{\hbox{\hbox{\lower 3.21385pt\vbox{\vskip 3.01385pt\hrule height=0.4pt\vskip 3.01385pt\par}}\enskip\hbox{\lower 2.15277pt\hbox{\!\!\scriptsize$({\tt Conv})$}}}\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\hbox{$t\approx u$}}\hbox{$t\sim u$}}\par}\par}}}$}\hskip 14.22636pt\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\vbox{\hbox{\hskip 16.36122pt\hbox{}}\vbox{\hbox{\hbox{\lower 3.21385pt\vbox{\vskip 3.01385pt\hrule height=0.4pt\vskip 3.01385pt\par}}\enskip\hbox{\lower 2.15277pt\hbox{\!\!\scriptsize$({\tt Ctxt})$}}}\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\hbox{$t\sim u$}}\hbox{$\mathtt{C}\!\left<t\right>\sim\mathtt{C}\!\left<u\right>$}}\par}\par}}}$}\hskip 14.22636pt\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\vbox{\hbox{}\vbox{\hbox{\hbox{\lower 3.21385pt\vbox{\vskip 3.01385pt\hrule height=0.4pt\vskip 3.01385pt\par}}\enskip\hbox{\lower 2.15277pt\hbox{\!\!\scriptsize$({\tt Conv})$}}}\hbox{\hskip 0.1389pt\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\hbox{$t\sim u$}}\hbox{$t\doteq u$}}}\par}\par}}}$}\hskip 14.22636pt\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\vbox{\hbox{}\vbox{\hbox{\hbox{\lower 3.21385pt\vbox{\vskip 3.01385pt\hrule height=0.4pt\vskip 3.01385pt\par}}\enskip\hbox{\lower 2.15277pt\hbox{\!\!\scriptsize$({\tt Trans})$}}}\hbox{\hskip 8.03821pt\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\hbox{\hbox{}\hskip 15.00002pt\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\hbox{$t\doteq s$}}\hbox{$s\doteq u$}}}}\hbox{$t\doteq u$}}}\par}\par}}}$}\end{array}

We write
\bm{\mathcal{H}}^{\tt ax}\vdash t\doteq u
(resp.
\bm{\mathcal{H}}^{\tt ax}\vdash t\approx u
,

\bm{\mathcal{H}}^{\tt ax}\vdash t\sim u
) if
t\doteq u\in\bm{\mathcal{H}}^{\tt ax}
(resp.
t\approx u\in\bm{\mathcal{H}}^{\tt ax}
,
t\sim u\in\bm{\mathcal{H}}^{\tt ax}
).

Lemma D.1.

Let
t,u\in\Lambda
. Then
\bm{\mathcal{H}}^{\tt ax}\vdash t\doteq u
iff

\mathsf{H}\vdash t\doteq u
.

Proof.

By double implication:

(\Rightarrow)

By mutual induction on the length of the proof
of
\bm{\mathcal{H}}^{\tt ax}\vdash t\doteq u
,
\bm{\mathcal{H}}^{\tt ax}\vdash t\approx u
and

\bm{\mathcal{H}}^{\tt ax}\vdash t\sim u
, one shows that

\mathsf{H}\vdash t\doteq u
. Cases on the last rule applied:

({\tt Refl})
: Then
t=u
and using

({\tt ref})
, one concludes that

\mathsf{H}\vdash t\doteq u
.

({\tt Base})
: Then
(t,u)\in\mathsf{H}
and using
({\tt base})
, one
concludes that
\mathsf{H}\vdash t\doteq u
.

({\tt Sym})
: Then

\bm{\mathcal{H}}^{\tt ax}\vdash u\approx t
and by
i.h.
, one obtains

\mathsf{H}\vdash u\doteq t
thus using
({\tt sym})
, one
concludes that
\mathsf{H}\vdash t\doteq u
.

({\tt Conv})
: Then

\bm{\mathcal{H}}^{\tt ax}\vdash t\approx u
thus by
i.h.
, one
concludes that
\mathsf{H}\vdash t\doteq u
.

({\tt Ctxt})
: Then
t=\mathtt{C}\!\left<t^{\prime}\right>

and
u=\mathtt{C}\!\left<u^{\prime}\right>
for some
t^{\prime},u^{\prime}\in\Lambda

such that
\bm{\mathcal{H}}^{\tt ax}\vdash t^{\prime}\sim u^{\prime}
. By
i.h.
, one
obtains that
\mathsf{H}\vdash t^{\prime}\doteq u^{\prime}
thus using

({\tt ctx})
, one concludes that

\mathsf{H}\vdash t\doteq u
.

({\tt Conv})
: Then

\bm{\mathcal{H}}^{\tt ax}\vdash t\sim u
and by
i.h.
, one concludes
that
\mathsf{H}\vdash t\doteq u
.

({\tt Trans})
: Then
\bm{\mathcal{H}}^{\tt ax}\vdash t\doteq s

and
\bm{\mathcal{H}}^{\tt ax}\vdash s\doteq u
for some
s\in\Lambda
. By

i.h.
on both, one has that
\mathsf{H}\vdash t\doteq s
and

\mathsf{H}\vdash s\doteq u
thus using
({\tt tra})
, one
concludes that
\mathsf{H}\vdash t\doteq u
.

(\Leftarrow)

Let us first show the following property
(
P1
): provability of
\bm{\mathcal{H}}^{\tt ax}\vdash t\doteq u

and
\bm{\mathcal{H}}^{\tt ax}\vdash t\sim u
is symmetric. By mutual
induction on
\bm{\mathcal{H}}^{\tt ax}\vdash t\doteq u
and

\bm{\mathcal{H}}^{\tt ax}\vdash t\sim u
. Cases on the last rule
applied:

({\tt Conv})
: Then

\bm{\mathcal{H}}^{\tt ax}\vdash t\approx u
and using

({\tt Sym})
, one has that

\bm{\mathcal{H}}^{\tt ax}\vdash u\approx t
thus using

({\tt Conv})
, one concludes that

\bm{\mathcal{H}}^{\tt ax}\vdash u\sim t
.

({\tt Ctxt})
: Then
t=\mathtt{C}\!\left<t^{\prime}\right>
,

u=\mathtt{C}\!\left<u^{\prime}\right>
for some
t^{\prime},u^{\prime}\in\Lambda
such
that
\bm{\mathcal{H}}^{\tt ax}\vdash t^{\prime}\sim u^{\prime}
. By
i.h.
, one has
that
\bm{\mathcal{H}}^{\tt ax}\vdash u^{\prime}\sim t^{\prime}
thus using

({\tt Ctxt})
, one concludes that

\bm{\mathcal{H}}^{\tt ax}\vdash u\sim t
.

({\tt Conv})
: Then

\bm{\mathcal{H}}^{\tt ax}\vdash t\sim u
and by
i.h.
, one has that

\bm{\mathcal{H}}^{\tt ax}\vdash u\sim t
thus using

({\tt Conv})
, one concludes that

\bm{\mathcal{H}}^{\tt ax}\vdash u\doteq t
.

({\tt Trans})
: Then
\bm{\mathcal{H}}^{\tt ax}\vdash t\doteq s

and
\bm{\mathcal{H}}^{\tt ax}\vdash s\doteq u
for some
s\in\Lambda
. By

i.h.
on both, one has that
\bm{\mathcal{H}}^{\tt ax}\vdash s\doteq t
and

\bm{\mathcal{H}}^{\tt ax}\vdash u\doteq s
thus, using
({\tt Trans})
,
one concludes that
\bm{\mathcal{H}}^{\tt ax}\vdash u\doteq t
.

We then show the following property (
P2
): provability of

\bm{\mathcal{H}}^{\tt ax}\vdash t\doteq u
is a congruence. Let
\bm{\mathcal{H}}^{\tt ax}\vdash t\doteq u
,
let us show that
\bm{\mathcal{H}}^{\tt ax}\vdash\mathtt{C}\!\left<t\right>\doteq\mathtt{C}\!\left<u\right>
. By
induction on the length of the proof of
\bm{\mathcal{H}}^{\tt ax}\vdash t\doteq u
.
Cases on the last rule applied:

({\tt Conv})
: Then

\bm{\mathcal{H}}^{\tt ax}\vdash t\sim u
thus using

({\tt Ctxt})
, one obtains

\bm{\mathcal{H}}^{\tt ax}\vdash\mathtt{C}\!\left<t\right>\sim\mathtt{C}\!\left<u\right>
and
using
({\tt Conv})
, one concludes that

\bm{\mathcal{H}}^{\tt ax}\vdash\mathtt{C}\!\left<t\right>\doteq\mathtt{C}\!\left<u\right>
.

({\tt Trans})
: Then
\bm{\mathcal{H}}^{\tt ax}\vdash t\doteq s

and
\bm{\mathcal{H}}^{\tt ax}\vdash s\doteq u
for some
s\in\Lambda
. By

i.h.
on both, one has that

\bm{\mathcal{H}}^{\tt ax}\vdash\mathtt{C}\!\left<t\right>\doteq\mathtt{C}\!\left<s\right>
and

\bm{\mathcal{H}}^{\tt ax}\vdash\mathtt{C}\!\left<s\right>\doteq\mathtt{C}\!\left<u\right>
thus using

({\tt Trans})
, one concludes that

\bm{\mathcal{H}}^{\tt ax}\vdash\mathtt{C}\!\left<t\right>\doteq\mathtt{C}\!\left<u\right>
.

Let us now show the main property. By induction on the length of
the proof of
\mathsf{H}\vdash t\doteq u
. Cases on the last rule applied:

({\tt ref})
: Then
t=u
and using

({\tt Refl})
,
({\tt Conv})
and

({\tt Conv})
, one concludes that

\bm{\mathcal{H}}^{\tt ax}\vdash t\doteq u
.

({\tt base})
: Then
(t,u)\in\mathsf{H}
and using
({\tt Base})
,

({\tt Conv})
and
({\tt Conv})
, one
concludes that
\bm{\mathcal{H}}^{\tt ax}\vdash t\doteq u
.

({\tt sym})
: Then
\mathsf{H}\vdash u\doteq t
thus
by
i.h.

\bm{\mathcal{H}}^{\tt ax}\vdash u\doteq t
and by property (
P1
),
one concludes that
\bm{\mathcal{H}}^{\tt ax}\vdash t\doteq u
.

({\tt tra})
: Then
\mathsf{H}\vdash t\doteq s

and
\mathsf{H}\vdash s\doteq u
and by
i.h.
on both, one has that

\bm{\mathcal{H}}^{\tt ax}\vdash t\doteq s
and
\bm{\mathcal{H}}^{\tt ax}\vdash s\doteq u
thus using

({\tt Trans})
, one concludes that

\bm{\mathcal{H}}^{\tt ax}\vdash t\doteq u
.

({\tt ctx})
: Then
t=\mathtt{C}\!\left<t^{\prime}\right>
and

u=\mathtt{C}\!\left<u^{\prime}\right>
for some
t^{\prime},u^{\prime}\in\Lambda
such
that and
\mathsf{H}\vdash t^{\prime}\doteq u^{\prime}
. By
i.h.
, one has

\bm{\mathcal{H}}^{\tt ax}\vdash t^{\prime}\doteq u^{\prime}
and by property (
P2
), one
concludes that
\bm{\mathcal{H}}^{\tt ax}\vdash t\doteq u
.
∎

Lemma D.2.

Let
t,u\in\Lambda
, then

(1)

If
\bm{\mathcal{H}}^{\tt ax}\vdash t\doteq u
, then there exist
n\in\mathbb{N}
and
s_{0},\dots,s_{n}\in\Lambda
such that

t=s_{0}
,
u=s_{n}
and

\bm{\mathcal{H}}^{\tt ax}\vdash s_{i}\sim s_{i+1}
for
0\leq i<n
.

(2)

\bm{\mathcal{H}}^{\tt ax}\vdash t\approx u
if and only if

\mathsf{R}\vdash t\doteq u
or
\mathsf{R}\vdash u\doteq t
from an
axiom, or
t
and
u
are meaningless.

Proof.

(1)

By induction on the length of the proof of

\bm{\mathcal{H}}^{\tt ax}\vdash t\doteq u
. Cases on the last rule applied:

({\tt Conv})
: Then

\bm{\mathcal{H}}^{\tt ax}\vdash t\sim u
and we set
n:=0
with

s_{0}:=t=u
which concludes this case since using

({\tt Refl})
and
({\tt Conv})
, one
has
\bm{\mathcal{H}}^{\tt ax}\vdash s_{0}\sim s_{0}
.

({\tt Trans})
: Then
\bm{\mathcal{H}}^{\tt ax}\vdash t\doteq s^{\prime}

and
\bm{\mathcal{H}}^{\tt ax}\vdash s^{\prime}\doteq u
for some
s^{\prime}\in\Lambda
. By

i.h.
on both, one has
n_{1},n_{2}\in\mathbb{N}
and
s_{1}^{0},\dots,s_{1}^{n_{1}},s_{2}^{0},\dots,s_{2}^{n_{2}}\in\Lambda

such that
t=s_{1}^{0}
,
s^{\prime}=s_{1}^{n_{1}}
,
s^{\prime}=s_{2}^{0}
,
u=s_{2}^{n_{2}}
,
\bm{\mathcal{H}}^{\tt ax}\vdash s_{1}^{i}\sim s_{1}^{i+1}

for all
0\leq i<n_{1}
and

\bm{\mathcal{H}}^{\tt ax}\vdash s_{2}^{i}\sim s_{2}^{i+1}
for all
0\leq i<n_{2}
. We set
n:=n_{1}+n_{2}
,
s_{i}:=s_{1}^{i}
for
0\leq i\leq n_{1}
and
s_{n_{1}+i}:=s_{2}^{i}
for
0<i\leq n_{2}
which concludes this case since
s_{1}^{n_{1}}=s^{\prime}=s_{2}^{0}
.

(2)

By double implication:

(\Rightarrow)

By induction on the length of the proof of

\bm{\mathcal{H}}^{\tt ax}\vdash t\approx u
. Cases on the last rule
applied:

({\tt Refl})
: Then

\mathsf{R}\vdash t\doteq u
using
({\tt Refl})
.

({\tt Base})
: Then
(t,u)\in\mathsf{H}
. We distinguish two cases:

(t,u\in\mathsf{R})
: Then

\mathsf{R}\vdash t\doteq u
using

({\tt Base})
.

t,u
meaningless.

({\tt Sym})
: Then

\bm{\mathcal{H}}^{\tt ax}\vdash u\approx t
and by
i.h.
, one has
that either
\mathsf{R}\vdash u\doteq t
or

\mathsf{R}\vdash t\doteq u
from an axiom, or
t
and
u
are
meaningless, thus concluding this case.

(\Leftarrow)

We distinguish three cases:

\mathsf{R}\vdash t\doteq u
from an axiom: We
distinguish two cases:

({\tt Refl})
: Then
t=u
and
using
({\tt Refl})
, one concludes that

\bm{\mathcal{H}}^{\tt ax}\vdash t\approx u
.

({\tt Base})
: Then
(t,u)\in\mathsf{R}
thus

\bm{\mathcal{H}}^{\tt ax}\vdash t\approx u
using

({\tt Base})
.

\mathsf{R}\vdash u\doteq t
from an axiom: We
distinguish two cases:

({\tt Refl})
: Then
u=t
and
using
({\tt Refl})
, one concludes that

\bm{\mathcal{H}}^{\tt ax}\vdash t\approx u
.

({\tt Base})
: Then
(t,u)\in\mathsf{R}
thus

\bm{\mathcal{H}}^{\tt ax}\vdash t\approx u
using

({\tt Base})
and
({\tt Sym})
.

t
and
u
are meaningless: Then using

({\tt Base})
, one concludes that

\bm{\mathcal{H}}^{\tt ax}\vdash t\doteq u
.
∎

Lemma D.3.

Let
t,u\in\Lambda
such that

\bm{\mathcal{H}}^{\tt ax}\vdash t\sim u
, then there exist a context

\mathtt{C}
and terms
t^{\prime},u^{\prime}\in\Lambda
such that
t=\mathtt{C}\!\left<t^{\prime}\right>
,
u=\mathtt{C}\!\left<u^{\prime}\right>
and

\bm{\mathcal{H}}^{\tt ax}\vdash t^{\prime}\approx u^{\prime}
.

Proof.

By induction on the length of the proof of

\bm{\mathcal{H}}^{\tt ax}\vdash t\sim u
. Cases on the last rule applied:

({\tt Conv})
: Then

\bm{\mathcal{H}}^{\tt ax}\vdash t\approx u
and we set
\mathtt{C}:=\diamond
,
t^{\prime}:=t
and
u^{\prime}:=u
which concludes this case since
t=\diamond\!\left<t\right>=\mathtt{C}\!\left<t^{\prime}\right>
,
u=\diamond\!\left<u\right>=\mathtt{C}\!\left<u^{\prime}\right>
and
\bm{\mathcal{H}}^{\tt ax}\vdash t^{\prime}\approx u^{\prime}
by
hypothesis.

({\tt Ctxt})
: Then
t=\mathtt{C}^{\prime\prime}\!\left<t^{\prime\prime}\right>

and
u=\mathtt{C}^{\prime\prime}\!\left<u^{\prime\prime}\right>
for some terms
t^{\prime},u^{\prime}\in\Lambda
such that
\bm{\mathcal{H}}^{\tt ax}\vdash t^{\prime\prime}\sim u^{\prime\prime}
.
By
i.h.
, there exists a context
\mathtt{C}^{\prime}
and terms
t^{\prime},u^{\prime}\in\Lambda
such that
t^{\prime\prime}=\mathtt{C}^{\prime}\!\left<t^{\prime}\right>
,
u^{\prime\prime}=\mathtt{C}^{\prime}\!\left<u^{\prime}\right>
and
\bm{\mathcal{H}}^{\tt ax}\vdash t^{\prime}\approx u^{\prime}
. We set

\mathtt{C}:=\mathtt{C}^{\prime\prime}\!\left<\mathtt{C}^{\prime}\right>
which concludes this case
since
t=\mathtt{C}^{\prime\prime}\!\left<t^{\prime\prime}\right>=\mathtt{C}^{\prime\prime}\!\left<\mathtt{C}^{\prime}\!\left<t^{\prime}\right>\right>=(\mathtt{C}^{\prime\prime}\!\left<\mathtt{C}^{\prime}\right>)\!\left<t^{\prime}\right>=\mathtt{C}\!\left<t^{\prime}\right>
and
u=\mathtt{C}^{\prime\prime}\!\left<u^{\prime\prime}\right>=\mathtt{C}^{\prime\prime}\!\left<\mathtt{C}^{\prime}\!\left<u^{\prime}\right>\right>=(\mathtt{C}^{\prime\prime}\!\left<\mathtt{C}^{\prime}\right>)\!\left<t^{\prime}\right>=\mathtt{C}\!\left<u^{\prime}\right>
.
∎

Let us recall the first axiom:

F

Lemma D.4.

Let
t,u\in\Lambda
such that
\mathsf{R}\vdash t\doteq u
.
Then
t
has a
\omega
-normal form if and only if
u
has a

\omega
-normal form.

Moreover, they share the same
\mathtt{S}_{\omega}
-normal form.

Proof.

Let
t,u\in\Lambda
such that
\mathsf{R}\vdash t\doteq u
. Using

Lemma

6.1
, one deduces that
t\mathbin{={{}_{\!\!\mathsf{R}}}}u
. By induction on the length of the sequence

t\mathbin{={{}_{\!\!\mathsf{R}}}}u
.

({\tt refl})
: Then
t
is equal to
u
and trivially

t
has a
\omega
-normal form if and only if
u
has a

\omega
-normal form. Moreover, they also share the same

\omega
-normal form.

({\tt one-step})
: Then
t\rightarrow_{\omega}u
and
one concludes by Confluence (
F
).

({\tt sym})
: Then
t\mathbin{={{}_{\!\!\mathsf{R}}}}u
and one
concludes by
i.h.
.

({\tt trans})
: Then
t\mathbin{={{}_{\!\!\mathsf{R}}}}s
and

s\mathbin{={{}_{\!\!\mathsf{R}}}}u
for some
s\in\Lambda
. We
distinguish two cases:

t
has a
\omega
-normal form: Then by
i.h.
on

t\mathbin{={{}_{\!\!\mathsf{R}}}}s
, one deduces that
s
has the
same
\omega
-normal form as
t
thus by
i.h.
on
s\mathbin{={{}_{\!\!\mathsf{R}}}}u
, one concludes that
u
has the same

\omega
-normal form as
t
.

u
has a
\omega
-normal form: Symmetric of
the previous case.

∎

Lemma D.5.

If
u\in\Lambda
has a
\omega
-normal form, then for
any
t\in\Lambda
, if
\bm{\mathcal{H}}^{\tt ax}\vdash t\doteq u
then

\mathsf{R}\vdash t\doteq u
.

Proof.

Let
t,u\in\Lambda
such that
u
has a
\omega
-normal
form and
\bm{\mathcal{H}}^{\tt ax}\vdash t\doteq u
. Using

Lemma

D.2
:
1
, one has
n\in\mathbb{N}
and
s_{0},\dots,s_{n}\in\Lambda
such that
t=s_{0}
,
u=s_{n}
and
\bm{\mathcal{H}}^{\tt ax}\vdash s_{i}\sim s_{i+1}
for

0\leq i<n
. Let us show the following:

If
s_{n}
has a
\omega
-normal form, and

\bm{\mathcal{H}}^{\tt ax}\vdash s_{i}\sim s_{i+1}
for
0\leq i<n
,
then
\mathsf{R}\vdash s_{0}\doteq s_{n}
.

We reason by induction on
n\in\mathbb{N}
. Cases:

n=0
: Then
s_{0}=s_{n}
and thus

\mathsf{R}\vdash s_{0}\doteq s_{n}
by
({\tt Refl})
.

n=m+1
: Then using
Lemma

D.3
,
there exist a context
\mathtt{C}
and terms
s^{\prime}_{m},s^{\prime}_{m+1}\in\Lambda
such that
s_{m}=\mathtt{C}\!\left<s^{\prime}_{m}\right>
,
s_{m+1}=\mathtt{C}\!\left<s^{\prime}_{m+1}\right>
and

\bm{\mathcal{H}}^{\tt ax}\vdash s^{\prime}_{m}\approx s^{\prime}_{m+1}
. By

Lemma

D.2
:
2
, we
distinguish two cases:

\mathsf{R}\vdash s^{\prime}_{m}\doteq s^{\prime}_{m+1}
(resp.
\mathsf{R}\vdash s^{\prime}_{m+1}\doteq s^{\prime}_{m}
) from an axiom: Then

\mathsf{R}

\vdash\mathtt{C}\!\left<s^{\prime}_{m}\right>\doteq\mathtt{C}\!\left<s^{\prime}_{m+1}\right>
by contextuality (resp. and symmetry)
thus using

Lemma

D.4
,
one deduces that
s_{m}=\mathtt{C}\!\left<s^{\prime}_{m}\right>
has a

\omega
-norm form. By
i.h.
, one concludes that

\mathsf{R}\vdash s_{0}\doteq s_{m}
, thus

\mathsf{R}\vdash s_{0}\doteq s_{m+1}
holds by

({\tt Trans})
.

s^{\prime}_{m}
and
s^{\prime}_{m+1}
are meaningless: Since

s_{m+1}=\mathtt{C}\!\left<s^{\prime}_{m+1}\right>
has a
\omega
-normal
form, let say
s\in\Lambda
, then
s_{m+1}\mathbin{={{}_{\!\!\mathsf{R}}}}s
, which implies

\mathsf{R}\vdash s_{m+1}\doteq s
by

Lemma

6.1
. Using Full
Genericity (
G
), one deduces that
s_{m}=\mathtt{C}\!\left<s^{\prime}_{m}\right>
has the same
\omega
-normal form
s

thus
\mathsf{R}\vdash s_{m}\doteq s
by

Lemma

6.1
. Therefore

\mathsf{R}\vdash s_{m}\doteq s_{m+1}
using
({\tt Sym})

and
({\tt Trans})
. By
i.h.
, one has that

\mathsf{R}\vdash s_{0}\doteq s_{m}
and we conclude that

\mathsf{R}\vdash s_{0}\doteq s_{m+1}
using

({\tt Trans})
.
∎

Proof.

Using

Lemma

D.5
and

Lemma

.

Lemma D.6.

(1)

\bm{\mathcal{R}}\subseteq\bm{\mathcal{H}}^{*}
.

(2)

\bm{\mathcal{H}}\subseteq\bm{\mathcal{H}}^{*}
.

Proof.

(1)

Let
\mathsf{R}\vdash t\doteq u
. Let us show that
(t,u)\in\bm{\mathcal{H}}^{*}
by induction on the length of the proof

\mathsf{R}\vdash t\doteq u
. Cases on the last rule applied:

({\tt ref})
: Then
t=u
thus
(t,u)\in\bm{\mathcal{H}}^{*}
trivially holds.

({\tt base})
: Then
(t,u)\in\mathsf{R}
and using

H
, one deduces that

t
is meaningful if and only if
u
is meaningful so that

(t,u)\in\bm{\mathcal{H}}^{*}
.

({\tt sym})
: Then
\mathsf{R}\vdash u\doteq t

and, by
i.h.
,
(u,t)\in\bm{\mathcal{H}}^{*}
; one
concludes by symmetry of the logical equivalence in the definition of
\bm{\mathcal{H}}^{*}
.

({\tt tra})
: Then

\mathsf{R}\vdash t\doteq s
and
\mathsf{R}\vdash s\doteq u
for some
s\in\Lambda
, and by

i.h.
on both, one deduces that
(t,s)\in\bm{\mathcal{H}}^{*}
and
(s,u)\in\bm{\mathcal{H}}^{*}
respectively. Therefore one concludes by
transitivity of the logical equivalence in the definition of
\bm{\mathcal{H}}^{*}
.

(2)

By (
1
) one has that

\bm{\mathcal{R}}\subseteq\bm{\mathcal{H}}^{*}
, then it is sufficient to show
that
\bm{\mathcal{H}}^{*}
equates all meaningless terms. Let
t,u\in\Lambda
be both meaningless. By Surface Genericity
(
I
), one has that
\mathtt{C}\!\left<t\right>
is
meaningful if and only if
\mathtt{C}\!\left<u\right>
is meaningful, thus
(t,u)\in\bm{\mathcal{H}}^{*}
.
∎

6.2

Lemma D.7.

Let
t,u\in\Lambda
and
x\in\mathcal{X}
such that

t
is meaningful,
x
is fresh in
t
and
u
and

\lambda x.tu
is meaningful, then
u
is meaningful.

Proof.

Let
t,u\in\Lambda
and
x\in\mathcal{X}
such that

t
is meaningful,
x
is fresh in
t
and
u
and

(\lambda x.t)u
is meaningful. Then by

Lemma

2.5
, there exists
\Pi\triangleright_{\mathcal{V}}\Gamma\vdash(\lambda x.t)u:\sigma
. Then

\Pi
is necessarily of the following form:

({\tt app})

\Pi_{1}\triangleright_{\mathcal{V}}\Gamma_{1}\vdash\lambda x.t:\mathcal{M}\Rightarrow\sigma
\Pi_{2}\triangleright_{\mathcal{V}}\Gamma_{2}\vdash u:\mathcal{M}
\Gamma_{1}+\Gamma_{2}\vdash(\lambda x.t)u:\sigma

with
\Gamma=\Gamma_{1}+\Gamma_{2}
. Since
\Pi_{2}\triangleright_{\mathcal{V}}\Gamma_{2}\vdash u:\mathcal{M}
, one then deduces that
u
is meaningful
using
(Logical)
.
∎

See
6.7

Proof.

Let
t,u\in\Lambda
such that

\mathcal{C}_{\mathsf{H}_{\mathsf{v}}\cup\{(t,u)\}}
is
consistent. Suppose by absurd that there exists a context

\mathtt{C}
such that
\mathtt{C}\!\left<t\right>
is meaningful and

\mathtt{C}\!\left<u\right>
is meaningless. Let us show that

\mathcal{C}_{\mathsf{H}_{\mathsf{v}}\cup\{(t,u)\}}
is
inconsistent.

Let
s\in\Lambda
be a meaningful term and
x\in\mathcal{X}
fresh. On one hand, by definition of
meaningfulness, there exists a testing context
\mathtt{T}
such
that
\mathtt{T}\!\left<\mathtt{C}\!\left<t\right>\right>\rightarrow^{*}_{\mathrm{v}}v
for some value
v\in\Upsilon
. In particular,

(\lambda x.s)\mathtt{T}\!\left<\mathtt{C}\!\left<t\right>\right>\rightarrow^{*}_{\mathrm{v}}(\lambda x.s)v\rightarrow^{*}_{\mathrm{v}}s
thus using

Lemma

6.1
, one deduces that

\bm{\mathcal{T}}_{{\tt V}}\vdash(\lambda x.s)\mathtt{T}\!\left<\mathtt{C}\!\left<t\right>\right>\doteq_{{\tt V}}s
.
Since
\bm{\mathcal{H}}_{{\tt dCBV}}
is a
\lambda_{{\tt dCBV}}
-theory, then

\bm{\mathcal{H}}_{{\tt V}}\vdash(\lambda x.s)\mathtt{T}\!\left<\mathtt{C}\!\left<t\right>\right>\doteq_{{\tt V}}s
and
thus
\mathcal{C}_{\mathsf{H}_{\mathsf{v}}\cup\{(t,u)\}}

\vdash(\lambda x.s)\mathtt{T}\!\left<\mathtt{C}\!\left<t\right>\right>\doteq s
. On the
other hand, by definition of meaninglesness,

\mathtt{T}\!\left<\mathtt{C}\!\left<u\right>\right>
necessarily meaningless. Suppose by
absurd that
(\lambda x.s)\mathtt{T}\!\left<\mathtt{C}\!\left<u\right>\right>
is
meaningful, then using

Lemma

D.7
, one deduces that

\mathtt{T}\!\left<\mathtt{C}\!\left<t\right>\right>
is meaningful which is impossible thus

(\lambda x.s)\mathtt{T}\!\left<\mathtt{C}\!\left<u\right>\right>
is therefore
meaningless. By contextuality and transitivity

\mathcal{C}_{\mathsf{H}_{\mathsf{v}}\cup\{(t,u)\}}

\vdash(\lambda x.s)\mathtt{T}\!\left<\mathtt{C}\!\left<u\right>\right>\doteq s
and by
sensibility
\mathcal{C}_{\mathsf{H}_{\mathsf{v}}\cup\{(t,u)\}}\vdash\Omega\doteq s
. Since
s
is an arbitrary meaningful
term, one deduces that
\mathcal{C}_{\mathsf{H}_{\mathsf{v}}\cup\{(t,u)\}}
is inconsistent contradicting the hypothesis and
concluding this proof.
∎

See
6.8

Proof.

We first prove that
\bm{\mathcal{H}}^{*}_{{\tt dCBV}}
is a maximal consistent and
sensible
\lambda_{{\tt dCBV}}
-theory and then show that it is unique.

(1)

Maximality: By
Corollary

6.6
,
\bm{\mathcal{H}}^{*}_{{\tt dCBV}}
is a
consistent and sensible
\lambda_{{\tt dCBV}}
-theory. Let
t,u\in\Lambda
such that
\bm{\mathcal{H}}^{*}_{{\tt dCBV}}\cup\{(t,u)\}
is
consistent. By inclusion, one deduces that

\mathcal{C}_{\mathsf{H}_{\mathsf{v}}\cup\{(t,u)\}}
is
consistent, thus that
\bm{\mathcal{H}}^{*}_{{\tt dCBV}}\vdash t\doteq u
using

Lemma

6.7
. The theory

\bm{\mathcal{H}}^{*}_{{\tt dCBV}}
is therefore maximal.

(2)

Unicity: Let
\mathcal{E}
be a maximal consistent and
sensible
\lambda_{{\tt dCBV}}
-theory. Let
t,u\in\Lambda
such
that
\mathcal{E}\vdash t\doteq u
. By inclusion, one
deduces that
\mathcal{C}_{\mathsf{H}_{\mathsf{v}}\cup\{(t,u)\}}
is consistent thus using

Lemma

6.7
, one has that

\bm{\mathcal{H}}^{*}_{{\tt dCBV}}\vdash t\doteq u
and therefore
\mathcal{E}\subseteq\bm{\mathcal{H}}^{*}_{{\tt dCBV}}
. Suppose by absurd that there exists
t,u\in\Lambda
such that
\bm{\mathcal{H}}^{*}_{{\tt dCBV}}\vdash t\doteq u
but
not
\mathcal{E}\vdash t\doteq u
. Since
\bm{\mathcal{H}}^{*}_{{\tt dCBV}}
is
consistent, then by inclusion, one deduces that
\mathcal{E}\cup\{(t,u)\}
is consistent which contradict the fact that

\mathcal{E}
is a maximal consistent and sensible

\lambda_{{\tt dCBV}}
-theory. One concludes that
\mathcal{E}=\bm{\mathcal{H}}^{*}_{{\tt dCBV}}
.
∎
